# APL and the ORN→KC remapping of odor–odor correlations

%%% hover
re-drawn: Of the PN→KC wiring: a randomized version of the hemibrain wiring that the model otherwise uses, made either by permuting the claws among the KCs of each type or by a shuffle that keeps every glomerulus pair's count of shared KCs. Each KC keeps its number of claws and each glomerulus its number of claws; which KC gets which glomerulus changes. A model on a re-drawn wiring stands for another fly with the same statistics, so a result that holds on re-drawn wirings does not depend on the one mapped brain.
%%%

Nov 3, 2026 · @Tom

## Bottom line

APL output accounts for roughly a quarter to a third of the pair-specific ORN→KC reorganization in these two matrices; most of it survives APL block. Blocking APL output doubles the mean KC pair correlation (0.16 → 0.34) but leaves the rank order of pair distances largely intact (Spearman 0.87 across the 120 pairs). Rank agreement with the ORN matrix rises only from 0.66 to 0.75 (16 odors), closing about 28% of the gap to a rank-preserving feedforward model (\~0.98), and the same pairs deviate from the ORN prediction with and without APL (Spearman 0.82 between the two residual vectors).

Where APL does act pair-specifically, it pushes in the direction of the wild-type remapping (Spearman 0.39 with the WT KC−ORN residuals) and toward natural-source structure: alignment with VCF distances drops from 0.55 to 0.42 when APL is blocked, and the APL-blocked soma geometry becomes claw-like (Spearman with KC-claw distances 0.68 → 0.85). So APL is a real contributor, but it is the amplifier of the last stage rather than the source of the structure.

Two cautions shape the ranking below. First, a single monotone (sparsening) transform explains 94% of the control-vs-TNT differences, so most of what looks pair-specific in a linear comparison is non-specific sparsening; what survives is mainly an odor-level effect (broad, strong activators 2h, IaA, pa, eb are decorrelated extra; 1-8ol, euc, B-myr, t2h are spared) plus one block effect (fruity esters {2h, IaA, pa} pushed away from small esters {2-but, eb, ep}). Second, these are two cross-fly mean matrices with no per-fly spread, so residuals of \~0.03 are near the likely noise floor.

Most likely mechanisms, in order: (1) KC spike-threshold sparsening acting on connectome-structured input with heterogeneous KCs; (2) APL feedback whose gain tracks total PN drive and acts partly locally in the calyx; (3) presynaptic shaping of PN boutons in the calyx; (4) APL-independent KC–KC lateral inhibition; (5) structured centrifugal input to the calyx (LH, MB-C1). Measurement confounds (response density, indicator nonlinearity) need to be excluded before any of these is quantified. For the model, the pair of matrices is the most useful new constraint: one parameter set should reproduce both, with only APL output toggled.

## What the two matrices say

Blocking APL output raises every pair's correlation by a similar amount; the pair-specific remainder is small and mostly explained by one monotone transform. The control (TNT3c) matrix has mean correlation distance 0.84 (mean r = 0.16); the APL-blocked (TNT) matrix 0.66 (r = 0.34). That doubling of mean correlation matches the response-rate doubling Lin et al. (2014) reported for APL block, and the direction of the two files is consistent with the labels (the matrix with the denser, more correlated code is the TNT one).

&#91;embedded content: TNT3c\_dist\_apl16.csv and TNT\_dist\_apl16.csv (cross-fly mean 1 − r, 16 odors, 120 pairs)\]

Across the 120 pairs, control vs TNT: Spearman 0.87, Pearson 0.92. A linear fit explains 85% of the variance in control distances (residual SD 0.052); an isotonic (any monotone) fit explains 94% (residual SD 0.033). Sparse thresholded codes compress low correlations more than high ones, so within-family pairs (1-5ol–1-6ol, eb–ep, 6al–t2h, -aPine–euc) look "spared" in a linear comparison but sit exactly on the monotone curve. The control matrix also reproduces Remy's wild-type megamat KC matrix on the 66 shared pairs (Spearman 0.85), so the control cohort is representative; the TNT matrix matches that wild-type matrix equally well (0.87), i.e. the KC-specific rank structure is still largely there without APL.

What survives the monotone transform is mostly odor-level, not pair-level. Per odor, the extra decorrelation APL adds tracks total ORN drive (Spearman 0.50 with mean ORN ΔF/F across glomeruli): eb, IaA, pa and 2h get the most, 1-8ol, euc, 1-5ol and t2h the least. One genuine block effect stands out: fruity esters {2h, IaA, pa} are pushed away from small esters/ketone {2-but, eb, ep} beyond any monotone trend, while alcohol–aldehyde and aldehyde–terpene pairs are slightly spared.

| Pair block | Isotonic residual | Uniform-sparsening residual | Control 1 − r | TNT 1 − r |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| fruity esters × small esters (9 pairs) | +0.055 | +0.117 | 0.88 | 0.60 |
| fruity esters × ms (3) | +0.035 | +0.069 | 0.93 | 0.71 |
| aldehydes × paa (2) | +0.025 | +0.063 | 0.74 | 0.51 |
| alcohols × fruity esters (9) | +0.011 | +0.061 | 0.84 | 0.61 |
| all other between-group pairs (72) | −0.003 | +0.026 | 0.90 | 0.73 |
| alcohols × aldehydes (6) | −0.040 | −0.009 | 0.73 | 0.57 |
| aldehydes × terpenes (6) | −0.028 | −0.010 | 0.86 | 0.73 |

Residuals are control minus the value predicted from TNT by an isotonic fit, or by a two-parameter thresholded-Gaussian "uniform sparsening" null (same latent drive correlations, higher threshold in control). The fruity×small-ester block differs from the other between-group pairs (Mann–Whitney p ≈ 1e-6), but its nine pairs share six odors, so treat it as one observation, not nine. Coarse clustering is unchanged by APL block: the same six groups (fruity esters; small esters; alcohols + aldehydes; terpenes; ms; paa) appear in the ORN, control and TNT matrices, and the one KC-specific regrouping, B-myr joining the terpenes rather than paa, is present with and without APL.

Limits of this analysis: both matrices are cross-fly means with no per-fly spread, so a residual of \~0.03 is at the likely noise floor; the two cohorts differ in genotype and rearing temperature; without the response rates I cannot separate correlation changes from density changes (denser TNT responses also admit more cells into the "responsive" set used for the RDMs); and the four validation2 odors were matched to the ORN panel at the concentrations in consensus\_mean.csv (paa −4, -aPine −1.5, euc −1.75, B-myr −2), which may not be what the APL experiment used.

## How much of the ORN→KC remapping is APL?

Removing APL output moves the KC geometry only part of the way back toward the ORN geometry, and the part that moves is the part that matters least for the natural-source story. On the 12 odors shared with the megamat panel (66 pairs), I compared both new matrices against the lab's existing distance tables (ORN terminals, PN boutons, KC claws, Remy's wild-type KC soma, the two paper models, ECFP chemistry, and the hyperbolic VCF embedding used in the thesis, averaged over its 10 seeds).

| Reference space (66 pairs, 12 megamat odors) | Control (TNT3c) | APL blocked (TNT) | WT KC soma (Remy) |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| ORN terminals | 0.78 | 0.85 | 0.88 |
| PN boutons | 0.73 | 0.79 | 0.86 |
| KC claws | 0.68 | 0.85 | 0.82 |
| WT KC soma | 0.85 | 0.87 | 1.00 |
| Uniform model | 0.78 | 0.84 | 0.85 |
| Hemibrain (wd20) model | 0.76 | 0.83 | 0.84 |
| Chemical structure (ECFP) | 0.62 | 0.70 | 0.70 |
| Natural sources (VCF, hyperbolic) | 0.55 | 0.42 | 0.45 |

Values are Spearman rank correlations between pair-distance vectors. Bootstrapping pairs (5,000 draws, 90% CI) gives TNT − control differences of +0.07 \[−0.01, +0.16\] against ORNs, +0.17 \[+0.07, +0.27\] against KC claws, −0.13 \[−0.24, −0.03\] against VCF, and +0.09 \[−0.01, +0.19\] against chemistry. With all 16 odors (120 pairs, ORN matrix built from the lab's consensus glomerular means, which reproduce the tidy-table ORN distances at Spearman 0.99), rank agreement with ORNs goes from 0.66 (control) to 0.75 (TNT), +0.09 \[+0.03, +0.16\].

Three ways of scoring APL's share give similar answers. The gap between the control's ORN agreement and the \~0.98 a rank-preserving feedforward model produces is closed by 28% (16 odors) to 34% (12 odors) when APL is blocked. The variance of the pair residuals around a linear ORN prediction falls by 18% (16 odors) to 30% (12 odors), and the residual vectors with and without APL correlate at 0.82, so the same pairs deviate in the same direction either way. For natural-source alignment, the KC−ORN residual correlates with VCF distance at 0.44 in the control, 0.43 in wild type and 0.36 with APL blocked, so the "corr minus ORN vs VCF" effect is mostly APL-independent as well.

What APL does contribute is in the right direction. Its pair-specific component (control minus the linear prediction from TNT) correlates with the wild-type KC−ORN residuals at 0.39 (p = 0.001) and with VCF distance at 0.28 (p = 0.02), and not with chemical distance (−0.03). The clearest single case is the separation of the fruity esters {2h, IaA, pa} from the small esters {2-but, eb, ep}: these two groups are fairly close in ORN space (mean 1 − r = 0.51) and in the APL-blocked KCs (0.60) but far apart in the control KCs (0.88). Conversely, the APL-blocked soma geometry is as claw-like as the wild-type soma or more (0.85 vs 0.82), so APL is the main thing that makes the soma differ from the claws on this panel.

What APL does not do: the B-myr regrouping with the terpenes, the divergence of paa from B-myr and of the small esters from the aldehydes (ep–6al, eb–t2h, ep–t2h), and the relative convergence of alcohols, aldehydes and terpenes within their families are all present with APL output blocked. The APL-independent part of the claw→soma step still raises VCF alignment (claws 0.30 → TNT soma 0.42), although those are different flies and indicators, so that number is suggestive only.

Two model observations fall out of the same table. The hemibrain (wd20) model's deviations from the ORN prediction have the right size (SD 0.114 vs 0.117 in wild type) and correlate with the measured deviations at 0.60, whereas the uniform model's deviations are tiny (SD 0.044) and correlate at 0.44; the hemibrain model's overall Spearman with the data is not better because its misses are as large as its hits. And the chapter-2 draft figure 7 (claw clustering resembles ORNs early in the response and KC somata late) points the same way as the TNT data: the remapping is built during the response by feedback, and APL is the obvious but not the only candidate for that feedback.

## Candidate mechanisms, ranked

The ranking weighs three things: what the TNT pair shows (section above), what the thesis stage-by-stage data show (the step from KC claws to KC somata carries the largest change, PN boutons a small one), and what is established about the circuit. Shares are rough; only APL's is measured.

1. **KC spike-threshold sparsening acting on structured input, with heterogeneous KCs** (amplifier + substrate). Most likely. A sparse thresholded code compresses low input correlations far more than high ones, which is exactly the monotone transform that explains 94% of the control-vs-TNT difference, and it operates whether or not APL is present. The amplifier cannot change rank order by itself, so the substrate must carry pair-specific structure: the hemibrain (wd20) model produces deviations from the ORN prediction of the right size and with 0.60 rank agreement with the data, which says the connectome already holds much of it, while heterogeneity the model lacks (claw number per KC, which sets selectivity and overlap in [Ahmed et al. 2023](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC10529417/); subtype-specific excitability and inhibition in [Inada et al. 2017](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuron.2017.06.039); food-PN convergence onto α/β and α′/β′ KCs in [Zheng et al. 2022](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9413950)) would decide which KCs cross threshold for which odors.
2. **APL feedback with drive-dependent gain and partly local action in the calyx.** Measured at roughly a quarter to a third of the pair-specific remapping, in the right direction. Its odor-level signature here (extra decorrelation of the strongest, broadest activators) is what a feedback whose gain tracks total PN input should do ([Prisco et al. 2021](https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.74172): APL response scales with PN input and is regionalized around it). Its one clear block effect (fruity vs small esters, groups that overlap partially in ORN space) is what localized inhibition predicts: odors that share glomerular input inhibit each other's KCs more than odors that share none, and [Amin et al. 2020](https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.56954) showed APL activity and its effect on KCs are spatially restricted, with self-inhibition stronger than lateral inhibition. α′/β′ KCs recruit APL most ([Inada et al. 2017](https://doi.org/10.1016/j.neuron.2017.06.039)), so subtype weighting is part of this mechanism, not a separate one.
3. **Presynaptic shaping of PN boutons in the calyx.** Medium. The thesis finds PN boutons already shifted toward natural-source structure (ρ = 0.18 vs 0.09 in ORNs) and reorganized relative to PN dendrites. APL synapses onto boutons as well as claws ([Prisco et al. 2021](https://doi.org/10.7554/eLife.74172)), so part of this is inside mechanism 2 and is TNT-sensitive; the rest (other calyx inputs, bouton-to-bouton heterogeneity within a PN) is not.
4. **Slow, within-response dynamics: adaptation, synaptic depression at PN→KC, APL build-up.** Medium as a modulator. The draft figure 7 (claw clusters ORN-like early, soma-like late) and the nuclear-GCaMP soma readout, which integrates the late phase, mean that any slow feedback or depression process shows up as "soma-specific" remapping. This is the time axis of mechanisms 1–3 rather than a separate cause, but it determines which model readout window should be compared with the data.
5. **APL-independent KC–KC lateral inhibition.** Low to medium. KCs have abundant axo-axonic connections, and through mAChR-B these suppress odor-evoked calcium in the axons of neighbouring γ KCs ([Manoim et al. 2022](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC9613607/)). That suppression is local to the axon terminals in the lobes: the same paper finds mAChR-B in the axonal lobes and cell bodies but not in the calyx dendrites, and no effect of its knockdown on odor responses in the calyx. The hemibrain data in the repo also include KC-claw→KC-claw synapses in the calyx that the model does not use, but no inhibition is established at them. Either route would survive APL>TNT and could carry part of the residual structure; the axonal one is not established to affect soma spiking, and the calyx one is a conjecture that the axonal finding does not support. (Corrected 2026-11-24: the earlier text cited the axonal finding as if it supported the calyx synapses.)
6. **Structured centrifugal input to the calyx (LH centrifugal neurons, MB-C1, aminergic neurons).** Low to medium, speculative but attractive. Odor-category tuning in the lateral horn is stereotyped and innate, and the thesis already notes that PN over-convergence onto KCs resembles PN input to LHNs; a GABAergic LH→calyx pathway would inject category structure directly into KC input and would be APL-independent. Nothing in the present data tests it.
7. **Measurement and analysis confounds.** Not a mechanism, but able to inflate apparent pair-specificity: odors with more responsive KCs get inflated Pearson correlations with everything, the "responsive cell" selection used for RDMs depends on density, nuclear GCaMP6s saturates, and claws and somata were imaged with different indicators. These must be ruled out before the shares above are taken literally.
8. **Experience-dependent plasticity of PN→KC or APL→KC synapses.** Low. Invariance of KC geometry across flies argues for genetically specified or shared-experience structure; it cannot be tested in silico with current data.

## In silico experiments (olfsysm + al\_analysis)

The single most informative experiment is a joint fit to both matrices: one parameter set must reproduce the control matrix with APL on and the TNT matrix with APL output set to zero. Everything else below is a way of localizing what fails when it does not. All experiments share one harness.

**Harness.** Input: the 16 APL-panel odors, built by concatenating `megamat_orn_deltas()` and the four validation2 columns (paa, -aPine, euc, B-myr) from `validation2_orn_deltas()` at the concentrations actually used in the APL experiment. Base model: the chapter-3 claw model (`one_row_per_claw=True, prat_claws=True, pn_claw_to_apl=True, claw_dynamics=True, use_connectome_APL_weights=True`, `target_sparsity` 0.05 or 0.10, `n_spikes_for_response=2`), with the paper wd20 model as the simple comparator. Readout matched to the lab pipeline: binarized `responses` and `spike_counts`, silent model KCs dropped, Pearson across KCs, 1 − r. Score every run with the same statistics used in this doc, so the targets are explicit: mean r 0.16 (APL on) vs 0.34 (APL off); Spearman 0.87 and isotonic R² 0.94 between the two model matrices; Spearman with ORNs 0.66 → 0.75; fruity×small-ester block residual about +0.05; residual-vs-VCF correlation 0.44 → 0.36. Sweeps that only change thresholds, APL scales or coupling should use `make_fixed_param_kc_simulator` rather than re-tuning.

1. **E1, APL knockout, two flavours (mechanism 2 vs 1).** Acute: tune normally, then zero `rv.kc.wAPLKC` (and `wAPLPN` in bouton models) and call `run_KC_sims(mp, rv, regen=False)`, or pass the tuned `fixed_thr` with `wAPLKC=0.0`; thresholds stay fixed, mimicking TNT. Homeostatic: `target_sparsity_factor_pre_APL=1.0` with zero APL, so thresholds alone set the rate (three days at 30 °C may have allowed some homeostasis in the real flies, so both are worth having). Readout: compare model APL-on vs APL-off to control vs TNT. If the model reproduces the mean shift and the 0.87 rank agreement but its two matrices relate by a perfect monotone map (isotonic R² ≈ 1, no fruity×small-ester block), the data's block implicates local or heterogeneous APL (E2, E5). If the APL-off model matches the TNT matrix (Spearman > 0.85) the feedforward substrate is adequate and the problem is the APL stage; if it does not, the substrate is wrong (E4, E5).
2. **E2, local APL (mechanism 2).** Sweep `apl_unit_coupling` over {0, 0.1, 0.3, 1, 3, 10, `max_apl_unit_coupling()`} in `apl_coupling_sweep.py` matched mode, so response counts are equal across couplings. Readout: block residual and residual-vs-VCF as functions of coupling. Prediction: weaker coupling increases separation of odor groups that share glomerular input (fruity vs small esters) more than groups that share none (alcohols vs terpenes), up to the point where self-inhibition dominates and decorrelation falls (Amin et al. 2020).
3. **E3, drive-dependent gain (mechanisms 2 and 3).** Data target: APL's extra decorrelation per odor tracks ORN drive (Spearman 0.50). Vary how much PN drive is flattened before it reaches APL: expose `mp.pn.inhsc`, `mp.pn.inhadd` and the `mp.ln` parameters in `fit_mb_model` (currently not set there), or pre-scale `orn_deltas` columns in Python; toggle `pn_claw_to_apl`; sweep `wAPLPN`×`wPNAPL` with `step_model_pn_apl.py` in bouton models. Readout: per-odor APL effect vs ORN drive, and whether the strongest odors (eb, IaA, pa, 2h) are the ones decorrelated most.
4. **E4, substrate structure (mechanism 1).** With APL on and off, run `pn2kc_connections` ∈ {uniform, hemidraw, hemibrain wd20, hemibrain `prat_claws`, fafb-left, fafb-right} plus three shuffles of the hemibrain matrix passed through `_wPNKC`: Curveball degree-preserving (row and column sums kept, as in the thesis S4 null), KC-type-preserving (shuffle within α/β, α′/β′, γ), and glomerulus-frequency-preserving with real claw counts. Readout: Spearman between the model's deviations from the ORN prediction and the measured deviations (0.60 for wd20 today). Prediction if pair structure matters: real > type-preserving > degree-preserving > uniform. Vary `weight_divisor` (None, 10, 20, 40) and `dist_weight` to see whether synapse counts add anything; the repo note says `dist_weight` made things worse, which is itself informative.
5. **E5, KC heterogeneity (mechanism 1).** Compare uniform thresholds with `homeostatic_thrs=True`; `equalize_kc_type_sparsity` with `ab_prime_response_rate_target` above the others (α′/β′ are the most excitable type); a `fixed_thr` vector that scales with claw count (thr ∝ n\_claws^k, k ∈ {0, 0.5, 1}); `n_claws_active_to_spike` ∈ {2, 3} as a coincidence requirement; and per-type APL scaling through `_wAPLKC`/`_wKCAPL` (α′/β′ highest). Readout: Spearman with the control matrix and residual-vs-VCF. Prediction: heterogeneity increases deviations from the ORN prediction for the strongest odors without changing mean rate.
6. **E6, readout window (mechanism 4).** With `return_dynamics=True`, build matrices from spikes in \[0, 0.25\] s and \[0.25, 0.75\] s after odor onset and from a GCaMP-like kernel applied to spike trains (olfsysm currently counts spikes over the whole simulation including the 0.5 s pre-odor period, so a window option is needed in `run_KC_sims` or in Python from `spike_recordings`). Compare early vs late model matrices with the early vs late claw matrices of draft figure 7 and with the soma matrix; sweep `claw_tau` and `apl_taum`. Prediction: the late window should look more soma-like and more VCF-aligned than the early window only if feedback carries the remapping.
7. **E7, confound controls (item 7 above).** Push model outputs through the exact RDM pipeline (responsive-cell selection, chapter-3 logistic scaling of spike counts, Pearson on graded values), and build a density-matched null: shuffle KC identities within each odor keeping per-odor response counts, compute the expected correlation under independence, and report residual or partial correlations after regressing out per-odor density. Apply the same null to the real per-fly TNT and TNT3c data once available.
8. **E8, KC–KC inhibition (mechanism 5).** New term in `sim_KC_layer` (olfsysm.cpp ≈2389): subtract `wKCKC · activity(t−1)` at claws or somata with weights from `from_pratyush/2025-12-05/KCclaw-to-KCclaw_*`, scaled so that with APL off the response rate matches the TNT rate. Readout: does the APL-off model acquire the APL-independent residual structure (residual-vs-VCF 0.36)?
9. **E9, centrifugal structured input (mechanism 6).** Add an LHN layer from hemibrain PN→LHN weights (threshold-linear units) and LH-centrifugal and MB-C1 projections onto boutons and claws from the hemibrain, as an extra inhibitory drive in the bouton-model path. Readout: VCF alignment gained beyond APL, and whether it reproduces the APL-independent block effects (ep–6al, eb–t2h, paa–B-myr).
10. **E10, joint fit.** Extend `make_kc_data_loss` to take two target matrices (control, TNT) plus per-odor rates for both conditions, and fit threshold, APL scales, coupling and per-type scales with `optimize_mb_model_params`, with the TNT term evaluated at `wAPLKC_scale=0` and otherwise identical parameters. A fit that succeeds pins down the APL stage; a fit that fails tells you which pairs the substrate cannot produce, which is the list to take back to E4, E8 and E9.

11. **E11, claw coincidence combined with claw-level inhibition (mechanisms 1 and 2).** E5 found that requiring 2 or 3 active claws for a KC to spike (`n_claws_active_to_spike`) is the only KC-level change that moves the claw models toward the data (overall decorrelation up to the data's level, deviations from the ORN prediction toward the wild-type pattern), and E7's density null shows what it does: it removes the model's non-specific excess of shared responders and leaves exactly the fruity × small-ester shared-responder block that APL removes in the data. E2's APL units acted on claws, but before a coincidence test existed. Design: claw-dynamics base at 5 % with `n_claws_active_to_spike` ∈ {2, 3}, crossed with (a) the APL split into connectome spatial units (`apl_unit_coupling` 0 and 1) at matched response counts, (b) per-claw connectome APL weights scaled ×2 / ×4 for claws from the glomeruli with the most ORN drive shared by the fruity and small esters, and (c) E8's KC–KC inhibition once implemented; APL on and acute off for each. Readout: the block residual (+0.047 binarized, +0.055 graded), E7's excess-similarity profile (control: block pairs = other between-group pairs = −0.05), Spearman with the control matrix, agreement of deviations with wild type. Prediction: if (a) or (b) produces the block, a claw-level APL is sufficient and E10 should run on that model family; if only (c) does, KC–KC inhibition is the candidate; if none does, pair-specific APL action is not reproducible with these substrates (E9).
12. **E12, APL weight proportional to KC breadth (mechanism 2).** E7 shows mechanically what the data's one robust pair-specific APL effect is: with APL blocked, the fruity-ester and small-ester responder sets overlap far beyond what density predicts (excess shared-responder distance −0.19 vs −0.12 for other between-group pairs); with APL intact the excess is gone (−0.046 vs −0.048). APL therefore removes KCs that respond to both groups, i.e. broadly tuned KCs, whereas the model's global APL (E1), spatial units (E2), bouton-level APL (E3) and type-specific weights (E5) all suppress proportionally and leave the shared responders in. Design: in the bouton base (E3's APL-on reference), scale each KC's APL→KC weight by breadth^k, with breadth measured in the APL-off model (number of odors responded to, or total PN drive summed over claws) and k ∈ {0, 0.5, 1, 2}, APL scale re-matched to equal response counts (`multiresponder_APL_boost` / `_multiresponder_mask` already give a binary version); also the Amin et al. 2020 variant with self-inhibition stronger than lateral inhibition (per-KC APL weight ∝ the KC's own KC→APL weight). Readout: the block, E7's excess-shared-responder statistic for block vs other pairs, isotonic R², deviations vs control. Prediction: block reproduced with the excess removed only for the block pairs → APL's pair-specific action is breadth-dependent suppression (a testable prediction: multi-responder KCs are the ones APL silences); not reproduced → the shared responders are not the broad KCs and something pair-specific (E8, E11) is needed. Complements E11, which weights per claw before the coincidence test.
13. **E13, bouton base with claw coincidence (mechanisms 1 and 3).** Combine the two knobs that moved anything: E3's bouton-level APL (which reproduces the odor-level drive dependence of APL's effect) and E5's claw-coincidence rule (which brings the overall decorrelation to the data's level and removes the non-specific excess shared responders), on the bouton base rather than E11's claw-dynamics base. Score as E1. Question: does the combination reach the data's mean distance, drive dependence and density-null excess at once, still without the block? If so, the block is isolated as the single remaining target for E8, E11 and E12.
14. **E14, oracle co-responder suppression (ceiling test for the block).** E11 (APL targeted at the shared-drive claws), E12 (breadth-weighted APL) and E13 (bouton base with coincidence) all leave the model's fruity × small-ester co-responders in place (APL-on excess shared-responder distance −0.18 to −0.42 vs −0.05 / −0.08 in the control data). Before building more mechanisms: is removing exactly those KCs even sufficient? Design: define the co-responders from the APL-off model (KCs responding to at least one fruity and at least one small ester), multiply the APL→KC weights of their claws by f ∈ {2, 4, 8, 1000} (after re-tuning, f = 1000 means the APL acts on them only) and re-match the response count; controls with an equal number of random KCs and with co-responders defined on alcohols × aldehydes; claw-dynamics base with and without the coincidence-3 rule; APL on and acute off. Readout: the block residual and the excess-similarity profile (does the block appear while other between-group and within-group excess stay at the control level?), Spearman with the control matrix, per-odor rates, deviations vs control and wild type. Prediction: block with the rest intact → an activity-defined removal of co-responders is sufficient and E8 / E12 / E10 should aim at mechanisms that single those KCs out; block but other pairs break → the data's APL acts more selectively than any per-KC weight (pair-specific terms, E8); no block even then → the block cannot be produced by suppressing responders at this rate, and the readout and substrate need revisiting.
15. **E15, breadth × shared-input APL weighting (mechanism 2; mechanistic version of E14's oracle).** E14 showed that inhibiting the APL-off co-responders of the fruity and small esters about four times harder than other KCs (with claw coincidence 3) reproduces the block at the measured size (+0.052 vs +0.055) and improves nearly every other statistic (Spearman with the control matrix 0.62, deviations vs wild type 0.73, residual vs VCF +0.38), and that those cells are broad responders (7 of 16 odors) that all pool claws from the eight glomeruli whose drive is shared by the two ester groups; E12's breadth-only rule reached a block of +0.015. Design: on the coincidence-3 claw-dynamics base, APL→KC weight per KC ∝ (APL-off breadth)^k × (1 + number of claws from the shared-drive glomeruli)^m, k ∈ {0, 1, 2, 3}, m ∈ {0, 1, 2}, APL re-tuned to 5 %; plus the Amin et al. 2020 self-inhibition rule (weight ∝ the KC's own APL-off drive to the APL); APL on and acute off. Readout: block residual, E7's excess profile, Spearman with control, deviations vs control and wild type, per-odor rates (the cost E14 found). Prediction: a (k, m) that reaches the oracle's block with per-odor rate agreement kept above about 0.6 → E10's model family is this rule; breadth alone (m = 0) insufficient → the shared-glomerulus input is part of the rule; neither → the oracle's population is not capturable by a static per-KC weight and E8's pair-specific term is needed.
16. **E16, per-claw saturation as the linear-summation form of the coincidence rule (mechanism 1).** The claw-coincidence rule (`n_claws_active_to_spike`) is in every model that has come close to the data (E5, E11, E13, E14), but it replaces somatic integration by counting thresholded claw events: under it the threshold vector is per claw, the somatic Vm is inert, and somatic mechanisms (E8's KC–KC inhibition, homeostatic and per-type thresholds) are disabled, which sits badly with linear summation of claw inputs at the KC soma. A linear-input analysis of the saved runs says what the rule does: every Pratyush claw already has the same weight and 92–95 % of the linear model's responders already have three or more substantially active claws, so its effect is that a strongly driven claw cannot count for more than one, i.e. a per-claw saturating nonlinearity summed linearly at the soma with threshold about N × cap. Design: (a) in olfsysm, claw activity = cap · tanh(drive / cap) (or a hard cap) before the sum across claws, ordinary somatic threshold tuning, cap swept over about five values around the coincidence models' tuned claw threshold; (b) without C++, cap the per-glomerulus PN output (each claw has one glomerulus), by lowering the PN tanh saturation or compressing `orn_deltas` per glomerulus, and re-tune; bouton and claw-dynamics bases at 5 %, APL on and acute off, also combined with E12's breadth weighting. Readout: the E1 statistics and E7's excess profile, and the Spearman of each capped model's matrices with the coincidence-2 / -3 models'. Prediction: statistics converge to the coincidence models' at some cap → the rule is a saturation and can be retired for a biophysical parameter usable with E8, E10 and homeostatic thresholds; no cap reproduces it → the effect lies in counting N distinct inputs, which would need a dendritic (per-claw spike) justification.
17. **E17, panel-general shared-input APL weighting and the anatomical check (mechanism 2; non-circularity test of E15).** E15's rule reproduces the block, but its shared-claw term used glomeruli chosen from the two ester groups themselves (DM2, VM2, DM1, DM6, VM7d, DM4, VM3, VA2), so it is partly circular. A rule the fly could implement must be defined without reference to the block. Design: coincidence-3 claw-dynamics base; per-claw APL→KC weight ∝ (1 + score of the claw's glomerulus)^m, m ∈ {1, 2, 3}, with scores (a) the glomerulus's co-drive summed over all 120 odor pairs of the panel, (b) its tuning breadth (panel odors with normalized drive above half-maximum), (c) a random permutation of (a) across glomeruli as control, each also with the breadth term k = 2; APL re-tuned; APL on and acute off. Anatomy, without simulation: Spearman of the hemibrain per-claw APL→claw and claw→APL synapse counts with the glomerulus scores and with E15's shared-drive membership. Readout: as E15 (block residual, excess profile, Spearman with control, deviations, VCF, per-odor rates, AUC for the oracle set). Prediction: (a) or (b) reproduces the block → a non-circular anatomical rule exists and E10's family can use it; only the block-specific set works → the data's block implicates those glomeruli specifically, a prediction about which glomeruli's claws APL should target; synapse counts already correlate with the scores → the connectome partly encodes the rule (the base model already uses those weights, so the question is how far short they fall). Added under the idle-host rule on 2026-11-04 after E15.

18. **E19, per-claw threshold × breadth-weighted APL × KC–KC inhibition (mechanisms 1 + 2 + 3 combined; first joint model).** E16 showed the coincidence rule is a per-claw threshold (olfsysm `claw_thr`: each claw's drive enters the somatic sum as max(x − thr, 0)) that keeps somatic integration, and E8 showed that breadth-weighted APL (k = 3) with connectome-weighted KC–KC inhibition (scale 30–100, τ 50 ms) produces the fruity × small-ester block and the control's excess profile without an oracle, while neither fixes what the other does (the threshold sets the decorrelation level and the APL-off excess; the APL × KC–KC pair sets the block and the control agreement). Design: bouton base at the E16 operating points claw_thr ∈ {40, 70} (tuned threshold and APL scales kept), × breadth exponent k ∈ {0, 1, 2, 3} (KC↔APL scales re-matched to the k = 0 response count, as E12) × kc_kc_inh ∈ {0, 30, 100} (APL-on rate re-matched by threshold re-tuning, as E8); acute APL off. Readout: the E1 statistics, E7's excess profile in both conditions, per-odor rates, the APL-off / APL-on rate ratio and the APL-off residual vs VCF. Prediction: a variant with block ≥ +0.04, a control-like excess profile and mean distance ≥ 0.78 → the three mechanisms are complementary and E10 round 2 fits from there; block lost once the threshold is on → the block depends on the weakly driven claws the threshold removes; rate ratio ≤ 1.5 throughout → the KC–KC cost is intrinsic. Added under the idle-host rule on 2026-11-05 after E16 and E8.

19. **E18, bouton→claw vesicle depletion (mechanism 1, dynamic per-bouton saturation).** Short-term depression at the PN bouton makes each bouton's output a saturating function of its PN's rate (rate-based Tsodyks–Markram pool: steady-state fraction 1 / (1 + p τr r), release capped at 1/τr), shared by the ~25 claws on ~24 KCs each bouton contacts, APL-independent and so present in TNT; and it is temporal, the onset transient undepressed, the depressed state reached after ~1 / (p r + 1/τr) (40–200 ms), overlapping the 50–150 ms in which the model's KC responses are decided (E6). Design: olfsysm per-bouton pool (`bouton_ves_p`, `bouton_tau_r`) applied to the post-inhibition bouton output and to the spontaneous input; bouton base at 5 %, thresholds re-tuned per setting, release probability p ∈ {0.1, 0.3, 0.5, 0.8} × τr ∈ {0.05, 0.2, 1} s, APL on and acute off; then the best setting crossed with E12's breadth weighting and compared with the per-claw threshold (E16) as a replacement. Readout: the E1 statistics, E7's excess profile, per-odor rates, Spearman with the coincidence-3 / claw-threshold models' matrices. Prediction, revised after E16: a compressive transfer without a knee moved the model away from the coincidence models, so depletion is expected to do the same in its static limit; the experiment tests whether its dynamics (undepressed onset, bouton sharing) change that. Reproduces the threshold models' gains at some (p, τr) → depletion is a mechanism for the knee; moves away at every setting → the knee must be a threshold, not depression. Proposed 2026-11-04 from Tom's question about depletion and PN spiking; moved into the list by Tom on 2026-11-05.

20. **E20, per-glomerulus drive normalization ahead of the per-claw threshold (mechanism 1, input side).** The per-claw threshold that reproduces the coincidence rule is absolute in PN-rate units, so odors driving only weakly firing glomeruli lose their responders: every threshold model worsens the per-odor rate rank agreement with the KC data, and the joint fits at a 40 Hz threshold shrink the KC–KC term and score worse for the same reason. Design: bouton base; each glomerulus's PN→claw weights scaled by (median peak PN rate / that glomerulus's peak)^α, α ∈ {0, 0.5, 1}, crossed with claw_thr ∈ {0, 40, 70}, thresholds re-tuned, acute APL off; then the best α at the balanced E19 point (threshold 40, breadth exponent 2, KC–KC 30). Biophysically, homeostatic scaling of PN→KC synapses by each glomerulus's long-term activity. Readout: per-odor rate rank agreement and response fractions (primary), with the decorrelation level, excess sharing and block as the things that must not be lost. Prediction: rank agreement recovers with the threshold's gains kept → the threshold is usable in the joint fit without its rate cost; recovers only by losing decorrelation → the two targets conflict; no recovery → the rate pattern is set by the input (E21). Registered at Tom's request on 2026-11-05.

21. **E21, why the model's APL-blocked code shares too little among non-ester pairs (input side; diagnostic).** With the per-claw threshold on, the model's APL-blocked matrix matches the data's excess sharing for the fruity × small-ester pairs but shares too little among other between-group pairs, and its rank agreement with the APL-blocked data stays at 0.58–0.68 in every model (ceiling 0.97). Design: per-pair excess sharing of the best threshold model's APL-off code against the TNT data, by odor group and by involvement of the three odors at substituted ORN concentrations (paa, euc, −aPine); per-odor response fractions; per-glomerulus contributions; then either a concentration-sensitivity run for those odors or a targeted glomerulus gain (E20's machinery). Prediction: deficit concentrated on the substituted odors → ORN data at Yang's concentrations are the fix; spread across groups → the APL-blocked code is too sparse per glomerulus and the threshold too strong for non-ester glomeruli. Registered at Tom's request on 2026-11-05.

22. **E22, a softer per-claw knee (smooth threshold; mechanism 1, refinement).** The per-claw threshold that reproduces the coincidence rule is a hard knee, so an odor recruits KCs only through claws driven clearly above 40–70 Hz; the drive-normalization experiment and the APL-blocked sharing diagnostic both end at odors with narrow or weak input (methyl salicylate, beta-myrcene, eucalyptol, phenylacetaldehyde, 1-octanol) losing their responders while the fly recruits 10–14 % of KCs for them with APL blocked. Design: olfsysm `claw_thr_width`, a softplus knee of width w (w·log(1 + exp((x − thr)/w)), the hard knee as w → 0); bouton base at claw_thr 70 with w ∈ {0, 10, 20, 40} Hz, thresholds re-tuned at 5 %, acute APL off; then the best width at the three-mechanism grid's block-first point. Biophysically a graded dendritic nonlinearity rather than an all-or-none one. Readout: the weak odors' APL-off response fractions and the per-pair sharing deficit (primary), rate rank agreement, mean distance, excess sharing, block, rank agreement with control and TNT. Prediction: a width that recovers the weak odors' recruitment while keeping the decorrelation and the block → the knee is soft and the joint fit can use it; recovery only at widths that erase the decorrelation → the two effects are inseparable and the weak odors are an input problem; no recovery → the input is the problem. Added under the idle-host rule on 2026-11-05 after E20 and E21.

23. **E23, which KCs respond to the weak-input odors, and what wires them (data analysis; input side).** The threshold models recruit 0.2–1 % of KCs for methyl salicylate and beta-myrcene with APL blocked where the fly recruits 11–12 %, no uniform per-claw transfer fixes it, and these odors drive one or very few glomeruli in both the lab's ORN data and Hallem's. Either their ORN input is understated at the panel's concentrations, or the fly recruits KCs from a single strongly driven glomerulus. Design: from Yang's per-recording KC data, the responders to these odors with APL blocked and intact, their breadth and overlap with other odors' responders against the density expectation; from the hemibrain wiring, how many KCs have one or more claws from DL1 (methyl salicylate's glomerulus) and from beta-myrcene's few driven glomeruli, the largest population a single-glomerulus input could recruit; olfdb concentration data for these odors if any. Prediction: responders as broad as other odors' and overlapping by density → input understated (flag the odors, request ORN re-measurement); responders ≈ the single-glomerulus claw population → single-claw recruitment is real and a claw-level amplification should be modelled; responders narrow and more numerous than that population → the KC data for these odors need checking. Added under the idle-host rule on 2026-11-05 after E21 and E22.

24. **E24, input scaling for the terpenes and 1-octanol (sensitivity run; input side).** The weak-odor responder analysis found that eucalyptol, alpha-pinene, beta-myrcene and 1-octanol are recruited in the fly through ordinary broad KC populations whose co-tuning follows the odors' ORN profiles, so the model has the right glomeruli for them but too little drive (it recruits 2–7 % of KCs with APL blocked where the fly recruits 6–11 %), and their pairs carry most of the APL-blocked sharing deficit. Design: bouton base with the 70 Hz per-claw threshold, the four odors' ORN responses scaled by a common factor (1, 1.5, 2, 3; all glomeruli, no new ones), thresholds re-tuned at 5 %, acute APL off; methyl salicylate and phenylacetaldehyde left unchanged (their recruitment needs a different mechanism). Readout: the four odors' response fractions vs the data, their pairs' sharing deficit vs the rest, and the statistics that must not be lost (rank agreement with control and TNT, mean distance, block). Prediction: a factor that reaches the data's fractions and removes most of their pairs' deficit → the deficit is an input-level problem and the lab's ORN data understate these odors; fractions reached but deficit stays → the wrong KCs respond; fractions never reached → too few glomeruli for the threshold model regardless of level. Added under the idle-host rule on 2026-11-05 after E21 and E23.
25. **E25, joint fit with the structure-beyond-ORN targets and the APL-on excess profile in the loss (mechanisms 1, 2 and the APL stage; fit).** The joint fit (E10) found that under a loss made of the two matrices, the block, the response rates and monotonicity, the bouton model with breadth-weighted APL, KC–KC inhibition and APL re-balanced from claws to boutons reaches every fitted target at once, while the per-claw threshold only costs inside that loss (matrix agreement, per-odor rates; the optimizer shrinks the lateral term) yet is the one ingredient that moves the statistics the loss leaves out toward the data: the structure beyond the ORN input (against wild-type somata and against natural-source odor distances) and the APL-on excess profile. Design: the same family and optimizer, with three terms added to the loss (structure beyond ORN vs wild-type somata with APL on; natural-source alignment of the APL-off residual; the APL-on excess of the block pairs relative to the other pairs), three fits at breadth exponent 2 seeded from their E10 solutions: no threshold, 40 Hz, 70 Hz. Readout: the extended loss of each, the full scoring, and whether the threshold fits keep the lateral term. Prediction: if a threshold fit wins, the combined model becomes the reference and its per-odor rate cost the thing to explain; if the no-threshold fit still wins, the structure beyond the ORN input must come from the input side; if none reaches those targets, they need something outside this family.

26. **E26, supralinear per-claw transfer (a single strongly driven claw can fire a KC; KC dendrite).** The weak-odor responder analysis found that methyl salicylate is recruited in the fly through one claw (its responders outnumber the KCs with two claws from any one glomerulus), and the input-scaling run found that stronger input gives the terpenes and 1-octanol the right number of responders but not the fly's overlap with the fruity-ester responders: in the threshold model a KC fires only when two or more claws clear the knee, so a KC with one terpene claw among ester claws never fires for the terpene. Design: after the rectified per-claw offset each claw contributes ref x (y / ref)^p (olfsysm p.kc.claw_pow, claw_pow_ref), so for p > 1 a claw far above the knee carries a KC alone while moderately driven claws still need coincidence (a regenerative claw, the convex counterpart of the saturation of E16); bouton base with the 70 Hz threshold, p in {1, 1.5, 2, 3} at ref 50 Hz, thresholds tuned at 5 %, acute APL off, then the 40 Hz threshold at the best p. Readout: the five weak odors' APL-off response fractions, the APL-off sharing deficit by partner group (the terpenes' fruity-ester pairs and the ms / paa pairs), and what must not be lost (mean distance, the APL-on excess profile, the block, rank agreement with control and TNT, rate rank agreement). Prediction: a power that recruits the weak odors while keeping the decorrelation → single-claw recruitment is the missing ingredient and the three-mechanism point and the joint fit get it; recruitment only where the decorrelation is lost → the two demands conflict within one transfer and need glomerulus-specific gain or wiring; no recruitment → the weak odors' claws never clear the knee and the input is the problem. Added under the idle-host rule on 2026-11-05 after E23 and E24.

27. **E27, oracle gain on the weak odors' top glomeruli, and synapse-count claw strength (glomerulus-specific claw strength).** The weak-odor analyses agree that ms, paa, B-myr, euc and 1-8ol are not recruited by the threshold model because at the PN level they drive two or three glomeruli at rates no higher than the esters' five to seven, so every rule applied alike to claws favors the esters, and scaling their whole ORN profile recruits them without the fly's fruity-ester overlap; the in-model option left is glomerulus-specific claw strength. Design: bouton base with the 70 Hz per-claw threshold; a control; the PN→claw weights of the weak odors' top glomeruli (VA7m, DL1, D for ms; DM1 for B-myr and paa; DP1m, VC1 for euc and -aPine; DC2, DM5 for 1-8ol) scaled by 1.5, 2 and 3; the same gain of 2 on as many random ORN-driven glomeruli (control for any extra drive); and the anatomical version, each claw weighted by its hemibrain PN→KC synapse count (normalized to mean 1); thresholds tuned at 5 %, acute APL off. Readout: the weak odors' APL-off response fractions vs the data, the APL-off sharing deficit by pair type and partner group (the fruity-ester pairs), the ester over-sharing, and what must not be lost (rate rank agreement, mean distance, block, rank agreement with control and TNT). Prediction: recruitment with the fruity-ester overlap and the rest intact → the anatomical claw strength is carried into the combined model; recruitment without the overlap → the overlap needs another substrate; no recruitment at any gain → these odors' responders are not driven through their top glomeruli and their KC data need re-checking against the fly's own input. Added under the idle-host rule on 2026-11-05 after E23, E24 and E26.

28. **E28, the oracle glomerulus gain in the combined model, then the joint fit (glomerulus-specific claw strength x the three mechanisms).** The oracle gain (a 1.5x PN→claw gain on the eight glomeruli that carry the weak odors, on the 70 Hz threshold base) recruits the weak odors with the fruity-ester overlap and brings the structure beyond the ORN input to the data, but gives no block; the three-mechanism point (threshold 70, breadth-weighted APL exponent 2, KC–KC 100) gives the block, the control excess profile and the decorrelation level but little structure beyond the ORN input. Design: E19's block-first point at gains 1, 1.25 and 1.5 and its balanced point (threshold 40, breadth 2, KC–KC 30) at gains 1 and 1.5, each with the threshold re-tuned at the gain, breadth from the model's own APL-off counts, the KC↔APL scales re-matched and the KC–KC threshold re-tuned as in E19; then, if the point works, the gain as a fixed ingredient in the joint fit with the structure targets. Readout: the block, the control and APL-blocked agreement, the APL-on excess profile, the structure beyond the ORN input, the weak odors' fractions and sharing deficits, the rate ratio and the per-odor rate pattern, mean distance, isotonic R². Prediction: the block, the structure and the weak odors coexist with the rest kept → the combined model becomes the reference and the gain's substrate the question; the gain removes the block or the lateral term removes the gain's structure → the two compete for the same KCs and the joint fit must arbitrate; the ester fractions collapse → the gain must be smaller or odor-conditional. Added under the idle-host rule on 2026-11-05 after E27.

29. **E29, the hemibrain substrate check for the oracle glomeruli (wiring).** The oracle gain says the KC code behaves as if the weak odors drove their top glomeruli (VA7m, DL1, D, DM1, DP1m, VC1, DC2, DM5) about 1.5x harder at the PN level than the model gives them; before calling that a measurement question, the model's calyx wiring is checked against the hemibrain for those glomeruli. Design: data analysis, no simulation. Per driven glomerulus, uniglomerular PNs in the hemibrain and their calyx presynaptic sites (neuPrint per-ROI counts) vs the PNs, boutons, claws and PN→KC synapses the model's bouton wiring carries; the oracle set vs the other glomeruli by rank and a permutation test; a candidate per-glomerulus correction (hemibrain calyx output over the model's synapses, median-normalized). Readout: the per-glomerulus table, the oracle-vs-rest comparison, the candidate correction; a corrected-wiring run on the threshold base if warranted. Prediction: the oracle glomeruli under-represented by about the gain → the oracle is a wiring deficit, fix the wiring and re-run E27 / E28; not under-represented → the gain is not in the calyx anatomy and the input side is the remaining explanation. Added under the idle-host rule on 2026-11-05 after E27 and E28.

32. **E32, the ORN→PN stage inside the structure-loss joint fit (PN normalization).** The substrate check (E29) put the weak odors' problem upstream of the calyx wiring: the ORN input measurements or the ORN→PN transform. The model's PN layer (olfsysm's divisive normalization, pn_inhadd / pn_inhsc, and the PN saturation) has run at its defaults in every experiment; a normalization that depends on the number of active glomeruli is the canonical antennal-lobe mechanism that favors narrowly tuned odors at the PN level, the uniform alternative to the glomerulus gain. Design: stage 1, the PN normalization parameters swept on the bouton base with the 70 Hz per-claw threshold (pn_inhadd over the values of the drive-dependent-gain experiment, pn_inhsc at 0.5x, 2x and 4x its default), thresholds tuned at 5 %, acute APL off, scored as E27 plus the weak odors' top-glomerulus PN rates; stage 2, the 70 Hz and no-threshold structure fits with the PN parameters added to the fitted set, seeded from E25's optima, only if stage 1 finds a setting that lifts the weak odors. Readout: the weak odors' APL-off response fractions, the terpene / 1-8ol x fruity-ester sharing deficit, the natural-source alignment, and E25's statistics. Prediction: a stronger normalization recruits the weak odors without an oracle set → the gain is the antennal lobe's and the next step is PN data; the PN parameters do not move the weak odors → the problem is in the ORN measurements for ms, B-myr, paa, euc and 1-8ol; recruitment without the fruity-ester overlap → the overlap needs the oracle set's identity. Proposed by atlas on 2026-11-05 after E29; taken by hal under the idle-host rule the same day (E30 and E31, atlas's replication and APL→bouton-bound proposals, remain open).

30. **E30, replication of the structure-loss joint fits with a second wiring draw (robustness).** Every joint-fit conclusion rests on one hemibrain wiring and one seed, and the wiring-substrate experiment put the single-draw noise of the structure statistics at about 0.15; the structure-target fit's central numbers (structure beyond the ORN input vs wild type 0.54 with a threshold against 0.46 without; natural-source alignment 0.25 against 0.15) are inside that band. Design: the three structure-loss fits (no threshold, 40 Hz, 70 Hz; breadth exponent 2; 150 evaluations) re-run on a claw-permutation re-draw of the bouton wiring, seeded from their optima. Readout: every statistic of the structure-target fit as a difference between draws; the extended-loss ordering of the three models is primary. Prediction: the ordering and the structure gap repeat → the trade-off is a property of the family; the ordering flips or the gap vanishes → the result is draw noise and the claim that the threshold is selected is withdrawn until more draws exist; only the no-threshold fit moves → the threshold fits are robust and the baseline is not. Proposed by atlas on 2026-11-05 after E25; taken by hal under the idle-host rule the same evening (E31 remains open).

31. **E31, is the extreme APL→bouton scale needed? The 70 Hz structure fit with the APL→bouton scale bounded (APL's site of action).** The structure-target joint fit's best model (per-claw threshold 70 Hz) puts APL on the PN boutons at 12x the baseline's tuned APL→bouton scale with the APL→KC scale at 0.4x, and the fits with the oracle gain go further (8x to 18x): inhibition acting almost entirely presynaptically, under a low somatic threshold. The bouton-APL experiment showed presynaptic APL is what gives the odor-level drive dependence; the joint fits suggest it is also what lets a threshold model keep the block and the rate ratio while gaining structure. Design: the 70 Hz structure fit re-run from its optimum three ways, the APL→bouton scale bounded at no more than 3x the baseline (where the no-threshold and 40 Hz optima sit), fixed at the baseline's value with the other three parameters free, and free but with the APL→KC scale forced to at least 0.8x the baseline (a KC-side route). Readouts as the structure-target fit, plus the split of the loss into the fitted-statistics terms and the structure terms. Prediction: if the bounded fits lose little (within about 0.1 of extended loss), the extreme is a flat direction of the loss and the mechanism is APL on boutons at a few times the baseline; if they lose the block or the rate ratio, the presynaptic route is the only way this family holds both with a threshold, a testable statement about APL→bouton synapses; if the KC-side route recovers the loss with a higher somatic threshold, the two routes are interchangeable here and only imaging can tell them apart.

33. **E33, a re-drawn fit of the combined model (robustness of the oracle gain's structure).** The combined-model joint fits are the program's best, but the replication on re-drawn wiring showed that the absolute structure numbers of every joint fit are the real wiring's. Design: the two combined-model fits (70 and 40 Hz, gain 1.5 on the oracle set, breadth 2, structure loss) re-run on the replication's second wiring draw, seeded from their optima at the gain-tuned thresholds. Readout: the gain's effect within the draw (against the replication's threshold fits without the gain) on natural-source alignment, the block, the structure against wild-type somata and the fitted statistics. Prediction: the gain still lifts the natural-source alignment and keeps the block on the re-draw → the gain's structure is its own; the lift vanishes → it needs the real wiring; the block is lost → the coexistence is wiring-specific. Added under the idle-host rule on 2026-11-06 after E28 and E30.

34. **E34, the combined model with interpretable bounds: the oracle gain at 1.25 and 1.5 in the structure fit with the APL→bouton scale capped at 3× (glomerulus-specific claw strength × the three mechanisms, bounded).** The joint-fit arm of the combined-model experiment showed the gain and the block coexist once the four parameters are re-fitted (extended loss 1.93 / 1.94 at gain 1.5), at the cost of the control flies' per-odor rate pattern and the structure against wild type, with APL on the boutons at 8 to 18 times the baseline; the bound control showed the loss is flat above about 3× on that scale, so those parameters are not interpretable as stated, and the point results suggested a gain of 1.25 keeps part of the block. Design: four fits with the structure loss at breadth exponent 2 and the APL→bouton scale capped at 3×, gain 1.5 and 1.25 at the 70 and 40 Hz thresholds, seeded from the joint-fit arm's optima. Readouts as the structure-target fit (extended loss and its split, block, natural-source alignment, the APL-on excess profile, per-odor rate rank agreements, structure vs wild type, fitted parameters). Prediction: if the capped gain-1.5 fits stay within about 0.1 of the uncapped ones, the combined model with capped presynaptic APL is the reference model and its parameters can be stated; if they lose the block or the natural-source alignment, the gain needs the extreme presynaptic APL that the no-gain fit does not; if gain 1.25 keeps most of the structure gain at half the per-odor rate cost, the gain's size becomes a parameter to fit; if it loses the natural-source alignment, the recruitment is a threshold phenomenon in the gain.
35. **E35, divisive APL inhibition: shunting at the KCs and gain reduction at the PN boutons in place of the subtractive forms (the algebra of APL's inhibition).** In the KC data, blocking APL output multiplies every odor's response fraction by about the same factor (1.65 to 2.90 across the 16 odors, median 2.2, the spread at the bootstrap noise of the recordings), whatever the odor's fraction; every fit's APL is odor-selective instead, removing the sparse odors' responders preferentially (the 70 Hz structure fit without the gain: esters ×1.7, the weak-input odors ×2.8, B-myr ×12) and, with the gain, over-suppressing 2h, IaA and 2-but (×3.1 to 3.6; data ×1.8 to 2.4), which is where the gain's per-odor rate cost comes from and why ms, paa and B-myr stay the sparsest odors with APL on in every fit. The model subtracts a fixed amount from each bouton's rate and each claw's drive, so an odor whose responders sit just above threshold loses a larger share of them. APL is GABAergic onto the KCs, where inhibition is shunting (divisive) in the usual biophysics, and presynaptic inhibition of PN terminals is described as a gain reduction, so the experiment replaces each subtraction with a division (the drive divided by one plus the inhibition) at the KCs, at the boutons, or both, with the APL scales tuned as before. Design: on the bouton base with connectome APL weights, tuned to 5 % with APL on and APL removed acutely, the three divisive forms at no per-claw threshold and at 70 Hz against the subtractive references, then the best form at the three-mechanism point; if the suppression becomes uniform without losing the block or the structure, the structure-loss joint fit with that form at 70 and 40 Hz, without and with the 1.5× gain. Readout: the suppression uniformity (the spread across odors of the log ratio of the APL-off to the APL-on response fraction; data 0.15 at a noise of 0.13, the fits 0.3 to 0.55) and its rank correlation with the APL-on fraction (data about 0, the fits strongly negative), with the per-odor rate rank agreements and all the usual statistics. Prediction: if a divisive form makes the suppression uniform and keeps the block and the structure, the subtractive algebra was the reason for the sparse odors' APL-on deficit and the gain's rate cost should shrink, the first non-oracle route to the per-odor rates; uniform suppression with the block lost would mean the block needs the subtractive form's selectivity; suppression that stays odor-selective under every form would make the uniformity a property of the KC input distributions rather than of the inhibition, pointing back at the input side.
36. **E36, KC-spike-driven APL in the combined model: APL driven by the KCs' spikes (plus the bouton rates) instead of the summed claw input (APL's input).** The divisive APL inhibition experiment found that the model's odor-selective APL suppression comes from what drives APL, not from the algebra of its inhibition: in the bouton base, used since the bouton coincidence experiment, APL sums the input to all claws, so its activity barely changes across odors (peak 28 for ms to 37 for pa at 70 Hz) and every odor gets about the same threshold shift, which removes most of a sparse odor's few marginal responders. Driving APL by the KCs' spikes on the same base at 70 Hz reproduces the data's uniform suppression (spread across odors of the log ratio of APL-off to APL-on responders 0.12 against the data's 0.15; rank correlation with the APL-on fraction −0.12 against −0.12) and lifts the per-odor rate rank agreement with APL on from 0.57 to 0.67. Design: the three-mechanism points (70 Hz, breadth 2, KC–KC 100; 40 Hz, breadth 2, KC–KC 30) with spike-driven APL at gain 1 and 1.5 on the eight oracle glomeruli, then the structure-loss joint fits with spike-driven APL (gain 1.5, APL→bouton capped at 3×; and without the gain at 70 Hz), against their claw-driven counterparts. Readout: the suppression uniformity, the per-odor rate rank agreements, the block, the structure beyond the ORN input, the natural-source alignment and the extended loss. Prediction: if spike-driven APL keeps the block and the structure, the reference model's APL becomes spike-driven and the gain's per-odor rate cost shrinks; if the block is lost, the block depends on the claw-driven APL hitting all odors alike, a tension between two features of the data; if the fits recover selectivity by other means, the structure targets favour odor-selective suppression within this model family.
37. **E37, spike-only APL: the bouton→APL input removed from the fitted models, and where their remaining odor-selective suppression comes from (APL's input, continued).** KC-spike-driven APL made the model's suppression as evenly spread across odors as the data's at 40 Hz, but the sparse odors still lose relatively more of their responders (rank correlation of the per-odor suppression with the APL-on fraction −0.67 to −0.78 against −0.12 in the data) and the per-odor rate rank agreement with APL on did not move (0.37–0.52, against 0.74–0.83 with APL off). Two candidate sources remain: the PN bouton rates, which still feed APL in the spike-driven base and which the divisive-inhibition experiment showed keep APL partly selective, and the KC–KC term, inhibition in proportion to the neighbours' activity that survives APL block. Design: at the three spike-driven capped optima (70 Hz without the gain, 70 and 40 Hz with the 1.5× gain), every fitted scale held and the bouton→APL weights set to zero, the KC–KC scale set to zero, or both, with the somatic threshold re-tuned to the fit's APL-on response count; then the structure-loss joint fits re-run with APL driven by KC spikes alone (bouton→APL fixed at zero, APL→bouton still fitted and capped at 3×), seeded from the spike-driven optima. Readout: the suppression uniformity and its rank correlation with the APL-on fraction, the per-odor rate rank agreements, the block, the structure beyond the ORN input, the natural-source alignment and the extended loss. Prediction: if removing the bouton→APL input makes the suppression uniform with the block and the structure kept, the per-odor rate pattern with APL on should rise toward its APL-off level and the reference model's APL becomes KC-driven only; if only removing the KC–KC term does it, the block and the uniform suppression compete; if neither does, the selectivity is a property of the input distributions and the per-odor rate pattern is an input question.
38. **E38, Hallem's receptor data as the ORN input for the weak-input odors: a bound on the input uncertainty and a direct test of single-glomerulus recruitment (the input side).** The APL-blocked sharing diagnostic traced the models' too-low sharing among non-ester pairs with APL output blocked, the residual disagreement with the APL output blocked (TNT) matrix and most of the per-odor rate deficit to five weak-input odors (ms, B-myr, euc, paa, 1-8ol), and the oracle gain showed that 1.5× more drive from eight glomeruli recruits them with the fly's overlap. The lab's ORN input and Hallem and Carlson's receptor data agree for the esters and aldehydes and disagree for exactly these odors: methyl salicylate is a one-glomerulus odor in Hallem (DL1 at 258 Hz; 70 Hz in the lab's data), phenylacetaldehyde and 1-octanol peak in different glomeruli in the two datasets, beta-myrcene is weak in both. Design: the model run on input matrices in which the four Hallem odors (eucalyptol is not in Hallem's set) take Hallem's spike deltas on the 16 glomeruli the two datasets share (the other 20 keeping the lab's values, or set to zero, or the elementwise maximum of the two), one odor at a time and together, on the bouton base with the 70 Hz per-claw threshold tuned at 5 % and at the reference model's fitted parameters with the threshold re-tuned; Hallem's 10^-2 is one log unit above Yang's concentration for three of the four odors, so these inputs are upper bounds. Readout: the weak odors' response fractions with APL off and on against the fly's, the APL-off sharing deficit by pair type, the structure beyond the ORN input, the rank agreement with both matrices, the per-odor rate rank agreements, the block, and the claw composition of the recruited ms responders. Prediction: if Hallem's input recruits ms, paa and 1-octanol at the fly's fraction with the fly's overlap, the lab's ORN data understate these odors and the oracle gain stands for an input error with a concrete measurement target (DL1's response to methyl salicylate); if 258 Hz in one glomerulus recruits nothing, single-claw recruitment is a circuit property the model lacks whatever the input; if ms is recruited by a broad population instead of the fly's narrow one, the fly's methyl salicylate channel is more than DL1 drive.
39. **E39, decomposing the oracle gain: which of the eight glomeruli recruit which odor, carry the natural-source alignment, and cost the esters' response rates (glomerulus-specific claw strength, by glomerulus).** The 1.5× gain on VA7m, DL1, D, DM1, DP1m, VC1, DC2 and DM5 is in the reference model, its biological reading (understated input or a glomerulus-specific gain) is open, and its benefits (the weak odors recruited, the natural-source alignment at the data's level) and its cost (the control flies' per-odor rate rank agreement falling to 0.41–0.47 because the fit's APL then removes the esters' responders) were measured for the set as a whole. Design: at the reference model's fitted parameters (40 Hz, breadth 2, KC–KC 18, APL→bouton capped at 3×; and the 70 Hz capped fit), the gain applied to each glomerulus alone, to all but one, to the per-odor sets (VA7m, DL1, D for methyl salicylate; DM1 for phenylacetaldehyde and beta-myrcene; DP1m, VC1 for eucalyptol and alpha-pinene; DC2, DM5 for 1-octanol), and to the full set and none, with the breadth weights recomputed and the somatic threshold re-tuned to the fit's APL-on response count. Readout: the per-odor response fractions with APL on and off, the per-odor rate rank agreements, the natural-source alignment, the structure against wild-type somata, the block, the rank agreement with both matrices, and a glomerulus-by-statistic table of marginal contributions. Prediction: if the alignment and the recruitment come from one subset and the ester cost from another, a smaller gain set keeps the structure without the cost and the measurement list shortens to it; if every statistic needs most of the set, the gain is a distributed input correction; if the ester cost appears with any glomerulus, it belongs to the fit's operating point rather than to the gained glomeruli.
40. **E40, joint fits with a reduced gain set: DC2 alone, and DC2 + DM5 + DM1, optionally with Hallem's ms input (glomerulus-specific claw strength, which glomeruli).** The decomposition of the oracle gain found, at the reference model's fitted parameters, that DC2 alone carries the natural-source alignment and (at 40 Hz) the full set's extended loss, DM5 the block and DM1 the APL-on response-rate pattern, while the five weak-odor glomeruli recruit their odors without adding structure and are each dispensable for the loss; but every variant sat at parameters fitted for the eight-glomerulus set. Design: the structure-loss joint fit with the APL→bouton cap and the gain of 1.5 on DC2 alone and on DC2, DM5 and DM1, at 40 and 70 Hz, breadth exponent 2, seeded from the bounded combined model's optima; optionally DC2's gain fitted, and the core set with Hallem's ms input replacing the gain's ms entries. Readout: as the bounded combined model (extended loss and its split, block, natural-source alignment, structure vs wild-type somata, per-odor rate rank agreements, the weak odors' fractions). Prediction: a reduced set within about 0.1 of the eight-glomerulus loss shrinks the reference model's gain and splits the measurement question into the recruitment glomeruli and DC2's drive; DC2 alone within that margin makes the structural ingredient a single strongly driven glomerulus's gain, testable in PN bouton data; neither means the gain stays distributed once re-fitted.
41. **E41, the sub-threshold gain of the per-claw transfer (mechanism 1, the claw nonlinearity).** The per-claw threshold discards PN rates below 40–70 Hz entirely, while claw calcium responses imaged in vivo are graded down to weak inputs and KCs respond to weak stimuli; the softer knee kept the zero far below the knee. Design: a two-slope claw transfer (slope g below the threshold, 1 above, continuous), g in 0, 0.25, 0.5, 1, on the bouton base at 70 and 40 Hz tuned at 5 %, and g in 0.25, 0.5 at the reference model's parameters with the threshold re-tuned. Readout: as the per-claw saturation and oracle-gain experiments (mean distances, the excess profile, the natural-source alignment, the structure vs wild-type somata, the rank agreements, the weak odors' fractions, the block). Prediction: if a sub-threshold gain of a quarter to a half keeps the threshold's decorrelation and structure while recruiting the weak odors, the rule is a gain change at a knee and not a dead zone; if the structure falls monotonically toward the no-threshold model, the dead zone is what matters and the rule predicts no claw response below about 50 Hz; if the esters over-share as the weak odors are recruited, the sub-threshold gain is the knee in disguise.
42. **E42, the reduced gain sets on a re-drawn wiring (mechanism 4, glomerulus-specific claw strength).** On the real wiring the gain on DC2 alone matched or beat the eight-glomerulus reference model's extended loss and the DC2 + DM5 + DM1 core with Hallem's ms input was lowest, but re-drawing the wiring within KC type moves such fits by 1 to 2.5 units of loss, so a difference of 0.1 on one draw does not rank the gain sets. Design: the capped structure fits (breadth exponent 2, APL→bouton scale at most 3×) of the eight-glomerulus gain, of DC2 alone and of the Hallem-ms core, at 70 and 40 Hz, re-run on the second within-type claw-permutation draw of the bouton wiring (the draw of the combined model's re-draw), each seeded from its real-wiring optimum. Readout: the extended loss and its terms, the block, the ρ on odor-odor distances with both matrices, the response rate ρ, the structure vs wild-type somata and the natural-source alignment, with the real-wiring fits alongside. Prediction: if DC2 alone (or the Hallem-ms core) is again at or below the eight-glomerulus loss with the same trade, the reference model's gain shrinks to it; if the eight-glomerulus model is clearly better on the draw, the real-wiring ordering was the draw's and the gain set stays; if the ordering differs between thresholds or between the matrix and structure terms, the choice is a loss-weighting question.
43. **E43, trial-to-trial variability in the model (a methods check: noise through the model, and the data's pipeline applied to model trials).** Every model statistic comes from one deterministic run, while the KC matrices are built from three-trial means of noisy recordings: two single trials of the same odor are 0.44 (control) / 0.39 (APL output blocked) apart in cosine distance, which inflates the measured mean pair distances (0.84 / 0.66) over their noise-corrected values (0.79 / 0.58). Design: olfsysm's Gaussian noise on the PN rates (no C++ change), with the level set so the model's single-trial same-odor floor matches the data's, 10 pseudo-recordings of 3 trials each, on the 70 Hz bouton base and on the reference model, APL on and off; the data's noise-floor analysis run on the model trials. Readout: the single-trial and trial-mean floors; the raw mean distance of three-trial means against the raw data and the deterministic run; the corrected mean distance against the deterministic run; the many-trial grand mean against the deterministic run (response rate, per-odor fractions, the weak odors); the ρ on odor-odor distances with the data and the block on the noisy matrices. Prediction: if the noisy model processed like the data lands on the raw data while its corrected statistics equal the deterministic run's, the deterministic comparisons against corrected targets are the right ones; if noise through the thresholds raises the expected response rate or recruits the weak odors, every experiment's operating point is noise-dependent and part of the weak odors' deficit is a deterministic-model artefact; if implausibly large PN noise is needed to reach the data's floors, the variability is downstream of the PNs and the ceilings, not the floors, are the right comparison.
44. **E44, the operating point (a shared assumption: the response fraction the models are tuned to).** Every model is tuned so that 5 % of KCs respond with APL on, while the control flies' fraction is 6.8 % and the flies with APL output blocked 15.0 %; the knockout baseline varied 5 % against 10 % on the bases only, and every write-up since the Hallem-input test compares response fractions with the data at a different mean. Design: the bouton base at 70 and 40 Hz tuned to 3.4, 5, 6.8 and 10 %; the capped structure fit of the reference model re-fitted with its response-rate target at 6.8 % (and the DC2-alone model if the re-draw favours it); and the reference model with only its somatic threshold re-tuned to 6.8 %. Readout: as the reduced-gain-set fits, with the 5 % fits alongside, the response fractions compared with the data at the same mean. Prediction: if the statistics move little, the convention is harmless and the comparisons can be made at the data's mean from now on; if the block, the structure or the natural-source alignment change materially, the operating point is a hidden parameter of every mechanism result and the program should fit at the data's fraction; if the weak odors' APL-off fractions rise toward the data, part of their deficit is the operating point and the gain needed shrinks.
45. **E45, the glomerulus gain at the data's operating point (mechanism 4, glomerulus-specific claw strength, fitted at the control flies' response fraction).** The operating-point experiment found that the reference model re-fitted at the control flies' 6.8 % keeps its block, its agreement with both KC matrices and its structure, but over-recruits the weak odors with APL off, so part of what the gain of 1.5 on eight glomeruli supplied at 5 % comes free at the data's fraction; the gain's size, its set and whether it is needed at all were all settled at 5 %. Design: structure fits with the response-rate target at 6.8 % and the APL→bouton scale capped at 3×, at 40 and 70 Hz, for no gain, for the eight glomeruli at 1.25 (the 1.5 fits being the operating-point experiment's), and for the core of DC2, DM5 and DM1 with Hallem's ms input at 1.25 and 1.5, each seeded from the nearest 5 % optimum. Readout: as the reduced-gain-set fits, with the 5 % fits alongside, plus the weak odors' APL-off response rate and its share of the mean. Prediction: if no gain already keeps the block and brings the weak odors to the data's share, the gain was compensating the 5 % convention and the reference model loses it; if 1.25 does while 1.5 over-recruits, the gain shrinks and the measurement request stays with a smaller effect size; if the block or the structure needs 1.5 whatever the weak odors do, the gain's job is the block and the structure, and the over-recruitment is a cost to weigh against a gain on fewer glomeruli or the core.
46. **E46, the operating-point fits on a re-drawn wiring (mechanism 4, glomerulus-specific claw strength, re-fitted on the second wiring draw at the control flies' response fraction).** The gain fits at the data's operating point gave their two best models by 0.1–0.2 of extended loss, the eight glomeruli at 1.25 at the 70 Hz per-claw threshold and the DC2, DM5 and DM1 core with Hallem's ms input at 1.5 at 40 Hz, and the replications on re-drawn wiring showed that differences of that size can be the wiring draw's, with every re-draw losing most of the block. Design: the same structure fits (response-rate target 6.8 %, APL→bouton scale capped at 3×) on the second within-type claw-permutation draw, for the two best models and for the reference model (the eight at 1.5 at both thresholds), each seeded from its real-wiring optimum. Readout: as the gain fits at the operating point, each draw-2 fit beside its real-wiring counterpart and beside the 5 % draw-2 fits. Prediction: if the ordering holds, the reference model's gain shrinks to 1.25 at 70 Hz and the 40 Hz choice between the eight and the core is a loss-weighting question; if it flips, the gain stays at 1.5 and its size at the data's operating point is 1.25–1.5 within the draw spread; if every fit loses the block on the draw as at 5 %, the block's dependence on the real hemibrain wiring stands as a result of its own.
