47. **E47, the wiring feature behind the block (mechanism 4 and the PN→KC substrate: which structure of the hemibrain claw wiring the fitted models' block depends on).** Every re-drawn wiring loses most of the fruity × small-ester block that the real hemibrain claw wiring makes once the APL, lateral and threshold parameters are fitted with the structure targets (the replication on re-drawn wiring, the re-draw of the reduced gain sets), while unfitted the real wiring scored below its own shuffles (the substrate experiment). Design: the reference models at the control flies' response fraction (the eight glomeruli at 1.5 at 70 and 40 Hz, and at 1.25 at 70 Hz), at their fitted APL and KC–KC scales with only the somatic threshold re-tuned, on the real wiring and on five seeds each of four shuffles that keep different things: claw permutations (each KC's and each glomerulus' claw counts), Curveball shuffles (each KC's number of distinct glomeruli and each glomerulus' number of KCs), each with or without KC-type preservation; then refits on one Curveball draw of each kind. Readout: the block, the agreement with both KC matrices, the structure and natural-source statistics and the response-rate pattern per shuffle class against the real wiring, with each draw's co-input correlation with the real wiring. Prediction: if the Curveball shuffles keep the block, it rides on the wiring's degree structure and is not draw-specific; if only the within-type Curveball keeps it, the KC types' glomerulus preferences carry it; if all four lose it, the specific co-occurrence of glomeruli on KCs in the hemibrain carries the block and the wiring is a mechanism in its own right; if the unfitted and the fitted answers differ, the fitted parameters compensate for the wiring.
48. **E48, the block target through the noise (methods: the fitted target for the fruity × small-ester block, given the data's trial-to-trial noise).** The trial-to-trial variability experiment showed that a deterministic model processed like the data (matched per-trial noise, three-trial means over ten recordings) loses a third of its block (+0.054 to about +0.035), that the noise correction applied to such model trials gives the deterministic value back for two of three noise models, and that the same correction applied to the data gives +0.072 where the measured block is +0.055; so the block a deterministic model should be fitted to is nearer +0.072, and the fits are about a quarter short on their central target. Design: the reference model (the eight glomeruli at 1.5, at 70 and 40 Hz) and the eight at 1.25 at 70 Hz re-fitted at the control flies' response fraction with the loss's block target at the noise-corrected value, then these fits and their measured-target counterparts scored through the matched per-trial threshold jitter and the data's pipeline. Readout: the processed block (raw, corrected and on the grand mean), the floors, mean distances, agreement with both KC matrices and the response-rate pattern through the pipeline, beside the deterministic statistics and the fitted scales. Prediction: if the larger-target fits land on the measured +0.055 through the pipeline at no cost elsewhere, the fits' block target becomes the corrected value; if they land there at a cost in agreement, structure or response rates, the trade-off is a weighting decision; if the block saturates near +0.055 whatever the target, the mechanisms in hand cannot make a bigger block and that shortfall is the next target; if the processed block overshoots, the correction-based estimate is biased for the model's noise and the final models should be scored through the pipeline rather than against a corrected target.
49. **E49, the low-concentration KC data as constraints (the per-claw threshold's dead zone and the weak-odor reading tested on Yang's diagnostic-odor mixture and dilute natural-mixture KC data).** Every constraint so far is the APL16 panel at 10^-3 to 10^-2; the per-claw threshold every good model carries is a dead zone for weak input, and the models' weak-odor problem is an input-level one. In Yang's other panels the input is weak by construction: in control flies 2h and ma at 10^-7 recruit 5–6 % of KCs and farn at 10^-3 4 %, with APL output blocked two to three times as many, and the binary mixtures recruit fewer KCs than the union of their components. Design: fixed-parameter predictions (nothing re-tuned) of the threshold base and the fitted models (the reference model at 5 % and 6.8 %, the eight at 1.25 at 70 Hz) on the APL16 panel plus the low-concentration odors with ORN input within one log unit (2h, ma, farn, ea; substitutions flagged) and the mixtures with the components' input summed, with APL on and acutely off, deterministic and through the matched per-trial threshold jitter. Readout: per-odor response fractions against the data's, the APL-off/APL-on ratio per odor (data 2.3–3.1), the responders' breadth, and the mixtures against the union of the components. Prediction: if the models recruit the low-concentration odors near the data's fractions with the data's APL ratio, the dead zone is not what limits weak responses and the APL16 weak-odor deficit is odor-specific input; if they give near-zero responses even with the overstated input, the per-claw threshold is falsified as a hard rectifier at low concentration and a sub-threshold slope or noise through the threshold has to carry them; if the fractions are right but the APL ratio wrong, APL takes a larger share of weak odors' responders in the flies than in the model; if the model's mixtures are union-like where the flies' are sub-additive, a within-mixture suppression the model lacks.
50. **E50, the fitted block on more shuffle draws (methods: whether the block's recovery under re-fitting on shuffled wirings is a property of the kind of shuffle or of one draw).** The wiring-feature experiment (E47) found that, re-fitted on one draw, the shuffles that ignore KC type make the data's block in full while the within-type shuffles keep the least, which re-reads the re-drawn-wiring experiments (E30, E33, E42, E46; all within-type claw permutations) as having used the kind of shuffle on which the fitted block is least recoverable; at fixed scales, though, the draw-to-draw spread of the block within a kind was as large as the differences between kinds. Design: the 40 Hz reference model at the control flies' 6.8 % re-fitted (the structure loss, breadth exponent 2, APL→bouton capped at 3×, 150 evaluations from the real-wiring optimum) on seeds 1, 3 and 4 of the plain claw permutation and of the within-type Curveball, the two extreme kinds, each draw having its fixed-scale counterpart in E47's part A. Readout: per kind, the block, the extended loss, agreement with both KC matrices, response rate ρ, structure vs wild-type somata, the natural-source alignment (APL on / off / on minus off) and the fitted scales, as mean ± sd over four draws. Prediction: if the plain claw permutation keeps the block on every draw and the within-type Curveball loses part of it on every draw, the fitted block is a property of the kind of shuffle and E47's re-reading stands; if the kinds overlap over draws, the recovery is draw-specific and the claim narrows to the block being recoverable on some global shuffles, with the natural-source alignment the one wiring-specific result; if both kinds recover it on most draws, the re-draws' losses were partly local optima and re-fits on draws need several starts.
51. **E51, the noise-robust block (the fitting criterion for the central target: fitting the fruity × small-ester block through the per-trial threshold jitter, and what carries a block that survives it).** The block target through the noise (E48) found that every fit's block, +0.052 to +0.061 deterministically, shrinks to +0.01–0.025 once the model is run with the trial-to-trial noise that reproduces the KC data's same-odor floors and its trials are averaged and compared as the data are, that the fitted block is carried by a few pairs (2h and pa with eb and ep, over a large negative 2h / 2-but excess) made by KCs near threshold, and that no block target raises it, while the data's +0.055 is itself such a measurement with all nine pairs contributing. Design: (A) where the fitted block lives: for the reference model at 6.8 % and the eight glomeruli at 1.25 at 70 Hz, each block pair's APL-on excess decomposed by KC against those KCs' threshold margins, and each pair's excess per pseudo-recording from E48's trials; (B) a noise-aware loss for the joint fit, K fixed per-KC threshold-jitter draws with common random numbers around the KC simulation, the block term (or every term) computed on the trial mean of the K runs, three fits at 6.8 % on the measured +0.055 (the eight at 1.5 at 40 and 70 Hz, the eight at 1.25 at 70 Hz); (C) the new fits through E48's pipeline beside their deterministic-fitted counterparts. Readout: the processed block and which pairs carry it, the floors, ρ on odor-odor distances, the response-rate pattern, the natural-source alignment (three numbers), and the deterministic statistics of the new fits beside the old. Prediction: if the noise-fitted models reach a processed block near +0.055 at little cost, the fragility was the fitting criterion and the program's fits move to the noise-aware loss; if it stays at or below +0.03, the mechanisms in hand cannot make a noise-robust block of the data's size and the pairs that stay negative name the next mechanism target; a block spread over the nine pairs as in the data, or a pure weighting trade, are the other readings. Proposed by viki on 2026-11-08 from E48, E47 and E43; moved into the list by atlas under the idle-host rule.
52. **E52, the glomerulus pairs behind the natural-source alignment (wiring: which glomerulus pairs the hemibrain claw wiring puts on common KCs beyond every shuffle's margins, whether they are the natural-source pairs, and whether removing only their excess removes the alignment from the fitted models).** The wiring-feature experiment (E47) and the more-draws experiment (E50) found that a model re-fitted on any of the four shuffle classes can recover the block but never the natural-source alignment with APL off (0.20–0.32 on eight re-fitted draws against 0.37 on the real wiring and 0.36 in the KC data); all four classes keep the wiring's margins (claw counts per KC and per glomerulus, or the binary KC × glomerulus degrees, per KC type or not) and destroy which glomeruli share a KC beyond what the margins imply, so the alignment rides on glomerulus co-input structure, the feature the connectome literature reports as non-random (Zheng et al. 2022). Design: (A) for each of the 1431 glomerulus pairs of the bouton base's claw wiring, the number of KCs with claws from both, against 200 draws of each shuffle class (excess over the null mean, z, empirical p; "over-shared" if above every class's null); (B) a wiring-sharing score per megamat odor pair (the excess weighted by the two odors' ORN input to the two glomeruli, and the same from the raw counts and the null mean), correlated with the hyperbolic natural-source distance, with the KC data's residuals around the linear ORN prediction in both genotypes, and with the fitted real-wiring model's APL-off residual minus the shuffle draws'; (C) a targeted lesion that removes only the over-shared pairs' excess by claw swaps that keep every margin, and a control lesion of the same size among other pairs, each on three seeds under the three reference models at their fitted scales with the threshold re-tuned to 6.8 %. Readout: the over-shared pairs and their glomeruli; the sharing scores' ρ with the natural-source distance and with the residuals; under the lesions the natural-source alignment (APL on / off / on minus off), the block, structure vs wild-type somata, ρ on odor-odor distances and response rate ρ beside E47's real-wiring and shuffle rows. Prediction: if a small, natural-source-organized set of over-shared pairs exists and its targeted removal drops the APL-off alignment to the shuffle level while the control lesion does not, the hemibrain's natural-source structure sits in identified glomerulus pairs; if the excess is distributed or the targeted lesion leaves the alignment, the alignment is carried by the whole co-input structure and wiring controls must preserve the co-input matrix; if no pair exceeds every class's null, the alignment comes from how the per-type claw-count structure meets the odors. Proposed by hal on 2026-11-09 from E47's and E50's next steps; moved into the list under the idle-host rule.
53. **E53, KC–KC lateral inhibition as the carrier of a noise-robust block (mechanism 3 and the fitting criterion: joint fits with the KC–KC scale held at 30, started at the operating-point optima, scored through the matched trial noise).** The noise-robust block experiment (E51) found that the joint fits' block through the matched trial noise does not move under a noise-aware criterion (the fits follow their own nine draws), but that two simplex vertices the optimizer visited and left for the eight at 1.25 at 70 Hz (fixed per-claw threshold 60 instead of 78, KC–KC 27 and 33 instead of 12) keep +0.028 / +0.034 of block through independent noise against the optimum's +0.009 and the data's +0.055, paying with the APL-off response rate per trial (0.12 against 0.14; data 0.15) and response rate ρ (0.36–0.38 from 0.40); E51's part A says why KC–KC inhibition could do this: the block is carried by ordinary APL-on responders that APL holds at threshold, and KC–KC inhibition sparsens by competition among co-active KCs rather than by lowering every KC's drive. Design: part 1, the deterministic structure loss at the control flies' 6.8 % with the KC–KC scale held at 30 and the other three parameters fitted, started exactly at the seed's optimum with its KC–KC replaced, for the eight at 1.25 at 70 Hz (from E45's optimum) and the reference model, the eight at 1.5 at 40 Hz (from E44's), each through the trial-noise pipeline with E48's settings (σ calibrated per model on three tuning trials, the threshold re-tuned to hold the per-trial APL-on response rate, ten pseudo-recordings of three trials); part 2, KC–KC held at 20 and 45 for a base that keeps the block, to map the trade-off between the block through the noise and the APL-off statistics. Readout: beside the seeds' rows and E51's vertex rows, the deterministic block, the block through the noise (three-trial means and grand mean), the loss and its terms (the APL-off response rate and its per-odor pattern, the rate ratio), the floors, ρ on odor-odor distances APL on / off, response rate ρ, the natural-source alignment (APL on / off / on minus off), the fitted threshold and APL scales, and which pairs carry the block through the noise. Prediction: if the block through the noise stays at or above +0.025 with the APL-off response rate recovered to within 0.01 of the data's and the loss within about 0.2 of the seed's, KC–KC inhibition carries a noise-robust block in this family and part 2 maps the trade-off for the weighting question; if it falls back below +0.015 once the APL-off rate is recovered, the vertices' block came with their lower threshold and lower APL-off rate and the direction is closed; if the fit cannot recover the APL-off rate at KC–KC 30, that strength is incompatible with the data's APL-off statistics in this family; if the block survives for one base only, the mechanism depends on the operating point and part 2 runs on that base. Proposed by atlas on 2026-11-09 from E51's vertex result; moved into the list by viki under the idle-host rule.
54. **E54, the gain and the wiring behind the natural-source alignment (wiring and gain: whether the over-shared glomerulus pairs' share of the alignment is the food-glomerulus convergence itself or the glomerulus gain riding on it, and whether a re-fit on the lesioned wiring recovers it).** The glomerulus-pairs experiment (E52) found two wiring carriers of the fitted models' natural-source alignment, the KC-type organization of the glomerular input and the within-type over-sharing of the food glomeruli, the second shown by a lesion of the 55 over-shared pairs' excess that took the fixed-scale alignment with APL off from 0.36 to 0.31 while a matched control lesion did not; but four of the eight gained glomeruli are in those pairs (DM1 in six), so the lesion also redistributes gained claws, and the no-gain fits at 6.8 % have an alignment of only 0.25–0.27 against 0.36–0.37 with the gain. Design: (A) the three reference models at fixed scales with the gain set to 1 on the real wiring, beside the no-gain fits; (B) E52's targeted and control lesions under the two no-gain fits at fixed scales; (C) under the gained models the lesion split into the 15 over-shared pairs with ORN data that involve a gained glomerulus and the 17 that do not, each with a count-matched control; (D) the 40 Hz reference model re-fitted on a targeted-lesion draw (structure loss, exact start at the real-wiring optimum). Readout: the natural-source alignment (APL on / off / on minus off), the block, response rate ρ, structure vs wild-type somata and the loss, each lesion against its control and the real wiring. Prediction: if the no-gain models lose the alignment under the lesion too and both subsets carry part of the drop, the pairwise carrier is the convergence itself, amplified by the gain; if the no-gain models do not move and only the gained-involving pairs carry it, E52's pairwise share was the gain's redistribution and the wiring carrier is the type structure alone; if the re-fit recovers the alignment, the fixed-scale drop is compensable. Proposed by hal on 2026-11-09 from E52's caveats and next steps; moved into the list under the idle-host rule.
55. **E55, the margin proxy for the block through the noise (the fitting criterion: whether the deterministic threshold margins of each KC × odor entry predict a model's block through independent trial noise, so that noise-robustness can be scored without simulating the noise).** The noise-robust block experiment (E51) found that fitting the block on jittered runs needs far more draws than a fit can afford, and that the block's fragility is a property of the deterministic run: APL holds the APL-on response at threshold, half of all APL-on responses sit within one jitter σ, and each KC × odor entry's threshold margin (the factor on its threshold at which its response appears or disappears) gives its probability of changing on a jittered trial. Design: for every fitted model with pipeline rows (the 5 % reference model, the operating-point fits, the block-target re-fits and their exact-start repeats, the noise-aware fits and their vertices, the KC–KC-held fits), the deterministic margin table by E51 part A's method, the margin statistics, and a flip-model prediction of the block through the noise (every entry changes state independently with its probability; ten pseudo-recordings of three trials processed as the pipeline processes model trials), compared with the pipeline's observed block across models and per pair; and the cost of the margin computation per model. Readout: predicted against observed block through the noise (three-trial means and grand mean), the mean distances and floors, per model and per pair. Prediction: if the flip model tracks the pipeline across models, a margin-based term can stand in for jittered runs in the loss and the next experiment fits with it; if it ranks the models but with an offset, it is a ranking criterion to calibrate; if it does not track, the network's response to the jitter decides the block and noise-robustness has to be simulated. Proposed by atlas on 2026-11-09 from E51's next steps; moved into the list by atlas under the idle-host rule.
56. **E56, the network-aware margin proxy (the fitting criterion: whether one or two passes of the KC–KC loop, each KC replayed with the other KCs' replayed spikes feeding its inhibition, recover what the single-KC margins miss of the block through the noise).** The margin proxy (E55) found that replaying each KC at the pipeline's jittered thresholds with the network held at the deterministic run reproduces the pipeline exactly without KC–KC inhibition and misses, in proportion to KC–KC / threshold, what the inhibition does with the jitter, most where the noise-robust block's vertices and the KC–KC-held fits carry block through the noise. Design: for the same 21 models, from the reconstructed drives, a second and third replay pass in which each KC's KC–KC inhibition comes from the other KCs' replayed spikes of the same trial, at the pipeline's own jittered thresholds; per pass the pipeline's statistics and their agreement with the pipeline across models, and the convergence between passes. Readout: the block through the noise, the floors, response rates and mean distances per pass against the pipeline; the remaining network share per pass against KC–KC / threshold. Prediction: if a pass or two brings the strong-KC–KC models within the pipeline's sampling error, a noise-robustness term with the network's response exists at a few times a deterministic evaluation's cost and goes into the loss next; if the passes converge elsewhere, the proxy is a different model of the noise; if they do not converge, the recurrent loop needs the simulation. Proposed by atlas on 2026-11-09 from E55's next steps; moved into the list by atlas under the idle-host rule.
57. **E57, building the natural-source alignment back (wiring: the food-glomerulus convergence transplanted into shuffled wirings, the sufficiency test of the two carriers).** The glomerulus-pairs (E52) and gain-and-wiring (E54) experiments found the fitted models' natural-source alignment to have two wiring carriers, the KC-type organization of the glomerular input and the food-glomerulus convergence (32 over-shared pairs that have to be removed as a whole, and that carry the alignment only through the gain), both shown by removal. Design: the co-input lesion extended to raise a pair's shared-KC count by margin-preserving claw swaps; (A) within-type Curveball draws (type structure kept) with the 32 pairs' shared-KC counts raised to the real wiring's, (B) the same on plain Curveball draws (type structure destroyed), (C) the same number of raising swaps on count-matched control pairs, three draws each, the three reference models at their fitted scales with the threshold re-tuned to 6.8 %. Readout: the natural-source alignment (APL on / off / on minus off), the block, response rate ρ and the loss beside the untreated draws and the real wiring. Prediction: if the within-type draws with the convergence recover the real wiring's alignment and the plain draws do not, the two carriers are each necessary and jointly sufficient; if only part returns, another beyond-type structure carries the rest; if the plain draws recover it too, the convergence alone suffices given the gain; if neither, the convergence has to sit on the exact KCs. Proposed by hal on 2026-11-09 from E54's next steps; moved into the list under the idle-host rule.
58. **E58, the glomerulus gain as the carrier of a noise-robust block (mechanism: the glomerulus gain's magnitude, scored through the matched trial noise).** The block target through the noise (E48), the noise-robust block (E51) and the KC-KC-held fits (E53) leave one candidate for a block that survives the trial noise without the KC-KC cost: a drive large enough that the block's carriers sit several σ above threshold. The one axis of the fitted models that already moves the block through the noise that way is the glomerulus gain: at the data's operating point the eight-glomerulus gain at 1.5 carries +0.025 (40 Hz) and +0.016 (70 Hz) of block through independent noise where the gain at 1.25 carries +0.009 (E48; the data +0.055), with the APL-off statistics at the data's, and the deterministic fits show the gain and KC-KC inhibition substituting for each other (no gain: KC-KC 53 / 19; 1.25: 17 / 12; 1.5: 11 / 6), while the axis above 1.5 has never been run. Design: joint fits (the deterministic structure loss at the control flies' 6.8 %) with the eight-glomerulus gain at 1.75 and at 2 for the 40 Hz and the 70 Hz per-claw thresholds, each started exactly at the gain-1.5 optimum of the same threshold, each through the trial-noise pipeline with E48's settings; and E45's no-gain fits at both thresholds and its gain-1.25 fit at 40 Hz through the same pipeline, so each threshold has the whole gain axis (1, 1.25, 1.5, 1.75, 2) with the fits' own KC-KC scales beside it. Readout: beside E48's and E53's rows, the block through the noise (three-trial means and grand mean), the deterministic block, the loss and its terms, the response rates per trial with APL on and off, the deterministic response rate ρ with APL on and off, the gained odors' per-odor rates against the data, ρ on odor-odor distances through the noise, the natural-source alignment (APL on / off / on minus off), the floors, and the carriers' threshold margins. Prediction: if the block through the noise rises with the gain to +0.04 or more at 1.75–2 with the APL-off response rate within 0.01 of the data's and the loss within 0.2 of the gain-1.5 fit's, the gain carries a noise-robust block and the operating point moves to the larger gain; if it saturates at the gain-1.5 value while the loss rises, the gain's contribution is spent by 1.5 and a data-sized block through the noise needs a combination of carriers or a mechanism not in hand; if the fits at 1.75–2 cannot hold the operating point with the data's APL-off statistics, the gain is bounded at about 1.5 as KC-KC is by the APL-off per-odor spread; if the two thresholds disagree, the carrier depends on the per-claw threshold. Proposed by viki on 2026-11-09 from E51's and E53's next steps; moved into the list under the idle-host rule.
59. **E59, the co-input-preserving shuffle (wiring: the real wiring's full pairwise matrix of shared-KC counts matched on shuffled draws, with re-fits on convergence-bearing wirings).** The convergence transplant (E57) found the 32 over-shared pairs' shared-KC counts, put into within-type Curveball draws, to restore the APL-on side of the natural-source alignment and none of the APL-off side, although their removal from the real wiring (E54) loses part of it: necessary but not sufficient. Design: a simulated-annealing shuffle of same-type claw swaps (every KC's and every bouton's per-type claw count kept) that matches a draw's glomerulus x glomerulus shared-KC counts, all 1431 pairs, to the real wiring's while re-assigning which KCs hold them, reporting the residual and the fraction of claws that still differ from the real wiring; (A) on within-type Curveball draws (the type layer held), (B) on plain draws (the type layer left as drawn), three draws each, the three reference models at their fitted scales with the threshold re-tuned to 6.8 %; (C) re-fits of the reference model (structure loss, as E54's part D) on E57's first transplanted within-type draw and on A's first matched wiring. Readout: the natural-source alignment (APL on / off / on minus off), the block, response rate ρ and the loss beside the untreated draws, E57's transplanted draws and the real wiring; for the re-fits, the fits' rows beside the fixed-scale runs on the same wirings. Prediction: if A recovers the APL-off alignment and B does not, the carrier is the pairwise matrix's tail (the under-shared pairs and the weakly over-shared ones) on the type layer; if neither, it is higher-order (which KCs hold the pairs, which triples), and the next lesion goes there; if B recovers it too, the pairwise matrix alone suffices; if the re-fits recover the APL-off alignment or the block, the fits can convert the convergence and the fixed-scale results understate the wiring's contribution. Proposed by hal on 2026-11-09 from E57's next steps; moved into the list under the idle-host rule.
60. **E60, the batched proxy as the block term of a fit (the fitting criterion: whether the network-aware margin proxy, batched over trials and damped, is cheap enough to score a model's block through the noise inside a fit, and what a fit on that block chooses with KC–KC free).** The network-aware margin proxy (E56) reproduces the trial-noise pipeline from one deterministic run in five passes but at twenty-five times a deterministic evaluation's cost in its per-trial form; the KC–KC-held fits (E53) had to hold the KC–KC scale by hand to keep a block through the noise. Design: (A) the replay batched over the thirty trials with a relaxation step, timed and checked against E56 on its eleven models; (B) one fit of the eight glomeruli at 1.25 at 70 Hz from its 6.8 % optimum whose block term is the proxy's block through the noise (σ and the threshold ratio fixed at the seed's matched values, every other term deterministic, KC–KC free), then through the pipeline; (C) the fit against the seed, the KC–KC-held fits and the noise-aware fit of the same base. Readout: the proxy's cost per pass and its agreement per pass; the fit's deterministic block, block through the noise (proxy and pipeline), APL-off response rate and pattern, rate ratio, ρ on odor-odor distances, natural-source alignment (three numbers), loss and parameters. Prediction: if the proxy costs seconds per pass and the fit finds a block through the noise at or above +0.03 with the APL-off rate within 0.01 of the data's, a noise-robust block is fittable and the program's block term moves to the proxy; if the fit lands where the held fits did or trades the APL-off pattern for the block, the trade-off is the family's and the weighting is a decision; if the fit stays at the seed or the proxy stays costly, the criterion stays the simulated trials. Proposed by atlas on 2026-11-09 from E56's next steps; moved into the list by atlas under the idle-host rule.
61. **E61, the per-pair decomposition of the gap in ρ on odor-odor distances (diagnosis for the main goal: which pairs and odors carry the reference model's disagreement with the two KC matrices, with APL on and with APL off, and whether the ORN input misses them too).** ρ on odor-odor distances is one number over 120 pairs; the mechanisms that moved it and the ones that did not cannot be told apart without knowing which pairs the model mis-orders. Design, with no simulations: from the saved pair distances of the three reference models, the first joint fit (the best APL-on ρ), the best APL-off rows and the bouton baseline, against the data's two matrices: per pair the rank residual and the contribution to one minus ρ, as 16 × 16 matrices and ordered lists; per odor the leave-one-odor-out ρ and the mean absolute rank residual, both conditions, with the same for the ORN input's own disagreement, so that pairs the model and the input both miss (an input problem) are separated from pairs only the model misses (a mechanism problem); the model's pair-by-pair APL effect against the data's; and whether the contrasting models miss the same pairs. Prediction: if the gap concentrates on the weak-input odors' pairs and the input misses them too, the input is the lever; if on the ester or block pairs that the input gets right, the APL mechanism or the per-claw transfer; if it is spread over all odors, a global property that the per-odor rate oracle tests; if the APL-on and APL-off gaps sit on different pairs, two fixes are needed. Proposed by hal on 2026-11-09 as the second main-goal proposal; moved into the list by atlas under the idle-host rule.
62. **E62, the per-odor rate oracle (input: each odor's ORN input scaled so that the model's per-odor response fractions equal the KC data's, with the agreement of the odor-odor distances as the free readout).** The reference model ranks the odors' response rates at 0.47 with APL on against a ceiling of 0.98, and an odor's response rate shapes every distance it is in, so a wrong recruitment pattern caps ρ on odor-odor distances however right the pairwise structure is. Design: the three reference models at their fitted scales and thresholds on the real wiring; (A) each odor's ORN input vector multiplied by a scale, the 16 scales bisected together until each odor's APL-on response fraction equals the control flies', then APL off at the same scales; (B) the same with the scales set on the APL-off fractions against APL output blocked (TNT), APL on read out; (C) the scales themselves as a readout against the oracle gain's implied per-odor drive and Hallem's receptor data. Readout: ρ on odor-odor distances APL on / off beside the untuned models and the ORN input's own agreement (0.65 / 0.74), response rate ρ, the block, the natural-source alignment, structure vs wild-type somata. Prediction: if ρ rises to the 0.8s for both conditions the gap is mostly the recruitment pattern and the work goes to the input side; if it barely moves the gap is pairwise structure the mechanism does not produce; if one condition rises and not the other, the APL-state-specific structure is the problem. Proposed by hal on 2026-11-09 under Tom's main goal; moved into the list under the idle-host rule.
63. **E63, the two-ρ fit (the fitting criterion: ρ on odor-odor distances with the two KC matrices first, the response rates as constraints).** Thirty experiments moved the model's agreement with the two KC matrices (ρ on odor-odor distances, APL on against the control flies and APL off against APL output blocked (TNT); ceilings 0.98 / 0.97) by at most a fifth of their gap, and the mechanisms that bought the block and the alignment pushed the APL-off agreement below the ORN input's own (0.74); the main goal set on 2026-11-09 makes these two statistics the first thing every experiment is read against. Design: joint fits whose loss puts the two matrix terms first (weight 4 each) with the APL-on response rate, the APL-off / APL-on rate ratio and the per-odor response-rate terms kept as constraints at their usual weights and the block, the monotone relation and the structure statistics reported but not optimized, from the reference set at the 40 and 70 Hz per-claw thresholds (E44's optima), the no-gain base at 40 Hz (E45's fit) and the gain-1.25 base at 40 Hz (E45's), 150 evaluations each, each then scored through the trial-noise pipeline with E48's settings. Readout: the two ρ's beside the operating-point fits' and the ORN input's, the per-odor response rates, the block deterministic and through the noise, the structure statistics and the natural-source alignment (APL on / off / on minus off), and the fitted parameters. Prediction: if the two ρ's rise clearly above the operating-point fits' 0.65-0.68 / 0.63-0.68, the present mechanism set can reach further on the main goal than the structure loss let it and the cost (which statistics the fit gave up) names the trade-off; if they stay within the fits' spread, the gap is in the input (the weak-input odors' pairs, E61) or in a mechanism not in hand; if the no-gain base reaches as far as the gained ones, the gain's contribution to the two ρ's is replaceable. Proposed by hal on 2026-11-09 as the third main-goal proposal; moved into the list by viki under the idle-host rule.
64. **E64, the weak-input odors' ORN input as a bounded oracle (input: each weak odor's pattern across glomeruli searched within bounds to maximize the agreement of the odor-odor distances with the KC matrices).** The gap decomposition (E61) put 84 to 87 % of the gap in ρ on odor-odor distances on the weak-input odors' pairs, paa, B-myr and 2-but above all, which the models collapse onto the same KCs while the flies hold them apart, and the ORN input mis-orders the same pairs by about half as much; the per-odor rate oracle (E62) found the odors' recruitment level moves the agreement by at most a few hundredths, so the question is the odors' pattern across glomeruli, not their magnitude. Design: on the reference model (the eight at 1.5, 40 Hz) at its fitted scales, each of paa, B-myr, 2-but, euc, -aPine, ms and 1-8ol in turn (two passes) has its per-glomerulus ORN response multiplied by a factor vector bounded in [0.5, 2], candidates stacked sixteen at a time as pseudo-odors in one simulation of both conditions, each candidate's KC response replacing the odor's row in the other odors' fixed distance matrices; the objective is the sum of the two ρ's on odor-odor distances with a penalty on the odor's response fraction leaving ±15 % of the data's; a (1+15) evolution strategy, 40 generations per odor and pass; two strong odors (2h, 6al) as controls for what a free input buys an odor the model gets right. Readout: the full scoring of the searched input beside the untuned model and the rate oracle; per odor the gain in the two ρ's, the factor vector against Hallem's receptor data, and the odor's distances before and after against the data's. Prediction: if a bounded input lifts the two ρ's by several hundredths for the weak odors and little for the controls, the gap is the weak odors' input and the glomeruli the search moves are what to measure at Yang's concentrations; if it lifts them as much for the controls, a free input buys agreement for any odor and the result bounds the input's share without locating it; if it lifts little, the weak odors' input is not the limit and the mechanism carries the rest. Proposed by hal on 2026-11-09 under Tom's main goal; moved into the list under the idle-host rule.
65. **E65, the compressive readout of the model's spike counts (diagnosis for the main target: how much of the APL-effect loss is the comparison of raw spike counts with a saturating calcium signal; no simulations).** The main target set on 2026-11-09 is the per-pair APL effect on odor-odor distances (each pair's distance with APL on minus its distance with APL off), scored by the APL-effect loss (the root mean squared difference between the model's and the data's effect over the 120 pairs; lower is better, the data's own floor about 0.04), and about half of every model's loss is its mean part: the reference model's mean APL effect is +0.089 against the data's +0.178, at a loss of 0.130. The model's side is a cosine distance on raw spike counts, which a few dozen high-firing KCs set (in the reference model the top 1 % of KCs carry 67 % of each odor's squared response vector with APL on and single KCs fire up to 78 spikes; in the control flies the top 1 % carry 33 %), while the data are z-scored nuclear GCaMP6s fluorescence, which compresses large responses, and the readout-window experiment (E6) tested a linear kernel in time, never the amplitude. A read-only probe on archived spike counts found that under a square-root, a saturating or a binarized readout the reference model's mean APL effect rises to between +0.19 and +0.24 and its loss falls to 0.075-0.094 (better; floor about 0.04). Design, with no simulations and no model changed: (A) the data's amplitude statistics per recording and genotype (the amplitude distribution among responders, the share of each odor's squared response vector in the top 1 % and 5 % of KCs, the participation ratio, the number of odors each KC responds to), with one question for Yang on how the z-score is taken; (B) readouts applied to the archived spike counts of every variant in both conditions: raw counts (the target as defined), binarized at two or more spikes (against the data binarized and graded), one-parameter saturating and power-law readouts over a range of the parameter, and [calibrated readouts](#footnotes) whose parameter is fixed without reference to the APL effect (chosen per model so that the APL-on participation ratio, or the amplitude spread among responders, equals the control flies', or a rank-matching map onto the control flies' amplitude distribution) and applied unchanged to APL off, so that the APL effect is a prediction; (C) per readout, for the summary's best models and every variant: the APL-effect loss with its mean and pattern parts, the mean distances with APL on and off, the two ρ's on odor-odor distances, the block, the natural-source alignment (APL on / off / on minus off), the agreement of the variants' ordering by loss between readouts, the loss against the readout parameter, the number of odors per KC against the data's, and the loss through the saved trial-noise rows. A response stays two or more spikes under every readout, so the response rates and the realism bands are untouched, and the experiment does not change the definition of the target. Prediction: if the calibrated readouts agree with each other, bring the mean APL effect within about 0.03-0.05 of the data's and the loss to 0.06-0.09 and reorder the models, the mean deficit was the readout, which readout defines the model's side of the target is Tom's decision (both shown until then), and the later fits carry the calibrated readout and aim at the pattern part; if the loss depends strongly on the readout parameter within what the calibration allows, the target cannot be scored without the spike-to-fluorescence relation, which becomes a data item, and fits run under two bracketing readouts; if the calibrated readouts compress far less than the probe's and the mean deficit stays above 0.05, the deficit is the mechanism's, raw counts stand and the fit and wiring experiments go ahead as written. Proposed by atlas on 2026-11-09 from the main target set that day (the per-pair APL effect) and a read-only probe on archived spike counts; moved into the list by viki under the idle-host rule.
66. **E66, the per-odor profile of the APL effect (diagnosis and oracle for the main target: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss split into a per-odor and a pair-specific part, and each odor's APL strength set so that its response fraction is the data's in both conditions).** The main target is the per-pair APL effect on odor-odor distances (each pair's distance with APL on minus its distance with APL off), scored by the APL-effect loss (lower is better; the data's own floor about 0.04). Under a compressive readout of the model's spike counts (E65) most of the loss's mean part goes and what is left is the pattern across pairs: the reference model's pattern part is 0.095 under raw counts and 0.072 under a square-root readout. In the KC data 65 % of the variance of the per-pair effect is additive over odors (a pair's effect is the sum of its two odors' shares, which follow each odor's gain in response fraction when APL output is blocked, r = 0.78) and the remainder is at the floor; the reference model's pattern part splits into a per-odor part of 0.046 and a pair-specific part of 0.083 (0.043 and 0.057 under the square root), its shares correlate with the data's at 0.43, and the per-odor rate oracle (E62) found the model's APL too strong on B-myr and paa and too weak on the broad esters. Design: (A) with no simulations, the loss of the best models and of every archived variant split into its mean, per-odor and pair-specific parts under raw counts and under E65's readouts, the per-odor shares against the data's (with intervals from resampling recordings) and against each odor's response fractions, the pairs and KCs behind the pair-specific part, and the two archived oracles read the same way (the rate oracle's two arms singly and combined across conditions, the bounded-input oracle's (E64) searched input); (B) the oracle, at fixed scales on the three reference models, the first joint fit's base and the reference model with E64's searched input: each odor's input scale set on its APL-off response fraction, then a per-odor multiplier on the APL→KC and APL→bouton scales set on its APL-on fraction (odors are simulated independently, so a grid of global multipliers gives every odor its own), with the multiplier alone as a second arm; (C) the mechanism arm, the breadth exponent at 0, 1, 2 and 3 with the APL→KC scale re-tuned to hold the APL-on response rate, under both definitions of a KC's breadth. Readout: the loss and its three parts under each readout, the per-odor shares, ρ on the per-pair APL effect, the two ρ's on odor-odor distances, the block, the natural-source alignment (APL on / off / on minus off), the response rates against the realism bands, and the fitted multipliers against odor properties (total PN drive, the number of glomeruli above the per-claw threshold, the share of the odor's APL-off spikes in high-firing KCs, the share of its drive through the gained glomeruli). Prediction: if with both conditions' per-odor response rates right the per-odor part falls below about 0.02 and ρ on the per-pair APL effect rises above 0.5, the per-odor profile is the per-odor APL action and the multipliers name the rule an APL mechanism has to follow; if the per-odor part stays, the profile is set by which KCs an odor recruits (the collapse of the weak odors onto the same KCs) and the input pattern and the gain set carry it; if the per-odor part falls but the pattern part barely does, the pair-specific scatter is the target. A preview without simulations, from the rate oracle's archived arms combined across conditions, points to the second under raw counts (per-odor part 0.046 to 0.039, pattern part unchanged at 0.095) and only part of the way to the first under a square-root readout (per-odor part 0.043 to 0.025, pattern part 0.072 to 0.068). Proposed by atlas on 2026-11-09 under the APL-effect main target; moved into the list by hal under the idle-host rule.
67. **E67, the APL-effect fits (the fitting criterion: the per-pair APL effect first, on raw spike counts and under the calibrated readout, with the response rates as walled constraints).** The main target is the per-pair APL effect on odor-odor distances (each pair's distance with APL on minus its distance with APL off), scored by the APL-effect loss (lower is better; the data's own floor about 0.04), and no fit has optimized it: every fit so far led with the two matrices, the block, the structure statistics or the two ρ's on odor-odor distances. The compressive readout of the model's spike counts (E65) showed that the definition of the model's side decides about half of the loss: on raw spike counts the reference model scores 0.130 with a mean APL effect of +0.089 against the data's +0.178, and under a saturating readout calibrated on the model's APL-on counts to the control flies' spread it scores 0.075 with the mean at +0.199, the pattern across pairs being what is left (0.073); it also showed that the glomerulus-gain models' fruity x small-ester block does not survive the readout (0.054 to 0.011; the data's 0.047-0.072 under every reading of the data) and that the models leave more KCs silent with APL off than the flies do (48 % against 33 %). Which definition is the target is Tom's decision, so the fits run under both. Design: a loss in the joint-fit program whose leading term is the APL-effect loss divided by its floor (weight 2), computed either on raw counts or under the calibrated readout (its parameter re-set at every evaluation on that evaluation's APL-on counts and applied unchanged to APL off), with the APL-on response rate at the control flies' 6.8 %, the APL-off / APL-on response-rate ratio (a steep wall outside the data's 90 % interval, 1.9 to 2.5) and the per-odor response-rate terms (a wall where the response rate ρ with APL on falls below 0.37) as constraints, the two matrix terms held at weight 1 and the block, the monotone relation and the structure statistics reported but not optimized; fits of 150 evaluations over the somatic threshold, the APL→KC and APL→bouton scales (the latter capped at three times the base's) and the KC–KC scale, from the reference set at the 40 Hz per-claw threshold (E44's optimum), the first joint fit's family without gain or per-claw threshold at breadth exponents 3 and 2 (E10's optima), the 70 Hz base with the gain on DM1 alone and the no-gain 40 Hz base (the two-ρ fit's (E63) third fit), under each definition; then, from the best basins, a fit on the pattern part alone, a fit with both definitions in the loss, a fit that also asks for the block under the same readout, a fit constrained to the data's number of odors per KC with APL off, and the best fit of each definition through the trial-noise pipeline. Readout: for every fit beside its start, under both definitions whichever was in the loss: the APL-effect loss with its mean, per-odor and pair-specific parts (E66's decomposition), ρ on the per-pair APL effect, the block under each readout, the mean distances, the response rates and their ratio against the realism bands, the number of odors per KC, the two ρ's on odor-odor distances, the natural-source alignment (APL on / off / on minus off) and the fitted parameters. Prediction: if some start reaches 0.08 or below on raw counts, or 0.05 or below under the readout, inside the realism bands, the loss weighting was the limit and that fit becomes the reference for the target; if every start ends near 0.085 on raw counts and at 0.055-0.065 under the readout with the response rates at their walls or the pattern part unmoved (expected: the lowest pattern part of any archived model under the readout is 0.050, and the breadth weight that buys the mean costs the pattern), the present mechanism set is the limit, the reachable numbers become what every later mechanism is judged against, and the larger remaining part of the pattern names the next mechanism; if the two definitions send the same start to different regimes, the definition selects the model and the both-definitions fit says whether one model can serve both; if the data's number of odors per KC with APL off can be had only by giving up the mean APL effect, the readout alone does not settle the mean part. Proposed by atlas on 2026-11-09 under the APL-effect main target; rewritten after E65's result and moved into the list by viki under the idle-host rule.
68. **E68, the low end of the readout (diagnosis for the main target: whether the calibrated readout is like-for-like with the KC data below the response criterion, through measurement noise, the weight of one-spike entries and, in a second part, a sub-threshold signal from the membrane potential; part A has no simulations).** The main target is the per-pair APL effect on odor-odor distances (each pair's distance with APL on minus its distance with APL off), scored by the APL-effect loss (lower is better; the data's own floor 0.039). Since the compressive readout of the model's spike counts (E65) the model's side is also read through a [saturating readout calibrated on the control flies](#footnotes), under which the reference model (the glomerulus gain of 1.5 on eight glomeruli, 40 Hz per-claw threshold, tuned at the control flies' 6.8 %) scores 0.075 with a mean APL effect of +0.199 against the data's +0.178. At its low end that readout is unlike the data: a KC without a spike reads exactly 0 and one spike reads 0.7 z (two spikes 1.3 z, so the data's response criterion, a trial mean above 1, sits between one and two spikes), while in the control flies 24 % of each odor's squared response vector is in trial means at or below the criterion, 13 % in negative ones (14 % and 5 % with APL output blocked (TNT)). A read-only probe on the per-trial recordings, the archived spike counts and the archived jittered trials of the trial-noise pipeline found three things. Measurement noise of the data's size (0.4 z per trial) leaves the reference model's loss within 0.01 of 0.075 (0.069 to 0.083) and puts its mean APL effect at +0.20 to +0.22 (slightly further from the data's +0.178). The weight given to one-spike entries moves nothing for the reference model but moves the pattern part of the loss between 0.055 and 0.072 around the first joint fit's 0.058, and between 0.058 and 0.099 around 0.065 for the joint fit with breadth exponent 3 (one-spike entries hold 20 to 21 % of these models' squared readout vector with APL on, against 7 % of the reference model's), more than the APL-effect fits (E67) had moved any loss under the readout (0.004). And the data's mean APL effect depends on how the sub-threshold values are read (+0.133 with them set to 0, +0.178 graded, +0.212 corrected for trial-to-trial noise) while the model's does not: +0.065 of the data's +0.178 comes from products of a responding with a sub-threshold entry, a term four times larger with APL output blocked in the flies (0.023 to 0.088 of the mean cosine similarity) and no larger in the reference model read through the readout with the data's noise (0.063 to 0.054), whose APL-off recruits are strong (entries above 4 z: 2.9 % against the flies' 0.5 %) where the flies' are weak (entries between 0.5 and 1.5 z: 8.7 % in the control flies, 15.8 % with APL output blocked (TNT)). Design of part A, with no simulations and no model changed: (A1) the data's low end per recording and genotype from the per-trial values: the amplitude histogram and the trial-to-trial variability by amplitude; the reproducible and the noise share of the sub-threshold band, cross-validated over trials; every pair's cosine, and the per-pair APL effect with its per-odor and pair-specific parts, split into the terms between two responders, between a responder and a sub-threshold entry and between two sub-threshold entries, with intervals from resampling recordings; the negative values tested against each KC's response to the solvent and against the number of responders to the odor; and two readings of the data added to the four in use (negatives set to 0; values below a noise-defined floor set to 0); (A2) readouts with a low end applied to the archived spike counts of every variant (the key models, the APL-effect fits' starts and fits as they land, and the 531 variants without a realism flag): a floor b for the one-spike weight, (n + b) / (n + b + K), set so that the model's APL-on share of entries between 0.5 and 1 z equals the control flies'; measurement noise of the data's size, in closed form for the graded reading and sampled as pseudo-recordings of three trials for the others; and both, each model reading scored against the same reading of the data; (A3) the same through the archived jittered trials of the models that have them. Every low-end parameter is calibrated on control-fly statistics that involve neither the APL effect nor any odor-odor distance, jointly with the half-saturation K, and applied unchanged to APL off, so the APL-off amplitudes and the APL effect stay predictions; a response stays two or more spikes, so the response rates and the realism bands are untouched. Readout: per variant and reading, the loss with its mean, per-odor and pair-specific parts, the calibrated parameters, the amplitude histograms in both conditions beside the data's, the three terms, the mean distances, the block, the two ρ's on odor-odor distances and the natural-source alignment (APL on / off / on minus off). Prediction: if with K, the floor and the noise calibrated jointly every key model's loss stays within about 0.01 of its present calibrated value, the calibrated readout stands as defined, the tables gain the reading with sub-threshold values set to 0 on both sides (where the reference model's mean APL effect overshoots the data's by +0.06 to +0.07) and the choice between that reading and the graded one becomes part of Tom's decision on the target's definition; if the floor that the control flies' histogram fixes moves the joint-fit family's pattern part by 0.005 or more, the readout gains the floor as a second calibrated parameter, the APL-effect fits' rows are re-scored under it and later fits use it; in either case the negative values are placed, on the data's side if they are a property of the measurement (per KC, stimulus-general, alike in both genotypes; the reading with negatives set to 0 has a mean APL effect of +0.163) and in part B's hyperpolarization term if they depend on APL (22.4 % of the control flies' entries are below -0.25 z, 16.6 % with APL output blocked (TNT)). Part B, approved by Tom the same evening, adds the sub-threshold signal: fixed-scale simulations with the membrane potential saved, APL on and acute APL off, of the reference model at the 40 and 70 Hz per-claw thresholds, the first joint fit at breadth exponents 2 and 3 and the best APL-effect fit under each definition, read with a positive term for depolarization below the spike threshold and a negative term for hyperpolarization, both calibrated on the control flies, to test against a control with the potential permuted across the non-spiking KCs whether the model holds the flies' weak APL-off responses below its spike threshold or its APL-off recruitment is itself too strong. Proposed by hal on 2026-11-09 at Tom's question about the bottom end of the readout; part A approved by Tom the same evening and moved into the list by hal, part B approved shortly after.
69. **E69, the firing ceiling for the KCs (mechanism: a refractory period and spike-triggered adaptation in the model's KCs, the cell-level counterpart of the calibrated readout).** The main target is the per-pair APL effect on odor-odor distances (each pair's distance with APL on minus its distance with APL off), scored by the APL-effect loss (lower is better; the data's own floor 0.039). The model's KCs have no refractory period and no adaptation: in the reference model (the glomerulus gain of 1.5 on eight glomeruli, 40 Hz per-claw threshold, tuned at the control flies' 6.8 %) the median responder fires 3 to 4 spikes to the 0.5 s odor, but single KCs fire up to 78 spikes with APL on and 117 with APL off, and entries of 20 spikes or more carry 53 % and 84 % of each odor's squared response vector. The compressive readout of the model's spike counts (E65) showed that this tail is what holds the mean APL effect down on raw counts (+0.089 against the data's +0.178, at a loss of 0.130; 0.075 under the [calibrated readout](#footnotes)); the per-odor profile of the APL effect (E66) found that a handful of such KCs, shared by two odors through DM5, DM1 and DP1m, carry the largest pair-specific errors; the APL-effect fits (E67) found that the two definitions of the model's side select different models (the first joint fit's family at breadth exponent 2 under the readout, 0.056; the 70 Hz base with the gain on DM1 alone on raw counts, 0.078); and the low end of the readout (E68) found that with APL off every model adds strong responders where the flies add weak ones (entries above 4 z 2.6 % against the flies' 0.5 %; 48 % of KCs silent against 33 %), a gap in realism that does not depend on how the target is defined and that the odors-per-KC band also flags. Capping the archived spike counts after the fact at 20 and at 10 spikes takes the reference model's loss on raw counts to 0.090 and 0.078 and its mean APL effect to +0.146 and +0.194, and the calibrated readout's numbers are those of a cap of about ten spikes for the reference model and about five for the joint fits. Design: three parameters in the model that default to its present behavior, a refractory period and a per-KC adaptation variable that each spike raises and that decays with a time constant, added to the spike threshold; (A) at fixed scales on the reference model and on the best APL-effect fit under each definition, the 3 ms refractory period alone and three adaptation strengths at time constants of 50 ms, 200 ms and 1 s, with both response rates held at each base's by re-tuning the somatic threshold on the APL-off response rate and the APL scales on the APL-on one, so that no change is bought with the response rates; (B) re-fits under the APL-effect loss, on raw counts and under the calibrated readout, with the ceiling fixed at the weakest setting that brings the model's APL-off share of entries above 4 z to within twice the flies', and at the ceiling the whole-cell literature or Tom gives, the best fit under each definition then going through the trial-noise pipeline. Readout: the spike-count distribution and its spread against the flies', the APL-effect loss with its mean, per-odor and pair-specific parts under both definitions and binarized, the half-saturation the readout still needs, the APL-off amplitude statistics and the number of odors per KC against the realism bands, the response rates and their ratio, the block under each readout, the two ρ's on odor-odor distances and the natural-source alignment (APL on / off / on minus off). Prediction: if a ceiling within what the physiology allows brings the reference base to 0.09 or below on raw counts with the realism bands held, brings the two definitions within about 0.015 of each other and lets one fitted model serve both, the mean deficit under raw counts was the unbounded firing, the ceiling is proposed as part of the base model and the definition question shrinks to the indicator's share; if the strong APL-off tail goes but the weak responses and the number of odors per KC do not move, the ceiling sets how much the strong responders fire and not who responds, and the breadth of the APL-off recruitment needs a mechanism of its own; if the losses are reached only at a ceiling of a few spikes, the compression is the indicator's and the readout carries the mean part; if the response rates cannot be held or the loss under the readout rises, the conflict is reported with its numbers. Proposed by atlas on 2026-11-09 under the APL-effect main target; rewritten after the per-odor profile of the APL effect (E66), the first two stages of the APL-effect fits (E67), the low end of the readout (E68) and the odors-per-KC realism band, and moved into the list by hal under the idle-host rule.
70. **E70, the high-firing KCs that survive APL (diagnosis for the main target: which cells of the real wiring hold the mean APL effect down on raw spike counts, and how few cells a model's APL-effect loss rests on).** The main target is the per-pair APL effect on odor-odor distances (each pair's distance with APL on minus its distance with APL off), scored by the APL-effect loss (lower is better; the data's own floor 0.039). At the reference model's fitted scales (the glomerulus gain of 1.5 on eight glomeruli, 40 Hz per-claw threshold, tuned at the control flies' 6.8 %) shuffled wirings give a larger mean APL effect on raw counts than the real hemibrain wiring does (+0.12 to +0.22 against +0.089; the data's +0.178), and the difference is not the pairwise co-input of glomeruli (the lesions of the over-shared pairs, E52 and E54, and the draws matched on shared-KC counts, E59, leave it). It sits in a few cells: 244 KCs fire 20 or more spikes to some odor with APL off and carry 0.876 of each odor's squared response vector; 20 of them still reach 20 spikes with APL on, and with those 20 left out of both conditions the mean distance with APL on rises to the shuffled wirings' and the mean APL effect to +0.132, one KC alone accounting for +0.034. Since the proposal was written, the compressive readout of the model's spike counts (E65) showed that under the [calibrated readout](#footnotes) the same cells are what keeps the real wiring's mean effect at the data's (leaving them out raises the loss from 0.075 to 0.094); the per-odor profile of the APL effect (E66) found the largest pair-specific errors in a handful of KCs driven through DM5, DM1 and DP1m; the low end of the readout (E68) and the odors-per-KC realism band found the models' APL-off recruitment concentrated in too few, too strongly firing KCs; and the first fit of the batched proxy as the block term of a fit (E60) chose, like the fits with KC–KC inhibition held (E53), a regime that meets the odors-per-KC band with a mean APL effect of +0.034, a regime in which 38 to 128 KCs survive APL and carry about half to two thirds of the APL-on response vectors. Two further readings of the archive enter: the lowest raw-count loss of any archived model (the gain on DM1 alone, 0.088) goes to 0.119 when two of its 1732 KCs are left out, while the first joint fit's family is unmoved by leaving out fifty; and one control recording paired with one APL-blocked recording scores a loss of 0.111 against the mean of the other recordings (median of 88 pairs; pattern part 0.092), against the floor of 0.039 for the mean over recordings. Design: (A) with no simulations, for every archived variant the loss and its mean, per-odor and pair-specific parts with the highest-firing KCs, the survivors and the whole high-firing set left out, on raw counts and under the calibrated readout; the survivors' tuning and wiring on the real wiring against the shuffles and against high-firing KCs that do not survive (claws, glomeruli, the gained glomeruli, KC type, APL weight, KC–KC inhibition), and their share of the block and of the natural-source alignment; the classes against the number of odors per KC, for the fits with strong KC–KC inhibition as well; the survivors' share of the APL-on response against the mean APL effect across the archive; and the single-fly yardstick (single pairs of recordings against the mean of the others, with the model's single wirings and single pseudo-recordings beside them); (B) at fixed scales with the somatic threshold re-tuned to 6.8 %, on the 40 and 70 Hz reference models, three seeds per arm: the real wiring with the claws of the survivors re-drawn within KC type, with those of the whole high-firing set re-drawn, and with those of matched control KCs re-drawn; within-type claw-permutation draws with the real wiring's survivors' claws put back; and an oracle that multiplies the survivors' APL→KC weights by 2 and by 4; (C) if the best APL-effect fit (E67) under a definition also rests on its survivors, the same re-draws at that fit's scales. Readout: the APL-effect loss with its parts and ρ on the per-pair APL effect under both definitions, the mean distances with APL on and off, whether survivors re-form on the re-drawn wiring, the largest spike counts, the number of odors per KC and the response rates, their ratio and the response rate ρ against the realism bands, the block under each readout, the two ρ's on odor-odor distances and the natural-source alignment (APL on / off / on minus off). Prediction: if re-drawing the twenty survivors raises the mean APL effect on raw counts by 0.03 or more, the controls do not, and putting them back into a shuffled wiring lowers it, a small identified set of the real wiring's KCs holds the mean down; their wiring then names the cause (the gained glomeruli are expected), and whether they also carry the block and the natural-source alignment decides between a conflict to be weighted and a mechanism acting on those cells; if only the whole high-firing set does it, the property is distributed and a global compression (the readout or the firing ceiling, E69) is the route; if neither does, or survivors re-form on the re-drawn wiring, the inhibition at the fitted scales makes them and the cause is on the APL and KC–KC side. Under the calibrated readout the re-draw is expected to overshoot the data's mean and raise the loss, so whether these cells are a defect depends on the open definition of the target. Proposed by atlas on 2026-11-09 under the APL-effect main target; rewritten after the compressive readout of the model's spike counts (E65), the per-odor profile of the APL effect (E66), the first two stages of the APL-effect fits (E67), the low end of the readout (E68), the odors-per-KC realism band and the first fit of the batched proxy as the block term of a fit (E60), and moved into the list by atlas under the idle-host rule.
71. **E71, the input-scaled KC threshold (mechanism: a somatic threshold that scales with each KC's summed input, a property of the cell in both conditions; which KCs are released when APL is blocked).** The main target is the per-pair APL effect on odor-odor distances (each pair's distance with APL on minus its distance with APL off), scored by the APL-effect loss (lower is better; the data's own floor 0.039). The best fit so far, the first joint fit's family at breadth exponent 2 fitted under that loss at the control flies' 6.8 % (E67), scores 0.093 on raw spike counts and 0.056 under the [calibrated readout](#footnotes) with a mean APL effect of +0.177 against the data's +0.178, but it reaches that mean through the wrong cells. When APL is blocked, the 14 % of its KCs with the most claws take 34 % of the added responses and the third with the fewest claws 9 %; 47 % of its KCs stay silent and 10 % respond to eight or more odors (flies with APL output blocked (TNT): 33 % and 7 %); its binarized mean distance with APL off is 0.605 against the data's 0.711, and its binarized mean APL effect +0.207 against +0.144. The firing ceiling (E69) changes how much the released cells fire and not which cells they are; KC–KC inhibition strong enough to spread the release costs the effect (E53, E60); and the earlier test of thresholds by claw count (E5) turned out not to have applied them. Design: each KC's threshold is multiplied by a power a of its summed claw weight relative to the mean, the same with APL on and off; (A) at fixed scales, a from -0.5 to 1 on that fit, on the same with the firing ceiling and on the reference model, both response rates held by re-tuning the somatic threshold on the APL-off response rate and the APL scales on the APL-on one, with the breadth that the APL weights are built from recomputed (and, as a control, kept); (B) re-fits under the APL-effect loss with both definitions in one loss at the exponent that brings the number of odors per KC with APL off closest to the flies', a choice that involves no distance between odors; (C) the fit and its base through the trial-noise pipeline, with single pseudo-recordings scored as single pairs of recordings were. Readout: the APL-effect loss with its mean, per-odor and pair-specific parts on raw counts, under the calibrated readout and binarized; the mean distances in both conditions; who is released by claw count; the number of odors per KC and the response amplitudes with APL off against the flies'; the high-firing cells; the response rates, their ratio and the response rate ρ against the realism bands; the block under each readout, the two ρ's on odor-odor distances and the natural-source alignment (APL on / off / on minus off). Prediction: at fixed scales the binarized mean APL effect comes down toward the data's and the binarized loss falls, while the mean under the calibrated readout falls by 0.01 to 0.03 unless the weak responses of a spread release carry part of the effect as they do in the flies. If the re-fit ends within 0.005 of the best fit with the recruitment at the flies', the threshold joins the base model beside the ceiling. If the recruitment is fixed and the effect cannot be fitted back, the present family's APL effect is the release of its many-claw KCs, and the effect has to be made through weak responses spread over many KCs. If no exponent moves the number of odors per KC, the concentration is not the amount of a KC's input but which glomeruli give it. Proposed by atlas on 2026-11-10 from the results of the high-firing KCs that survive APL (E70), the APL-effect fits (E67) and the first part of the firing ceiling experiment (E69), and moved into the list by atlas under the idle-host rule.
72. **E72, the APL-effect fit on re-drawn wirings (robustness and the like-for-like comparison: the best fit of the main target on other wirings, singly and as a population of flies, and at low concentrations).** The main target is the per-pair APL effect on odor-odor distances (each pair's distance with APL on minus its distance with APL off), scored by the APL-effect loss (lower is better; the data's own floor 0.039). The best fit so far, the first joint fit's family at breadth exponent 2 fitted under that loss at the control flies' 6.8 % (E67; no glomerulus gain, no per-claw threshold), scores 0.056 under the [calibrated readout](#footnotes) and 0.093 on raw spike counts, with the pattern across pairs as what remains. That number is one wiring's: the model has the hemibrain wiring in both conditions, whereas the data's effect is the mean over 11 control recordings minus the mean over 8 recordings with APL output blocked (TNT), from different flies, so what is particular to one fly's wiring is averaged out of the data and not out of the model. In the archive, different model families on the real wiring share their per-pair errors about as much as one family shares them across wirings, the pattern part of one fitted family ranges from 0.079 to 0.119 on raw counts across eight wirings, and the mean over those eight has 0.077 against 0.097 for the average single wiring. Design: (A) with no simulations, every archived family with fits on several wirings, single wirings against their mean; (B) the best fit re-fitted under the APL-effect loss, both definitions in one loss, on four within-type claw permutations and on the three wirings that keep the real wiring's shared-KC counts for every glomerulus pair on other cells (E59), each scored alone and as the mean over wirings of the pair distances per condition, with the per-pair error split into the part common to all wirings and each wiring's own; (C) the no-gain 40 Hz per-claw-threshold base on the same permutations, as paired differences; (D) one parameter set fitted to four wirings and tested on four held out; (E) the population through the trial-noise pipeline; (F) the fit at fixed parameters on Yang's low-concentration odors, the test every per-claw-threshold model failed (E49). Readout: the APL-effect loss with its mean, per-odor and pair-specific parts under raw counts, the calibrated readout and binarized, per wiring and for the population; the spread between draws; the real wiring's rank; the pairs wrong on every wiring; the response rates, their ratio, the response rate ρ and the number of odors per KC against the realism bands; the block, the two ρ's on odor-odor distances and the natural-source alignment (APL on / off / on minus off); the low-concentration response fractions against the flies'. Prediction: single wirings land between 0.050 and 0.070 under the readout with the real wiring inside the range, a spread two to three times the margins by which recent fits were chosen; the mean over eight wirings lowers the pattern part by 0.012 to 0.018 on raw counts and by up to 0.010 under the readout. If so, the fit's standing is the family's, part of the remaining gap is one wiring's draw, and what is left for mechanisms is the error common to all wirings. If averaging gains nothing, the error is the input's and the mechanism's. If the real wiring is better than every draw, the hemibrain wiring carries part of the match, and the matched wirings say whether its pairwise co-input structure does. If the per-claw threshold's cost changes sign across draws, the conclusion that its absence sets the best family apart is withdrawn. Proposed by viki on 2026-11-10 from the APL-effect fits' (E67) next steps and read-only probes of the archived fits on re-drawn wirings, and moved into the list by viki under the idle-host rule.
73. **E73, the per-KC normalization of the recordings (the comparison's definition: the recordings' cell-by-cell z-score applied to the model, and the fits repeated under it).** The main target is the per-pair APL effect on odor-odor distances (each pair's distance with APL on minus its distance with APL off), scored by the APL-effect loss (lower is better; the data's own floor 0.039). The model's side has been read as raw spike counts or through the [calibrated readout](#footnotes), one saturating map for all KCs, and a model KC has counted as responding at two or more spikes. The recordings look different: each KC's values appear to be z-scored over that cell's whole recording. The mean of a KC's squared responses over its 51 trials has a sharp upper edge in every one of the 19 recordings (5.8 at most with APL output blocked (TNT), 5.0 in control flies, where a baseline-referenced z-score would leave a long tail), the largest trial mean is 7.3 to 7.4 in both genotypes, and a responder's mean amplitude is about 2 z whether its KC responds to one odor or to eleven. If so, amplitudes and the response criterion (above 1 z) are relative to each cell: a KC that responds strongly to many odors reads each of them lower, the equal amplitude of the responders with and without APL (2.00 and 1.99 z) is at least partly the normalization, and the response rates, their ratio and the number of odors per KC are measured with a criterion the model's is not like for like with. Design: (A) with no simulations, the edge tested per recording of the APL16 and the low-concentration panels against three candidate normalizations (over the whole recording, per trial, against a pre-odor baseline), with the share of the recording inside the response windows estimated two ways; (B) with no simulations, every archived variant read the same way (a session of the 16 odors and the solvent assembled from each model KC's spike counts, z-scored per KC, calibrated on the model's APL-on counts to the control flies' response rate and responders' amplitude and applied unchanged to APL off), scored against the same reading of the data graded, with sub-threshold values set to 0 and binarized, with the realism bands recomputed under the data's criterion, and the low-concentration predictions read within their own sessions; (C) the best family re-fitted with the APL-effect loss and the response rates computed through that reading, without and with the firing ceiling, and the better fit through the trial-noise pipeline. Readout: the APL-effect loss with its mean, per-odor and pair-specific parts under each reading; the response rates, their ratio, the response rate ρ and the odors-per-KC distributions under both criteria; the responders' amplitudes and the share of strong entries in both conditions; the order of the models under the reading against the present order; the firing ceiling's gain, the wiring population's gain and the input-scaled threshold's cost re-read. Prediction, from a crude emulation on the archived spike counts: the best fits' mean APL effect falls from +0.15 to +0.18 to +0.05 to +0.12 (the data's +0.178), so their loss rises from 0.052 to 0.061 to 0.07 to 0.14, while the pattern across pairs does not move (0.050 to 0.052) and the binarized loss falls from 0.08 to 0.10 to 0.05 to 0.07; the model's response rate with APL off as the data would measure it is lower than the one its fit was held to. If the re-fits recover the mean inside the realism bands, the family stands at a different operating point; if not, the mean part of the target is open again once the cells are weighted as the recordings weight them. If the reading changes little, the calibrated readout and the firing ceiling were adequate stand-ins and the reading becomes the single definition. If the recordings are not normalized per cell, the present readings stand. Proposed by hal on 2026-11-10 from the caveat the compressive readout (E65) and the low end of the readout (E68) left open, the firing ceiling's (E69) amplitude result and read-only probes of the per-trial recordings and the archived spike counts, and moved into the list by hal under the idle-host rule.
74. **E74, the APL effect split by odors per KC (analysis of which KCs carry the effect, in the recordings and in the models; fits asked for the flies' split).** The main target is the per-pair APL effect on odor-odor distances (each pair's distance with APL on minus its distance with APL off), scored by the APL-effect loss (lower is better; the data's own floor 0.039).
