After the re-fits under the per-KC normalization of the recordings (E73) the best family reads 0.056 with and without the firing ceiling, with a mean APL effect of +0.159 to +0.170 against the data's +0.178, so what is left under every definition is the pattern across pairs (0.050 to 0.056). The input-scaled KC threshold (E71) ended on the reading that the flies make their effect through weak responses and the models through KCs that turn into broad responders. A first look at the recordings says otherwise. When each pair's similarity is divided among the KCs by the number of the 16 odors each responds to, the flies' KCs that respond to eight or more odors with APL output blocked (TNT), 7 % of them, carry +0.142 of the +0.178 and those responding to four to seven carry +0.053 and a third of the pattern; the part of the effect below the response criterion is these broad KCs' weaker responses to their other odors. In the best fits the KCs at eight or more odors carry more than the whole effect (+0.26 to +0.29 under the [calibrated readout](#footnotes)), every other class takes away from it, and half or more of the pattern error is in the class at four to seven odors. Design: (A) with no simulations, the split for the recordings with recording-bootstrap intervals and for every archived variant, under the calibrated readout and under the per-KC reading with the data's measurement noise, and a class-resolved loss with its floor; (B) the firing-ceiling family with the input-scaled threshold at a power of 0.25 re-fitted under the per-KC reading, beside the fit of the same family without the scaling; (C) if the split holds up, fits of the ceiling family, without and with the scaling, with a class-resolved APL-effect term in the loss. Readout: the per-class effects and each class's share of the pattern error; the APL-effect loss with its mean, per-odor and pair-specific parts under each definition; the response rates, their ratio, the response rate ρ and the odors-per-KC distributions under both criteria; who is released when APL is blocked. Prediction: the split holds with the measurement noise in; no archived mechanism raises the middle class's contribution to the flies'; the scaling under the reading costs less than a wiring draw (about 0.006); a fit asked for the flies' split lowers the pattern part by 0.003 to 0.008. If it reaches the split with the realism bands held, the family can make the effect the way the flies do and the fit goes to re-drawn wirings. If the split can be had only at a cost on the total, the remainder of the pattern part needs a mechanism that gives moderately broad KCs graded responses to their weaker odors. Proposed by atlas on 2026-11-10 from the input-scaled KC threshold's (E71) next steps, the first re-fits under the per-KC normalization of the recordings (E73) and read-only probes of the per-trial recordings and the archived spike counts, and moved into the list by atlas under the idle-host rule.
75. **E75, the glomerulus profile of APL inhibition (oracle with an anatomical reading: glomerulus-specific APL weights at the cell, the claw and the bouton, found from the model and tested across wirings).** The main target is the per-pair APL effect on odor-odor distances (each pair's distance with APL on minus its distance with APL off), scored by the APL-effect loss (lower is better; the data's own floor 0.039). In the best family (the first joint fit's family at breadth exponent 2 fitted under that loss, no glomerulus gain, no per-claw threshold; 0.056 under the [calibrated readout](#footnotes) on the real wiring, 0.048 for the mean over eight wirings) the mean APL effect is the data's and what is left is the pattern across pairs, and the re-drawn wirings (E72) showed that this remainder is common to every wiring: the model's effect is too small by 0.069 on the 15 pairs among 2h, IaA, pa, eb, ep and ms and too large by 0.069 on the 6 pairs among 1-8ol, 1-5ol, 1-6ol and 6al, which together hold 41 % of the common error's squared sum. The flies' APL separates the first group (distance 0.549 with APL output blocked (TNT), 0.789 in control flies) and leaves the second close (0.482 and 0.608); the model gives both the same effect. How many KCs APL removes per odor is not what sets this (E66); which KCs two odors still share with APL on is, and since no draw of the wiring removes the error, the only wiring-independent way to say which KCs is by the glomeruli they sample. In the archive, APL aimed at the ester co-responders or at the claws of the glomeruli the esters share (E14, E15) moved both groups the right way and raised ρ on the per-pair APL effect by 0.3 to 0.5 on the old bases, where breadth weighting, panel-general glomerulus scores and permuted scores did not; none of this was run on the best family or under the main target. Design: (A) the firing-ceiling family's fits on four wirings through the trial-noise pipeline, as a population (the missing row of E72); (B) with no simulations, every archived variant's per-pair error against the common error; (C) on the best fit, a map of how the per-pair APL effect changes when APL's weight on one glomerulus is doubled, for each of the 36 driven glomeruli at three sites (the whole cell by its claws' glomeruli, the claw, the bouton), with the APL-on response rate held; (D) a per-glomerulus profile built from the map to cancel the common error, bounded at a factor of 4, cross-validated by leaving out one odor at a time and on split halves of the recordings, with a gate; then simulated, re-fitted on the real wiring and three draws as paired differences and populations, run on four held-out wirings without re-fitting, compared with a permuted profile, and read at Yang's low concentrations; (E) if it holds, the same on the firing-ceiling family and through the trial noise; (F) the profile against the hemibrain's and the male CNS's APL synapse densities per glomerulus and against each glomerulus's drive. Readout: the APL-effect loss with its mean and pattern parts and ρ on the per-pair APL effect under raw counts, the calibrated readout, binarized and the per-KC reading, per wiring and for the populations; the two groups' errors; the response rates, their ratio, the response rate ρ and the odors-per-KC distributions against the realism bands; the split of the effect by odors per KC; who is released when APL is blocked. Prediction: a profile exists that lowers the pattern part by 0.002 to 0.008 and raises ρ on the per-pair APL effect from 0.56 to between 0.62 and 0.72 (ceiling 0.83); crude emulations on the best fit's spike counts remove the two groups' errors but lower the pattern part by 0.004 at most, so the gain may be small. If the profile transfers to wirings it never saw, the remaining error is one of where APL acts, and the anatomy says whether connectome-averaged APL weights should replace the hemibrain's or whether it is a prediction for imaging APL in the calyx. If it holds on the real wiring only, a static weight per glomerulus is not the carrier. If no profile exists, APL's targeting is closed as the source for this family and the error is in the geometry the two conditions share, on the input side. Proposed by viki on 2026-11-10 from the next steps of the APL-effect fit on re-drawn wirings (E72), the standing next step of the compressive readout (E65), the APL-effect fits (E67) and the firing ceiling (E69), the first part of the APL effect split by odors per KC (E74) and read-only probes of the archive, and moved into the list by viki under the idle-host rule.
76. **E76, the data side of the main target (the comparison's definition: the recordings' per-KC unit and the spread among the flies with APL output blocked, and fits to the effect in fixed units).** The main target is the per-pair APL effect on odor-odor distances (each pair's distance with APL on minus its distance with APL off), scored by the APL-effect loss (lower is better; the data's own floor 0.039). In the best family the mean APL effect matches the data's +0.178 under the readings calibrated to the recordings, and the pattern across pairs (0.050 to 0.056) is what is left. Both statements rest on two things taken as given on the data's side. The first is the unit: the recordings are z-scored per KC, which scales down the KCs that respond to many odors. With each KC's responses in units of its baseline noise, the unit the realism bands now use, the flies' mean effect is +0.242, with the raw change in fluorescence +0.241, and over the KC's mean fluorescence +0.205, while the pattern across pairs is the same (ρ 0.95 to 0.98 with the z-based effect). Against the baseline-noise effect the best family reads 0.091 on raw spike counts in place of 0.052, all of the difference in the mean part, and the reference set with the firing ceiling moves ahead of it (0.079). The second is which recordings make up the mean with APL output blocked (TNT): six of the eight have mean distances of 0.69 to 0.75 and two have 0.52 and 0.54, and the difference survives the correction for trial-to-trial noise. The effect is +0.133 with the six alone and +0.313 with the two, and the step from the six to the two has a different pattern across pairs from the step from the control flies to the six (ρ 0.12). About half of the alcohol group's part of the error common to all wirings (E72) rests on those two recordings, and against the six alone no best fit's pattern part is below what an effect with no pattern scores. Design: (A) at once, the firing-ceiling family re-fitted under the present target with its response-rate terms against the data in baseline-noise units, the first fit under that rule and the comparator; (B) with no simulations, the per-pair APL effect in the units of the table with intervals over recordings, its floors and ceilings, and the split by odors per KC in baseline-noise units; (C) with no simulations, every archived variant against the fixed-unit effects on raw counts, under the [calibrated readout](#footnotes) re-calibrated in the same unit, and binarized; (D) with no simulations, the recordings one by one, the six and the two against every other split of the eight, and every archived variant against each subset; (E) at fixed scales, the breadth-2 fit and the firing-ceiling fit along a graded block of APL's output and along the somatic threshold, compared with the recordings' two steps and read as populations of such flies; (F) the firing-ceiling families fitted to the fixed-unit effect. Readout: the mean APL effect, the loss with its mean and pattern parts and ρ on the per-pair APL effect under each unit and against each subset of recordings; the order of the archive under each; the response rates, their ratio, the response rate ρ and the odors-per-KC distributions against the realism bands. Prediction: the pattern is the same in every unit and the mean is not, so the mean part is set by the unit chosen to within about 0.05; the fits raise the mean effect to between +0.19 and +0.22 and stay short of the fixed-unit mean. If a fit reaches it inside the realism bands, the choice of unit changes parameter values only; if not, the mean part is open again in fixed units, through responses that grow with a KC's breadth as the flies' do. If the two strongest recordings are flies further along an axis the model has (a more complete block, or a higher excitability that APL otherwise evens out), the model is read as a population with the flies' spread; if no axis makes their state, it becomes a target of its own, where the pattern is largest. Proposed by atlas on 2026-11-10 from the question the response-criterion decision left open, the next steps of the per-KC normalization of the recordings (E73), the population reading of the APL-effect fit on re-drawn wirings (E72) and read-only probes of the per-trial recordings, the per-KC scale table and the archived pair distances, and moved into the list by atlas under the idle-host rule.
77. **E77, the input side of the error common to all wirings (what reaches the KCs: the antennal lobe's normalization, the weak odors' input, the shape of each odor's input, the per-claw saturation and the glomeruli's weights, screened on the best fit; APL inhibition that grows with a bouton's or a claw's own activity as an oracle emulation; a bounded input oracle).** The main target is the per-pair APL effect on odor-odor distances (each pair's distance with APL on minus its distance with APL off), scored by the APL-effect loss (lower is better; the data's own floor 0.039). In the best family (the first joint fit's family at breadth exponent 2 fitted under that loss; 0.056 under the [calibrated readout](#footnotes) on the real wiring, 0.048 for the mean over eight wirings) the mean APL effect is the data's and the pattern across pairs is what is left. That remainder is common to every wiring (E72): too little effect among 2h, IaA, pa, eb, ep and ms, too much among 1-8ol, 1-5ol, 1-6ol and 6al. No fixed profile of APL's weight over the glomeruli that held up on left-out odors was found (E75), which sends the search to what reaches the KCs. The archived variants that carry least of the common error change the input, but under their right names they are a weaker antennal-lobe normalization on the one-row-per-KC model, Hallem's receptor data for the weak odors, random wirings of that model and a 130 Hz per-claw threshold; none has a smaller pattern error than the best fit, and the first is base-dependent. The ester group's error is three things: ms's pairs, wrong with APL off (the weak-input problem); the four nearest ester pairs, too similar in both conditions; and the fruity and small esters' block. Most of the two groups' errors are per-odor shares, too small for IaA, eb and 2h, and they follow how much each odor's response fraction gains when APL is blocked. A change of the input acts in both conditions, so it can reach only the part of the error a model inherits from its distances with APL off: about two fifths of the squared pattern error, half of the ester group's error and none of the alcohol group's, whose error the flies' own on-against-off curve leaves as well. The model's antennal lobe evens out the odors' total drive (summed PN rates within 8 % across the 16 odors, where the ORN input's sums differ 2.9-fold), and the flies' per-odor APL effect follows that drive. Design: (A) at once, the best fit re-fitted with Hallem's data as the input of ms, paa, B-myr and 1-8ol, and with the bounded oracle's searched weak-odor input; (B) with no simulations, the common error by kind of pair and by odor, and split for every archived variant into the part inherited from the APL-off distances, the on-against-off curve and a pair-specific part, with the model's PN-level distances against Remy's bouton and claw matrices; (C) on the best fit at fixed scales with both response rates held, a screen of the normalization's strength, the weak odors' input, a compression or expansion of each odor's input across glomeruli, a per-claw saturation and equalized glomerulus weights, and, as oracle emulations that act only with APL on, APL's weight on each bouton or claw growing with its own activity in the odor, with permuted controls and a gate; (D) a passing handle re-fitted on the real wiring and three draws as paired differences and populations, run on four held-out wirings, through the trial noise and at Yang's low concentrations; (E) if no static handle passes, each of the ten odors' input searched within a factor of two of the recordings against one half of the flies and scored against the other, and on re-drawn wirings; (F) what the handle or the searched input is against physiology. Readout: the APL-effect loss with its mean and pattern parts, the per-odor and pair-specific parts and ρ on the per-pair APL effect under raw counts, the calibrated readout, binarized and the per-KC reading, per wiring and for the populations, against all eight recordings with APL output blocked (TNT), against the six typical ones and against the effect in baseline-noise units; the response rates, their ratio, the response rate ρ and the odors-per-KC distributions against the realism bands under both criteria. Prediction: a static handle lowers the pattern part by 0.002 to 0.006 on the real wiring and no further than 0.043 for the mean over wirings (from 0.048), the weak odors' input and the normalization being the likeliest; the mean APL effect stays matched under the readout and may rise on raw counts with a weaker normalization. If a handle transfers to wirings it was not chosen on, part of the shared error is an error of the model's input, and the handle names the stage to measure or to fit. If only the emulated inhibition does, the missing mechanism is inhibition that depends on the odor at the scale of a bouton or a claw. If nothing does, and the searched input does not hold on recordings it never saw, neither the wiring, nor where APL acts, nor the input within a factor of two carries the error, and it is looked for in the comparison's definition. Proposed by viki on 2026-11-10 from the next step of the glomerulus profile of APL inhibition (E75) and a correction of its reading of the archive, the error common to all wirings (E72), the per-odor profile of the APL effect (E66), the Hallem-input and bounded-input experiments (E38, E64), the tables of the data side of the main target (E76) and read-only probes of the archive, and moved into the list by viki under the idle-host rule.
78. **E78, the high per-claw knee without a dead zone (selection at the claw: each claw passes on mainly what exceeds a knee near the top of the bouton rates).** The error of the per-pair APL effect that is common to every wiring (E72) is largest among the fruity and small esters, and a fixed profile of APL's weight over glomeruli did not remove it for odors it was not built on (E75). Its sharpest form is in odor pairs whose ORN input is nearly the same (eb and ep; IaA and pa; 2h and pa; 2h and IaA; and, outside the esters, 6al and t2h; 1-5ol and 1-6ol). In the flies their KC distances are 0.45 with APL intact and 0.29 with APL output blocked, 0.30 and 0.12 once trial-to-trial noise is removed, so APL adds +0.17 in every unit of the recordings. (Annotation 2026-11-11: those noise-removed values come from a correction that assumes the noise of two odors independent; estimated across repeats (E81) they are 0.37 and 0.22, an APL effect of +0.14, and the model's are well above zero.) With APL blocked they are as close as their inputs, and in recordings of PN boutons and KC claws they are not yet apart, so the separation is made between claw and soma. In the best fits APL adds +0.06 to +0.12, and through the model's own trial noise the pairs are no further apart than repeats of one odor. The only archived models that hold these pairs apart as the flies do, in both conditions, are those in which a per-claw knee sits near the largest PN rate and the somatic threshold falls to a tenth, so that a KC responds through the one or two claws on an odor's strongest boutons; there the esters' error goes, at the cost of silent weak odors and a larger error elsewhere. The best family was only ever given a knee at 40 Hz, which costs it. Design: (A) at once, the breadth-2 APL-effect fit with a hard knee at 100 and 130 Hz; (B) with no simulations, the archive's knee variants on these pairs and, in the fitted model, which of a KC's claws carry its drive; (C) at fixed scales with both response rates held, a knee at 100 to 130 Hz that passes a fraction of the input below it, and a knee set relative to each odor's strongest input, in both conditions; (D) behind a gate, re-fits with the best transfer on the real wiring and three re-drawn wirings, as paired differences and as a population, a held-out population, Yang's low concentrations, and then the trial noise and the firing-ceiling family. Readout: the APL-effect loss with its mean and pattern parts and ρ on the per-pair APL effect, against all eight recordings with APL output blocked, the six typical ones and the effect in fixed units; the near-identical pairs' distances in both conditions; the two pair groups' errors; every odor's response fraction and the response rate ρ; the low-concentration recruitment; the realism bands. Prediction: a knee near the top of the bouton rates removes the esters' error and raises ρ on the per-pair APL effect, as on the plain base; whether the pattern part falls depends on whether the sub-threshold gain keeps the weak odors and the alcohol pairs, which the archive does not say. If it holds on re-drawn wirings, the selection of responders is made at the claw and the transfer becomes a candidate for the base model; if only the pairs move, the esters' and the alcohols' errors are two ends of one trade in this family.
79. **E79, the APL calyx maps against the connectome (where APL is active in the calyx, from imaging and from wiring).** Yang's 2021 calcium imaging of APL in single calyx planes (pa, paa and ms as concentration series, 12 recordings from 8 flies) gives maps that repeat within a recording (r 0.95) and differ between odors in about a third of the recordings, with pa's response lower in the image than ms's in 11 of 12. Goal: decide whether APL's activity in the calyx is local enough, and placed where the PN boutons of the responding glomeruli are, for a spatially local APL to bear on the pair-specific part of the APL effect. Design (no simulations): the maps with a position-matched blank, split into the part shared by all odors and the odor-specific rest at several scales, and their dorsal-ventral profiles; maps predicted from the bouton positions of seven connectomes weighted by each odor's PN rates (and from claws, KC spikes and the anatomy alone), in posterior-view slabs, scored against the measured pa minus ms and paa minus ms maps with a glomerulus-label permutation null; the local APL-unit model's maps against its coupling; and how much pairs of glomeruli share calyx space, and whether that predicts the error common to all wirings on left-out odors. Readout: the match of predicted to measured maps, the measured spatial scale, and the share of the common error the co-localization removes on left-out odors. Prediction: the maps match the bouton prediction (the esters' shared glomeruli lie ventral, the alcohols' DC2 dorsal-middle), but co-localization does not carry the pair error, which would close spatially local APL at a measured scale; if it does carry it (15 % or more of the common error on left-out odors), a spatially local APL at the boutons is fitted in the best family. (2026-11-15: what the two parts of this design that used the connectomes read, the predicted maps and the co-localization, is withheld wherever it is quoted, since they were made from connectome tables whose use is on hold; the readings of the measured maps stand.)
80. **E80, the flies' pulse in the model and the remaining candidates for the common error (the model run with the odor pulse the flies were given; what a three-day block, each odor's recruitment, a shared lateral input or the KC types could add).** The error common to all wirings (too little APL effect among 2h, IaA, pa, eb, ep and ms, too much among 1-8ol, 1-5ol, 1-6ol and 6al) survived every change of what reaches the KCs (E77); of the candidates left, odor-dependent inhibition in the calyx is taken by E79 and the claw's transfer by E78. Goal: settle the rest on the model's side at the lowest cost that closes or opens each. Design: (A) the best fit and the firing-ceiling fit run with the flies' 2 s pulse instead of the model's 0.5 s, at fixed parameters and with both response rates re-held; in the early models the response was an onset transient and APL reshaped the late spikes more than the early ones (E6), and a longer pulse was never run under the main target; (B) on the best fit at fixed scales with both response rates held: a threshold lower with APL on than off, an input shared by all glomeruli, each odor's response fractions put on the flies' in both conditions, a per-KC or per-type threshold that rises with the KC's activity under the block and acts only with APL off, and APL weights by KC type, with the gate of E77; (D) without simulations, the response amplitude against the number of odors a KC responds to, in the flies and in the fits. Readout: for each handle the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the [calibrated readout](#footnotes) against the best fit's 0.056 and its share of the common error; the mean APL effect on raw counts against the flies' +0.178 (+0.242 in baseline-noise units). Prediction: no handle passes the gate (each KC type alone carries the same error; the one published test of a prolonged block of APL found little compensation; a breadth-dependent amplitude moves the mean by less than 0.01); a longer pulse raises the mean APL effect on raw counts by an amount that cannot be promised.
81. **E81, the flies' variability across the three repeats (what changes in the KC responses from one repeat of the panel to the next, and the models read through variability of that structure).** Every comparison of a model with the KC data "as the data are made" passes the model through trial-to-trial noise that was calibrated on one number, how far apart two presentations of one odor are (E43), and the noise-corrected reference values assume that noise is independent between odors. The recordings hold three repeats of the 17 stimuli, and in them the variability has a structure the model's does not: responses run down over the session (by about 40 % in control flies and 15 % with APL output blocked), each odor's response drifts (the first and third repeat differ more than adjacent ones), and within one repeat two different odors are more alike than across repeats, in every recording and more so with APL output blocked, which the present correction misreads and the model's noise overstates two- to fourfold. Goal: describe that structure, give the data's distances and per-pair APL effect with it removed, and re-read the best models through variability of the flies' kind. Design: (A) with no simulations, the run-down, the drift and the shared state in both genotypes, and noise-free distances from products of different repeats, with intervals and floors; (B) every archived variant against the noise-free effect, and the saved model trials under the same statistics; (C) at once, the breadth-2 and the firing-ceiling populations through the pipeline at half the matched jitter; (D) the model's variability re-specified with the flies' shares (a shared per-KC jitter at the level the flies' same-repeat similarity supports, independent amplitude and measurement noise added to the saved trials), and the populations, the pairs with near-identical input and the block re-read through it; (E) the APL effect within each repeat against the error common to all wirings, and a threshold change common to all KCs as the model's form of the shared state. Readout: the APL-effect loss with its parts for the two populations through the re-specified noise against their present 0.061 to 0.062 and 0.059, and without noise against the noise-free effect; the six near pairs' distances in both conditions; the response-rate ratio repeat by repeat. Prediction: the breadth-2 population's loss through the noise rises by 0.005 to 0.016 (the present jitter lends it mean effect), the firing ceiling regains a lead of 0.01 or more, the near pairs' shortfall shrinks to about half the flies' effect and sits in the distance with APL on, and the common error is present from the first repeat, which closes adaptation over the session as its source.
82. **E82, the flies' recording protocol in the model (the model given the flies' 2 s odor pulse and read through the indicator's kinetics over the flies' response window).** The flies had a 2 s pulse and a response is their calcium signal averaged over the six seconds after it, so spikes late in the pulse weigh more than spikes at its onset; the model has a 0.5 s pulse and its spikes are counted over the whole simulation. E80 showed that the raw-count readings of the main target depend on the pulse: with APL on the model fires almost only at the onset, with APL off some KCs go on firing. Goal: read the best families as the flies were recorded, find what in the model holds the response with APL off to the onset, and give the numbers for the choice of reading and of base model. Design: (A) with no simulations, the saved 2 s runs read through an indicator kernel (rise 0.2 to 1 s, decay 1 to 5 s) over the flies' window, with and without the [calibrated saturation](#footnotes), the late spikes by condition, odor and KC, and the model's predicted traces; (B) at the flies' pulse with both response rates re-held, the breadth-2 and the firing-ceiling families on four wirings each, then the time constant of the ceiling's adaptation, depletion at the PN boutons, and the time constants of KC–KC inhibition and of APL; (C) a gate for a setting that returns the short pulse's numbers under the flies' protocol and the gate of E77 for the error common to all wirings; (D) fits at the flies' pulse of the family with the ceiling and of the family without it. Readout: the APL-effect loss with its parts on the real wiring and as means of four wirings, at the 0.5 s pulse against the flies' protocol, for both families; the largest spike counts; the share of spikes after the first half second in each condition. Prediction: under the flies' protocol the firing-ceiling fit loses its lead (on the real wiring 0.050 at the short pulse becomes 0.064 to 0.071 at indicator decays of 5 to 3 s, against 0.058 for the fit without a ceiling under the calibrated saturation; floor 0.039), because its adaptation, set to outlast a 0.5 s odor, wears off within a 2 s one; an adaptation that outlasts the pulse returns the short pulse's numbers; the pattern across pairs does not improve.
83. **E83, the input and APL's glomerulus profile fitted on several wirings at once (changes at the level of the glomeruli, searched on four wirings and half of the recordings, tested on other wirings and the other half).** Two earlier experiments each found, on the hemibrain wiring alone, a change that lowers the error common to all wirings there: a profile of APL's weight over the glomeruli of a KC's claws (E75), which was never run on a re-drawn wiring, and an input searched glomerulus by glomerulus for ten odors (E77), which generalized to unseen recordings on that wiring and not to the mean over wirings. The saved runs show that the searched input changes every wiring's distances alike and that its change of the APL effect points partly the way the unseen recordings ask, at about twice the size they support. Goal: decide whether a static change at the level of the glomeruli, of the input or of where APL acts, carries the common error when it is fitted on several wirings at once. Design: (A) the changes that exist on wirings they were never run on (the profile on the seven re-drawn wirings; the searched inputs at 0.6 of their size on all eight); (B) the 36 glomerulus scores of APL's weight searched in simulation on the population of four wirings against one half of the recordings; (C) each of ten odors' input searched the same way within a factor of two; (D) every searched change read, at 0.6 of its size and with both response rates re-held, on four held-out wirings against the other half, as a two-by-two of searched and unseen wirings and recordings, with a gate, label-permuted controls and the agreement between the two halves' solutions; (E) a change that passes on the firing-ceiling family, at the flies' pulse, through the trial noise, and against the connectomes' APL synapses or the fly-to-fly spread of the ORN recordings. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the [calibrated readout](#footnotes) for the held-out wirings' population, against its 0.049 on all recordings and its 0.054 to 0.057 on a half, with the realism bands. Prediction: a gain of 0.002 to 0.005 on the held-out wirings and recordings for either change, about the gate's 0.003, with no change of the mean APL effect; if neither passes, the two-by-two says whether the wirings or the eight recordings with APL output blocked (TNT) are the limit.
84. **E84, the time course of the APL effect in the flies' traces (when in the response the flies' APL effect is made, what the indicator's kinetics are in these recordings, and whether the response with APL output blocked outlasts the control flies').** Every number of the KC data so far is a mean over one window of each response, 2 to 8 s after the onset of a 2 s odor pulse, and the reading of the models as the flies were recorded (E82) had to assume the indicator's kinetics. The frame-by-frame traces of the 19 recordings can now be read. A first reading shows a signal that rises through the pulse, peaks about 1.5 s after its end and has lost less than a fifth of its peak by the end of the window in 17 of the 19 recordings, far slower than the kernels E82 assumed; the two recordings with APL output blocked that carry the largest APL effect decay about three times faster than all the others. Goal: say when in the response the per-pair APL effect and the error common to all wirings are made, measure the indicator's kinetics and re-read the models through them, test the models' predictions for the traces, and establish whether the recordings behind the target were all measured alike. Design: (A) the traces read as the experimenter's pipeline reads them, with a check that the published window is reproduced, and each recording's records as recorded; (B) with no simulations, the per-pair APL effect in windows from the odor's onset to 20 s after it, as measured and with the trial-to-trial noise removed (from products of different repeats), with recording-bootstrap intervals, by group of pairs and against the error common to all wirings, and the response rates per window at a matched criterion; (C) the responding KCs' mean trace per genotype, per recording and per class of KC, its rise, peak and decay, and the two strongest recordings against the other 17; (D) kernels fitted to the traces under two limits of the firing (a burst at the onset, a steady drive over the pulse), E82's saved spike times re-read through them over the flies' window, and each model's predicted signal with APL off relative to APL on, window by window, against the flies'; (E) the carry-over of one presentation's decaying response into the next. Readout: the mean APL effect and each group's error by window; the APL-effect loss of the best families at the flies' protocol under the measured kernel against E82's primary reading (0.071 for the firing-ceiling fit with its 1 s adaptation, 0.052 with 3 s; floor 0.039); the signal with APL off relative to APL on, late against early, in the flies and in each model. Prediction: the effect is largest during the pulse (+0.21 with the noise removed) and falls to +0.16 by the end of the window, with both groups' errors present from the first second, so the time within a response does not make the common error; under the measured kernel the flies' window weighs all spikes of the pulse about equally, so the firing-ceiling fit with its 1 s adaptation reads about 0.061, not 0.071; the family with nothing to hold the late firing predicts a signal with APL off that grows 1.4-fold relative to APL on, which the flies do not show (1.07, 90 % interval 0.94 to 1.24), while the flies cannot tell a 1 s adaptation from a 3 s one.
85. **E85, the ORN input as a population of flies (each ORN fly's own responses as the model's input, read as the KC data are made).** Every model takes as its input the mean over the ORN flies of each glomerulus's response, while the KC data are means over recordings of distances computed within a fly, each fly with its own input. The wiring was made a population of flies in E72, and the mean over eight wirings is better than a single one; the input has stayed one averaged fly. In the megamat ORN recordings (9 flies) a single fly's response differs from the mean by a factor of about 1.4 to 1.8 per glomerulus and odor, most of it repeating across the fly's trials, and odors with near-identical input are 1.06 to 1.7 times further apart within a fly than their averaged vectors are. E83 found that a change of the input acts on every wiring alike but that a free search of it fits the recordings it is given; here nothing is fitted. Goal: say whether the flies' own differences in input, put into the model as they were measured, move the per-pair APL effect, the two distance matrices and the pairs with near-identical input toward the KC data, and whether the input searched in E77 is of a size and direction that flies differ by. Design: (A) with no simulations, each ORN fly's responses scaled as the lab's input is, their spread per glomerulus and odor and the share of it that repeats across trials, the ORN distances within a fly, and the four searched inputs of E77 and E83 set against that spread; (B) the breadth-2 APL-effect fits of the eight wirings with each of the nine flies' reproducible deviations from the fly mean as the input, both response rates re-held, with the deviations as measured and with each fly's glomerulus gains divided out as bounds, a label-permuted control, and a reading with nothing re-held; (C) populations of model flies (a wiring with an input fly) scored like the populations of wirings, the wiring and input draw terms, and a mixture of input flies fitted on four wirings and one half of the recordings and tested on the other four and the other half; (D) the firing-ceiling family read the same way if the reading moves the target. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout for the model flies against the eight wirings at the fly-mean input (0.048; floor 0.029), the mean APL effect (+0.178 for both), the mean distances and ρ on odor-odor distances with APL on and with APL off (0.745 and 0.567 against the flies' 0.840 and 0.661; ρ 0.67 and 0.76), and the APL effect on the pairs with near-identical input (+0.09 against the flies' +0.14, trial noise removed). Prediction: the pattern part ends between 0.045 and 0.049, a gain of 0.003 or more being less likely than not; the mean APL effect stays within 0.01; both mean distances rise by 0.01 to 0.03 and the near pairs' APL effect by 0.02 to 0.04; and the searched input at 0.6 of its size lies inside the flies' spread by size (84 % of its factors within one SD) without its direction being shown to be theirs.
86. **E86, the controls and the replication of the reduced searched input (whether the one change that held on unseen wirings and recordings is more than the better two of four searches).** In E83 the input that E77 had searched on the hemibrain wiring alone, reduced to 0.6 of its size, lowered the pattern part of the APL-effect loss on four wirings and on the half of the recordings it was never fitted to, by a little more than the gate asked and without the gate's control; the same search run on four wirings did not, and no two searches agree on the glomeruli. Goal: decide whether that input carries part of the error common to all wirings or is the best draw of a short search. Design: (A) with no simulations, the four searched inputs by the pairs they move and wiring by wiring; (B) the reduced input with its factors permuted across each odor's glomeruli, and inverted, on the four held-out wirings with both response rates re-held; (C) the search repeated on the hemibrain wiring with a second draw against the same halves and on a second split of the flies, each new input read the same way, with a verdict fixed beforehand (passes, carries below the gate, falls); (D) on a gain, what the input is, by the pairs it moves and against the fly-to-fly spread of the ORN recordings; (E) only on a pass, the firing-ceiling family, the flies' 2 s pulse and the trial noise. Readout: the pattern part under the [calibrated readout](#footnotes) for the held-out wirings against the unseen half (0.054 and 0.057 unchanged, lower is better, floor 0.029; the reduced input gains 0.0036 and 0.0031), with the realism bands. Prediction: the permuted and the inverted inputs lose (about 0.006 and 0.014), the re-searched inputs gain 0.001 to 0.003 on average, so a pass is less likely than not, and a fall closes fitted changes of the input for this family.
87. **E87, the form of APL's inhibition in the flies' own responses (which inhibition, applied to the KC responses with APL output blocked, gives the control flies' distances, and what the same inhibition makes of the models' responses without APL).** Every experiment on the error common to all wirings has changed the model, its APL, its input, its wiring, its claws or its time course, and read the model; none has asked the recordings what APL does to a fly's own KC responses. The recordings with APL output blocked hold those responses without APL, KC by KC, and E7 had already brought the two genotypes to one density with a threshold, before the per-pair APL effect was the main target. Goal: say whether the pattern of the flies' per-pair APL effect is predictable from their own responses with APL output blocked by a simple form of inhibition, which form, and whether the models' error then lies in the form of their APL or in their responses without APL. Design: (A) with no simulations, each KC's response to an odor in a recording with APL output blocked reduced by an amount that grows with the odor's response over all KCs (exponent q) and with the KC's breadth (exponent p), one constant set by the control flies' response rate, the predicted distances scored against the control flies', as trial means and with the trial noise removed, in z and in baseline-noise units, the form chosen on one half of the recordings and scored on the other, with the odors' strengths and the KCs' breadths permuted as controls and an inhibition from KCs of similar tuning beside; (B) the same forms applied to the APL-off responses of the breadth-2 and the firing-ceiling fits, as a two-by-two of whose responses and which inhibition; (C) the effect of a form within one fly as a target that needs neither control flies nor APL, its floor and ceiling, and every archived variant against it; (D) the forms as a static inhibition in place of APL on the eight wirings, and any handle that stands out in the archive. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss with sub-threshold responses set to 0 (the data's effect +0.133 there, floor of the pattern part about 0.027), the mean APL effect, ρ on the per-pair APL effect, the ester and alcohol groups and the pairs with near-identical input, with the forms' predicted response fractions and odors per KC against the control flies' and the realism bands for the simulations. Prediction: a uniform inhibition of the flies' own responses leaves a pattern part of about 0.045 and an inhibition proportional to the odor's response over all KCs about 0.032, 0.040 against 0.050 on unseen halves; the breadth-2 population's own APL has 0.049 and no form applied to its APL-off responses does better than about 0.044, so the error is expected in the models' responses without APL; the effect of a form within a fly has less than half the main target's floor; the static forms in simulation stay within 0.003 of the family's 0.048.
88. **E88, the common error split into who responds and how strongly (which factor of a pair's similarity, each odor's recruitment, the co-response of the responder sets or the shared responders' amplitudes, carries the models' error in the per-pair APL effect, and what APL does to each).** E87 read the error common to all wirings first in what the models' KCs do without APL and, once each odor's recruitment is the flies', partly in what the models' APL makes of those responses, and left open which KCs carry the overlap of the odor pairs concerned. With sub-threshold responses set to 0, the similarity of two odors is exactly the product of three factors: how many KCs each odor recruits, how many times more often than chance a KC that responds to one responds to the other, and how strong the shared responders' two responses are against all responders'. The first two are who responds, the third how strongly, and the per-pair APL effect is the fall of the product from APL off to APL on. Goal: assign the models' error in the per-pair APL effect to a factor and a condition, say for the ester and the alcohol pairs whether what is missing lies in the responses without APL or in what APL does to them, name the model's KCs concerned, and test in simulation the one step the earlier readings left on APL's side. Design: (A) with no simulations, the three factors in the recordings of both genotypes and in the breadth-2 and the firing-ceiling fits read as the KC data are made (each KC z-scored over its session, through the measurement noise, as trial means and with the trial noise removed by one estimator on both sides, after a check of the estimators on a model with known truth), the flies' factors put into the models' product one at a time, with floors from resampled recordings and the six typical recordings with APL output blocked beside all eight, on the fits as they are and on the runs with each odor's recruitment set to the flies'; (B) the change of each factor under APL by group of pairs, the co-response that a per-odor cut of the responses without APL and the flies' form leave against what APL leaves, the shared responders' amplitude ranks, and in the model the shared responders that survive APL and those it removes by breadth, KC type, claws and driving glomeruli; (C) every archived variant by the same factors, to find a handle that moves them; (D) the flies' form as a static inhibition in place of APL on the four searched wirings with the flies' per-odor recruitment, and at most three handles that stand out in the archive. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss with sub-threshold responses set to 0 for the model's own product and for each product with the flies' factors, the ester and the alcohol groups' errors, each factor's change under APL, and for the simulations the main target's scores with E83's gate and the realism bands. Prediction: with the flies' responder sets and the model's amplitudes the pattern part falls from about 0.044 to about 0.02 and both group errors go, with the model's sets and the flies' amplitudes it stays near 0.036; the ester group's error goes with recruitment (under APL an ester pair's recruitment falls to 0.44 of its value without APL in the flies and to 0.51 in the model) and the alcohol group's with the co-response (it rises 1.6-fold under APL in the flies and 1.2-fold in the model), each factor alone making the model worse because the two compensate; the alcohols' shortfall is expected on APL's side and the esters' excess, once recruitment is the flies', in the responses; the static form on the base with the flies' recruitment is expected within 0.003 of the unchanged fits' 0.050.
89. **E89, the co-response read with a checked estimate and the breadth weighting inside the models' APL (whether the models' error in the per-pair APL effect lies in who responds, once the co-response of two odors' responder sets has an estimate that passes its check, and whether another measure or exponent of the weighting of APL's inhibition by a KC's breadth does better).** E88 wrote a pair's similarity, with sub-threshold responses set to 0, as the product of each odor's recruitment, the co-response of the two responder sets and an amplitude factor of the shared responders, and put the flies' factors into the models' product one at a time. Every reading through the trial noise pointed to who responds, but on a model with known truth the co-response was read too low as trial means and too high with the trial noise removed, so by the rule fixed beforehand the split was not read. E88 also closed static forms of inhibition on the base with the flies' recruitment and named the breadth weighting of the feedback APL as what removes the broad, strongly responding KCs that similar odors share. Goal: read the split with an estimate of the co-response that passes the check, and decide whether the weighting of APL's inhibition by a KC's breadth, its measure and its exponent, carries a part of the pattern error, on the fits as they are and once each odor's recruitment is the flies'. Design: (A) with no simulations, four estimates of the co-response (the two of E88, their geometric mean, an estimate from latent classes after the trial noise is deconvolved, and a correction by each odor's agreement between repeats) checked on the models' known truth at the reading's own noise and at the flies' repeatability, there with a part of each KC's noise shared by the odors of a repeat as E81 found in the flies, then E88's cells, verdicts and changes under APL read again with one that passes; (B) the saved models in which only the weighting differs (E66's sweep of measure and exponent in the reference models, E67's fits at exponents 1 to 3) read by the factors beside their main-target scores; (C) twelve weightings, three measures of breadth (the family's spike total in the bouton base, the KC's summed response and the number of odors it responds to in the run that APL acts on) at exponents from 0 to 2, on the four searched wirings, on the breadth-2 fits as they are with both response rates re-held and on the base with the flies' per-odor recruitment with each odor's APL scale set again, the best against each half of the recordings then read on the four held-out wirings against the other half with E83's gate and the measure's values permuted across the KCs as the control. Readout: each factor's error against known truth by group of pairs and noise level; the pattern part of the APL-effect loss for the model's own product and for each product with the flies' factors; the change of the co-response under APL in the flies and the models, like for like; for the simulations the pattern part under the calibrated readout against the half not chosen on, the loss, raw counts, the mean APL effect, the block, the alcohol and ester groups' errors and co-response changes, and the realism bands. Prediction: the geometric mean of the two existing estimates is inside the tolerance at the reading's own noise (about 0.02 for 0.03 allowed) and near it at the flies' repeatability (0.03 to 0.04 without a shared part of the noise, 0.02 to 0.03 with one), the amplitude factor less well, so that the groups' assignment is likelier to be read than the split itself; like for like the alcohols' co-response rises about 1.5-fold under APL in the flies and 1.25- to 1.4-fold in the models, a narrower difference than the trial means showed; in the saved models the co-response already follows the weighting without the pattern part following it (E67's fits: 0.058 at exponent 1 for 0.056 at 2), so every weighting is expected within 0.003 of its reference, a gain of 0.003 or more on the held-out wirings being judged about one in six on the base with the flies' recruitment and one in ten against the unchanged fits, and the likeliest result is that the co-response under APL is dropped as a lead and APL's side is closed for the weighting.
90. **E90, the model's stages against the flies' stages (where along the pathway from PN to KC soma the model's odor-odor distances leave the flies' for the ester and the alcohol pairs, read on recordings of the PN dendrites, the PN boutons and the KC claws, and whether the main target's pattern is there between the claws and the somata of wild-type flies).** Every experiment since E60 held the models to the KC somata, and E87 to E89 left the lead in the models' responses without APL, between the claw and the soma. For the panel's 12 megamat odors three earlier stages are recorded in wild-type flies and had not been used stage by stage: the lab's GH146 PN-dendrite recordings by glomerulus, and Remy's per-frame matrices of PN boutons and KC claws; E77's one comparison set the model's PN rates against her matrices without the same reading. Goal: decide, for each group of pairs, whether the model differs from the flies before the claws, at the step from claws to somata with APL on, or at that step without APL, and whether the step in wild-type flies can serve as a second reading of the main target's pattern that uses no fly with APL output blocked (TNT). Design: (A) with no simulations, the distances at each stage in the flies with intervals over flies, the noise removed through block averages for boutons and claws, the two steps per pair (somata with APL on minus claws, somata with APL output blocked minus claws) against the per-pair APL effect and the error common to all wirings, the step's floor and the agreement of the claw flies' halves, and the ORN-to-PN transfer as recorded; (B) the breadth-2 fits of the eight wirings run at fixed parameters with bouton rates and APL's activity saved, their PN, bouton and claw stages read as the recordings were made (rate changes, per-unit z-scores over a simulated session, through measurement noise with the noise removed the same way, and for the claws with and without a share of the KC's spike signal), with a check of the noise removal on the model's known values and one check at the flies' 2 s pulse; (C) a verdict per group fixed beforehand, a difference counting only if it is 0.05 or more, outside the flies' interval, shared by six of the eight wirings and present in every reading; (D) only as the verdict says, the firing-ceiling family read the same way, or the antennal-lobe settings of E77's screen read at the PN stage beside their main-target scores. Readout: the stage table and the two steps by group for the flies and the model; the verdict; the step target's floor; the model's per-odor PN totals and entries against the PN dendrites'; nothing of the APL-effect loss moves. Prediction: in the flies the step with APL on is two to three times as large among the esters as among the alcohols (about +0.25 against +0.09) and correlates near 0.65 with the per-pair APL effect over the 66 pairs, while without APL the somata are within 0.1 of the claws; the model's PN stage equalizes each odor's total where the PN dendrites do not (coefficient of variation near 0.07 against 0.32); which of the two steps carries the ester group's error cannot be said before the model's claw stage is read, and the two likeliest verdicts are a step with APL on that is too small from the right input, and a step without APL that is too large from an input that is too alike.
91. **E91, APL's inhibition on the KC's summed drive (whether APL's inhibition of a KC is subtracted claw by claw, each claw's net drive clipped at zero as in every model so far, or from the sum of its claws, which selects responders by how many of a KC's claws an odor drives).** E87 and E88 closed static forms of inhibition in place of APL, and E89 closed the weighting of APL's inhibition by a KC's breadth; all three left untouched where the feedback APL's subtraction is made. In olfsysm it is made at each claw, and a claw's net drive cannot fall below zero, so a KC driven through one or two strong claws loses little to APL and a KC driven through many weak claws loses most of its drive; on the summed drive every claw's share is charged whether the odor drives the claw or not. The responses without APL are the same under both. Goal: decide whether the clipping at the claw limits the best family on the per-pair APL effect, and whether the summed form lets the breadth weighting, whose weights are spread several hundredfold over the responding KCs, be weakened at no cost. Design: a new olfsysm parameter, off by default, passes a share s of each claw's net-negative drive to the sum while APL's own drive stays as it is; (A) with no new parameter, what the clipping holds back in the breadth-2 fits of the four searched wirings, by class of KC, as a prediction of who is removed; (B) s at 0.5 and 1 by the family's weighting at exponents 2, 1 and 0.5 and with none, on those wirings with the response rate with APL on re-held (the APL-off runs are the saved ones), s at 1 on the base with the flies' recruitment, and the existing flag, which also changes APL's drive, for the record; (C) the best setting against each half of the recordings read on the four held-out wirings against the other half with E83's gate, with a static inhibition of the same size per KC and no dependence on the odor as the control, and a rule for a weaker weighting that is the family's equal; (D) the family fitted again with the summed form on the same wirings, with the same loss and free parameters, and compared with the clipped family as a population of eight wirings; (E) only on a pass, the firing-ceiling family, the flies' 2 s pulse and the trial noise. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout against the half not chosen on and against all recordings, the loss, raw counts, the mean APL effect, the ester and the alcohol groups' errors, the alcohols' co-response change under APL, the block, the number of claws that carry a responder's drive and the realism bands. Prediction: at the family's weighting and fixed scales the pattern part moves by less than 0.003, because APL's inhibition at a responder's claws is 0.07 of the odor's largest bouton rate in the fits; at weaker weightings the summed form recovers 0.002 to 0.008 of the 0.006 to 0.022 that the clipped form loses there; the ester group's error shrinks by 0.005 to 0.015 and the mean APL effect falls by 0.005 to 0.02 before a re-fit; a pass is judged about one in seven, an equal at a weaker weighting about one in four, and the likeliest result is that the clipping is not what limits the family and APL's postsynaptic side is closed in form, weighting and place.
92. **E92, the claw-to-soma steps read like for like and APL's inhibition as a gain at the claw (whether the models leave the flies between claws and somata with APL on once both sides' somata are read the same way, and whether APL's inhibition of a KC divides each claw's drive instead of being subtracted from it).** E90 compared the model's stages with recordings of the flies' stages and found the step from claws to somata with APL on too small for the ester pairs and the pairs with near-identical ORN input; its claws were read like for like, its somata were not (the flies' trial means carry their trial noise and Yang's per-KC z, the model's counts neither). Goal: read both sides' somata the same way, then test the one form of APL's inhibition of the KCs this family has not run. Design: no simulations first (the flies' somata with the trial noise removed from products of different repeats; the saved breadth-2 fits of eight wirings under the per-KC session z-score; E90's rule on the somata and on both steps by group; every archived variant against the line along which the saved breadth weightings trade the ester pairs against the alcohol pairs); then APL's inhibition as a divisor on each claw's drive, at the family's breadth weighting and at weaker ones, at fixed scales on four wirings with the response rate with APL on re-held, the choice against each half of the recordings read on four held-out wirings with the gate for fitted changes and a static control; the family fitted again only on a choice or an equal. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout, with its amplitude and shape parts, the mean APL effect, the two groups' distances with APL on and the realism bands. Prediction: like for like, the models' somata without APL are the flies' by group, and the error is made with APL on, in opposite directions for the ester and the alcohol pairs; the gain is level or worse at about three in four, which would close the form of APL's inhibition of the KCs.

93. **E93, the measured input for the glomeruli without ORN input (whether the responses that recordings hold for ten glomeruli which the model's input fills with zeros change which KCs respond and, through them, the per-pair APL effect).** The model's ORN input holds the 36 glomeruli that both of the lab's panels have; the other 18 of the wiring's 54 are filled with zeros, and their claws, a sixth of all, carry no odor. For ten of them responses exist: VA4, VL1, DL4 and VM4 in the lab's own recordings, and six more in Hallem's receptor data. No search of the input could reach these rows, and in the saved fits the KCs with many such claws almost never respond. Goal: put the measured rows in and see whether a part of the error common to all wirings was the input's coverage. Design: no simulations first (what each row carries and how reliably; who responds in the saved fits by a KC's share of zero-filled claws; the PN dendrites against the ORNs at the entries on which six searches of the input agree, with a rule fixed beforehand); then the breadth-2 fits of eight wirings with the ten rows added, Hallem's values scaled per odor to the lab's units, both response rates re-held, read against all recordings and both halves of both splits with the gate for fitted changes on the four held-out wirings, with the lab's rows alone, Hallem's values unscaled and VA4 alone as bounds, label- and odor-permuted controls, and the 66 megamat pairs beside; the model's stages read again with the fuller input; the family fitted again on a pass or an equal. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts, the mean APL effect, the target without APL, the alcohol pairs' co-response change under APL, the two groups' distances with APL on, and the realism bands. Prediction: an equal at about two in three (the rows change 5 to 15 % of some odors' responders and leave the per-pair effect within 0.003), a pass at about one in twelve; the PN dendrites do not depart from the ORNs in the searched list's direction.

94. **E94, the PN boutons' share of APL's inhibition (whether the per-pair APL effect depends on how much of APL's inhibition is made at the PN boutons and in which form, a subtraction or a gain).** The best family's APL acts on two paths: on the KCs at each claw, where its weighting, its summation and its form have been varied, and on the PN boutons, where the scale has stayed under a bound inherited from an earlier family and has not moved in any fit. In the saved fits about half of the drive that APL takes from the claws is taken at the boutons, as an offset that is the same for every odor, where the connectome puts a tenth of APL's calyx synapses. Goal: find what the family reads with less and with more of its inhibition at the boutons, and with a gain there in place of the subtraction. Design: no simulations first (what the bouton path does in the saved fits of eight wirings, its share of the claws' loss of drive against the connectome's synapse share, and the distances between odors at the bouton stage under other doses); then, at fixed scales on four wirings with the response rate with APL on re-held, the APL→bouton scale from none to four times the fitted dose at the family's breadth weighting, at twice and four times with a weaker weighting, and a gain at the boutons at a dose of its own, the choice against each half of the recordings read on four held-out wirings with the gate for fitted changes and a static control; the family fitted again with the bouton share at the connectome's, and above the bound or with the gain only on a choice or an equal. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts, the mean APL effect, the errors of the ester and the alcohol pairs, the alcohol pairs' co-response change under APL, the share of APL's action made at the boutons, and the realism bands. Prediction: a pass at about one in eight; an equal with the bouton share at the connectome's at about one in two after the refit; the gain level or worse; at the bouton stage the subtraction adds twice as much distance to the ester pairs as to the alcohol pairs, so more of it should raise the esters' effect relative to the alcohols'.

95. **E95, APL's inhibition as a static threshold raise per KC (whether the best family's APL, at the PN boutons and in all, does more for the per-pair APL effect than a fixed threshold raise per KC that is present only with APL on, and whether the offset that the family makes at the boutons can be made at the KCs).** In the saved fits of the best family, 97 % of the variation of what APL takes from a KC's drive at the PN boutons, and 90 % of what it takes at the claws, is a value per KC that is the same for every odor; APL's activity varies by less than a tenth over the odors, and over two fifths of it is there before the odor. On the real wiring a static inhibition per KC in place of the whole APL read within 0.005 of the fit in the pattern part under the calibrated readout and no worse on raw counts (probes). Goal: find how much of the family's match to the per-pair APL effect is a threshold difference per KC between the two conditions, what of the bouton path a threshold raise by claw count carries, and whether the bouton share can be the connectome's when the same offset is made at the KCs. Design: no simulations first (the loss of drive per KC and odor at the boutons and at the claws in the fits of eight wirings, split into a part per KC, a part per odor and the rest, and the part per KC against the claw count); then, at fixed scales on four wirings with the response rate with APL on re-held, the bouton path replaced by a static inhibition present only with APL on (each KC's own mean loss, by claw count alone, at other exponents on the claw count, permuted, and odor by odor), the whole APL replaced the same way, and an unweighted component of the APL→KC weights in the feedback APL with the bouton path at a tenth of its fitted scale, the choice against each half of the recordings read on four held-out wirings with the gate for fitted changes and permuted controls; the family fitted again at the connectome's bouton share with the unweighted component. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts, raw counts beside, the mean APL effect, the errors of the ester and the alcohol pairs, the alcohol pairs' co-response change under APL, the share of APL's action made at the boutons, the static inhibition against the KCs' thresholds, and the realism bands. Prediction: level; the static forms within 0.002 to 0.005 of the fits under the calibrated readout and level on raw counts, an equal by the rule at about one in three; by claw count alone the bouton path is carried and the whole APL is not; at the connectome's bouton share an equal at about one in three after the refit, and better than the earlier refit at that share at about four in five; a pass at about one in twelve.
96. **E96, APL's synapses on the KCs outside the claws (whether the synapses that APL makes onto a KC off its claws, which the model's weights leave out, can carry the offset that the best family makes at the PN boutons, so that the family holds with the bouton path at the connectome's share).** The best family makes about seven tenths of what its APL takes from the claws' drive at the PN boutons, where the connectome puts about a tenth of APL's calyx synapses; for a KC that path is a threshold raise in proportion to its claws, and with the path cut to a tenth a part of the APL→KC weights that is the same on every claw recovers most of the cost, where the weights that the connectome gives each claw lose the mean APL effect. The model's APL→KC weights count only the synapses on claws that its wiring keeps: on the real wiring that is 59 % of APL's calyx synapses onto the model's KCs, 30 % lying on no claw, and nearly as many again as those left out lie in the peduncle. The synapses on no claw reach nearly every KC, grow with the claw count less than in proportion, and are about as unequal among KCs with the same number of claws as what the bouton path takes (probes of the tables the model reads). Goal: find whether the family reads as its unchanged fits do with the bouton path at the connectome's share once the model has these synapses, and whether it is the counts themselves or any even component that does it. Design: no simulations first (the counts per KC, on kept claws, on dropped claws, on no claw and in the peduncle, against the claw count and against what the bouton path takes from the KC, in the model's tables and in the derived tables of four connectomes; the reading of the derived tables is withheld since 2026-11-15, their use being on hold, and no run used it); then, at fixed scales on four wirings with the bouton path at a tenth and the response rate with APL on re-held, a component of the APL→KC weights per KC from the synapses on no claw, of the calyx alone and with the peduncle's, clipped at each claw and on the KC's summed drive, with the counts permuted among KCs with the same number of claws and over all KCs, without the bouton path, and with all of APL's synapses unweighted, the choice against each half of the recordings read on four held-out wirings with the gate for fitted changes and the uniform component as control; the family fitted again at the connectome's bouton share with the component, from two starts. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts, raw counts beside, the mean APL effect, the errors of the ester and the alcohol pairs, the alcohol pairs' co-response change under APL, the shares of APL's action made at the boutons and by the component against the synapses' shares, and the realism bands. Prediction: level; at fixed scales within 0.003 to 0.006 of the fits with the mean APL effect held at about three in four; the counts and the permuted counts alike; after the refit an equal of the unchanged fits at about one in three and as good as the uniform component at about three in five; a pass at about one in fifteen.

97. **E97, the glomeruli's shares of the claws in other connectomes (whether the error that every wiring shares is in part the proportions of the one brain that all the wirings come from).** Every wiring that the best family has been read on, the hemibrain's and seven re-draws of it, gives each glomerulus the hemibrain's number of claws. Whether a glomerulus's share of the claws in the male CNS's two mushroom bodies differs from its share in the hemibrain, and how such differences compare with the changes that the searches of the input reached for, is the question. Goal: find whether the per-pair APL effect of the family depends on those proportions, and whether the hemibrain's are a fair stand-in for the flies', as the fly-mean ORN input was. Design (re-planned 2026-11-17 on the hemibrain and the male CNS, both as curated by Pratyush; the first design read connectome tables whose use is on hold, and none of its probes, counts or runs is kept): the male CNS's claws counted from his per-synapse table by the steps of the model's loader, with the model's real wiring as the hemibrain's side of every ratio and, as an identity before anything else, the same reader returning the model's claws from the hemibrain's table; no simulations first (the claws, boutons and PNs per glomerulus in three mushroom bodies of two brains, as ratios to the hemibrain, the male CNS's two sides against each other, and the six searched inputs' factors against the ratios); then the eight wirings with claws moved between glomeruli to the proportions of each of the male CNS's two mushroom bodies and to the mean of the two brains, both response rates re-held, read as populations against the eight as they are, with the ratios permuted among glomeruli, inverted, halved and doubled and a second draw of the moved claws as controls and the fuller input beside (84 points); the family fitted again at the mean proportions only if the population moves. Nothing is fitted to the KC recordings, so the verdict is read against all of them. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts, raw counts beside, the mean APL effect, the errors of the ester and the alcohol pairs, the six near pairs, the terms that a brain's and a side's proportions add beside those of a wiring draw and of an input fly, and the realism bands. Prediction: no move, within 0.0015 of the eight wirings' 0.0482, at about four in five; a part of the common error at about one in fourteen; a move of 0.003 or more with the controls flat at about one in thirty; worse by 0.0015 or more at about one in twelve; the searched inputs' factors going with the ratios at about one in eight. The other brain is one fly, a male, so a verdict of no move says common to two brains' proportions, not common to flies'.

98. **E98, the strength of APL's response in the flies against the model's APL (whether the best family's APL, which is nearly the same for every odor of the panel, responds as the flies' APL does wherever Yang's calyx maps allow the comparison).** In the saved fits of the best family APL's activity varies by about 3 % over the 16 odors and two fifths of it is there before the odor, while in Yang's 2021 calcium imaging of APL in the calyx (pa, paa and ms as concentration series, 12 recordings from 8 flies) the calyx-mean response grows with concentration in every odor; near 10^-3 the flies' APL responds about equally to the three odors, as the model's does, and from 10^-4 to 10^-5 of paa it falls to about half, where the model's is expected to keep four fifths or more (probes). Goal: find whether APL's own activity is among the things in which the family departs from the flies, at the panel's concentrations and along concentration, and which recordings would tell a flat APL from one that follows the KCs' response. Design: no simulations first (each fly's calyx-mean response by odor and concentration with a blank subtracted, as ratios within a fly with intervals over flies; the saved APL activity of the fits of eight wirings; what an APL in proportion to the KCs' response and the saved spike-driven fits would show; the pairs of panel odors that separate a flat from a proportional APL in the fewest flies); then the saved fits at fixed parameters on paa at 10^-5, the other sessions' estimates of ms, low concentrations of panel odors, Hallem's concentration series for pa and ms and a blank, the model's ratios read through a family of indicator nonlinearities with and without the pre-odor level; the 1 s pulse, the fuller input, the connectome's bouton share, the spike-driven fits and weaker PN normalization beside. Nothing is fitted, chosen or scored on the main target. Readout: for paa against ms near 10^-3 and for paa at 10^-5 against 10^-4, whether the model's ratio lies inside the flies' interval, is flatter or is steeper, on at least six of the eight wirings; the same with Hallem's input beside; the KCs' response to a blank with APL on and off. Prediction: the APL-effect loss does not move; across odors the model is inside at about five in six; along concentration it is inside at about two in five and flatter than the flies at about two in five, which would be a second measurement, beside the PN dendrites' totals, that the model's drive of APL does not scale with the input.
99. **E99, the evenness of APL's weights among KCs and over a KC's claws (which property of a part of the APL→KC weights holds the mean APL effect once the bouton path is at the connectome's share, and whether a weight made of counted synapses has it).** With the bouton path cut to a tenth, a part of the APL→KC weights that is the same on every claw holds the mean APL effect, and parts made of APL's counted synapses, off the claws, on them or both, lose 0.02 to 0.06 of it while reading alike on the pattern part. The counted parts differ from the uniform one in three ways that no run separates: a KC's amount grows with its number of claws less than in proportion, KCs with the same number of claws get unequal amounts, and the synapses on the claws lie unevenly over a KC's claws. Probes of the saved runs favour the second: with the off-claw part the fifth of the KCs with the fewest such synapses for their number of claws makes 36 % of the responses with APL on and the fifth with the most 5 %, where the uniform part leaves 20 and 18 %, and KCs with few and with many claws respond alike under both. Goal: find which of the three loses the mean, how even a counted weight has to be to hold it, and whether each KC's count of all of APL's synapses, spread evenly over its claws, does. Design: no simulations first (each part's weight per claw split into what lies between groups of KCs with the same number of claws, among the KCs of a group and within KCs, the saved means against the three, a prediction for every new setting written beforehand, and who responds with APL on in the saved runs); then, at fixed scales on four wirings with the bouton path at a tenth and the response rate with APL on re-held, the off-claw counts with every group given the same amount per claw and each KC's count kept relative to its group, with every KC given its group's mean, with half the inequality and as presence alone; the on-claw counts spread evenly over each KC's claws, and equal amounts per KC placed as the on-claw counts lie; and each KC's count of all of APL's synapses spread evenly, with and without the peduncle's, at three sizes; the family fitted again with that last part only if it holds the mean at fixed scales. Readout: the mean APL effect under the calibrated readout as shares of the gap between the uniform and the counted part, wiring by wiring, with raw counts beside; the pattern part of the APL-effect loss with its amplitude and shape parts for every setting; the realism bands. Prediction: the inequality among KCs with the same number of claws carries the loss at about three in five and shares it with the slope at about three in ten; the uneven placement within a KC carries most of what the on-claw weights lose at about one in two; all counted synapses spread evenly hold the mean at about one in two; no setting moves the pattern part.

100. **E100, the flies' APL at the weak odors and at the panel's concentrations (whether the best family's APL responds as the flies' APL does at weak input and at the panel's own concentrations, and whether where APL responds in the calyx follows the wiring).** The comparison of the strength of APL's response rests on 12 recordings of 2021 with pa, paa and ms, none with pa within one log unit of the model's input, and reads the calyx mean alone. Yang's folders hold more recordings of APL in the calyx with the same indicator (an inventory made from her stimulus files, notes and settings files): 23 processed recordings from 9 flies of January 2025 with 2h, ma, 4h-ol, farn and 2-but at low concentrations, each driving one glomerulus most, and paa at 10^-3 in 12 of them; a series of pa, paa and ms in whole log steps to 10^-3 and beyond (2022, 3 flies); 2h and ma as concentration series; and one fly with the 16 panel odors at the panel's concentrations, as raw movies only. The lab's ORN recordings hold the input for six of the weak stimuli at the recorded concentration and for three more within one log unit.
