Three are stimuli of the low-concentration KC recordings, where the family's APL takes a larger share of the responders than the flies' APL: at ma at 10^-7 the response rate is 0.050 in control flies and 0.114 with APL output blocked (TNT), against 0.016 with APL on and 0.054 with APL off in the family's fit on the real wiring. Goal: find whether the family's nearly flat APL is a measured departure from the flies at weak input and inside the panel, and whether APL's calyx maps can constrain a spatially local APL at all. Design: no new recording first (the inventory; the fits of the eight wirings at fixed parameters on the weak stimuli, APL on and acutely off; maps predicted from the positions of the PN boutons for five pairs of weak odors in three calyces, the hemibrain's right and the male CNS's two, both from tables curated by Pratyush (re-planned 2026-11-17: the eight calyces of the first design came from connectome tables whose use is on hold, and nothing made from them is used), with anatomy-alone and label-permuted controls and, for each pair, the chance that a true wiring-based map would be detected at the measured maps' reliability, worked out across the two brains for slabs near the plane's depth and for the whole calyx projected along its depth); then, after Yang's answer on which recordings may be used and on the channel, each fly's calyx response by stimulus with the recording's own solvent response subtracted, as ratios within a fly with intervals over flies, the model's ratios read through a family of indicator nonlinearities; the maps' repeat reliability, the share of one pattern common to all stimuli and the odor-specific remainder; and the predicted maps against the measured ones with the orientation fixed as far as it is known, each recording against each of the three calyces, with the two differences of the first map test (pa minus ms and paa minus ms in the 2021 recordings) read through the same calyces beside. Only the APL channel is read; nothing is fitted, chosen or scored on the main target. Readout: for six weak stimuli, each as a ratio over paa at 10^-3, whether the model's ratio lies inside the flies' interval, is flatter or is steeper on at least six of the eight wirings, the verdict by four of the six; ms over pa at 10^-3, decided only with four or more flies; whether the predicted maps exceed the label-permuted null in more than half of the recordings, in the hemibrain's calyx and in at least one of the male CNS's two, for at least three pairs, read on the slabs if at least three pairs have a power of 0.8 there, on the projection if at least three have it there, and on no pair otherwise; the common pattern's share at the weak odors; the KCs' response rates with APL on and off at the same stimuli beside the flies'. Prediction: the APL-effect loss does not move; at the weak odors the family is flatter than the flies at about three in five and inside at about one in four; ms over pa flatter at about one in two; for the maps predicted from the wiring, written without the withheld readings, no pair with enough power at about one in two, a fail at about one in four, not decided at about one in seven and a pass at about one in ten; a pass would give a spatially local APL at the boutons a premise, and no pair with enough power would leave how far the predicted maps agree across the two brains, against across the two sides of one, as the result.

101. **E101, the flies' APL against their KC claws in the dual-colour recordings (whether the flies' APL follows the summed response of their own claws, as the best family's APL is built to; whether the family's claws and APL carry pa, paa and ms across odors and along concentration as the flies' do; and whether APL's calcium follows the claws place by place).** The two readings of the flies' APL before this one rest on recordings of APL alone and cannot say what APL follows. The best family's APL is nearly flat (pa over ms 1.19 to 1.32 on the eight wirings, with its PN totals at 1.12 and the ORN input's totals at 2.67); if that is flatter than the flies', two repairs stand open, a drive of APL by the KCs' spikes and a weaker normalization in the antennal lobe. And the test of where APL responds, against maps predicted from the connectome, waits: its predicted maps and the check of their power rest on connectome tables whose use is on hold, and what that check read is withheld (2026-11-15). Yang's dual-colour recordings hold APL (GCaMP6f) and the KCs' claws in the calyx (jRGECO1a) in the same fly and plane, with pa, paa and ms as concentration series: 20 processed recordings from 7 flies that she listed herself (2022-10-16 to 12-17), 23 from 5 development flies of 2022-07 to 09 with the three odors at exactly 10^-3, and flies of her own in which only one of the two cell types carried an indicator. Goal: find what the flies' APL follows, so that a flat APL in the model can be placed in its claws or in APL's drive, and test a local APL against the claws themselves, without a connectome. Design: no new recording first (the table of the two-channel recordings; the family's evoked APL against its summed claw stage over the panel, from saved runs; the fits of the eight wirings at fixed parameters on the extended input with the claw stage saved; what the place test can detect, on made-up maps); then, after Yang's answer on which recordings may be used and on the channels, the controls first (the leak of green into red as a slope in the green-only flies, checked from one such fly to the other; the reverse leak; a static red marker), with every reading of the KC channel made as recorded and with the leak removed; each fly's APL and claw responses by stimulus; the difference between two odors' APL responses at a matched response of the claws; three ratios (pa over ms and paa over ms near 10^-3, paa at 10^-5 over 10^-4) for the claws, for APL and for APL's ratio over the claws' ratio, against the model's through one family of indicator nonlinearities for each indicator; and, per stimulus, the odor-specific part of APL's map from two presentations against that of the claws' map from the other two, with a null from the claws' maps of other odors. Both channels are read; only recordings with Yang's processed maps are read; nothing is fitted, chosen or scored on the main target. Readout: whether APL is one function of the claws' response, to within a factor of 1.5 for all three pairs of odors; for the claws, for APL and for their ratio, whether the model lies inside the flies' interval, is flatter or is steeper on at least six of the eight wirings in at least two of the three ratios; whether at least 5 of the 7 flies show an agreement of the odor-specific parts of at least 0.3 and above the null with none, once and twice the controls' leak removed; each class counted only if the flies not probed in planning give it too; the development flies as a repetition; the claws' own odor-odor distances and their change with concentration, described. Prediction: the APL-effect loss does not move; APL is one function of the claws at about three in five; the family's claws are flatter than the flies' at about one in two, with its APL following its claws as the flies' APL does at about three in five, which would place the flat APL before APL; APL's calcium follows the claws place by place beyond the leak at about one in two, which would give a spatially local APL a premise that does not rest on a connectome.

102. **E102, the antennal lobe's normalization at the strength the flies' APL asks for (whether the best family can keep its fit to the per-pair APL effect with an antennal lobe that lets APL's response fall with concentration as it does in the flies, and what that does to the KCs at low concentrations).** Three readings of the flies' APL found the best family's APL too flat: from paa at 10^-4 to 10^-5 the flies' APL falls to 0.53 (0.46 to 0.61), and to 0.39 in shuffled-order recordings, where the family's falls to 0.86 to 0.90 on all eight wirings. In the family that flatness is made before APL: APL follows its claws, the claws follow the PN totals, and the model's antennal lobe makes those totals nearly equal over odors (coefficient of variation 0.074 against 0.247 in its own ORN input and 0.312 in the flies' PN dendrites). Of the changes read beside, only a weaker normalization of the PN stage moved the family's APL towards the flies' (0.78 and 0.70 on the real wiring); a drive of APL by the KCs' spikes did not. At fixed scales on the real wiring that normalization costs 0.004 to 0.017 of the pattern part, raises the mean APL effect on raw counts from +0.120 to +0.165 and +0.186 (the flies' +0.178) and brings the per-odor response rates and the PN totals nearer the flies'. Goal: measure what an antennal lobe that scales as the flies' APL asks costs or gains on the main target, on a population of wirings and with the family fitted again, and whether the flies' steep APL and their KCs' responses at weak input can be had together. Design: no simulations first (the chain of totals from the ORN input to APL in saved runs; the KCs' response rates at low concentration under the two saved weaker settings; the screen's points split into amplitude and shape); then the eight wirings at six strengths of the normalization with both response rates re-held, each read on the main target, on the ratios of APL's response that the three experiments measured, on the KCs' response rates at 10^-5 to 10^-7 and on the PN totals; the weakest change of the normalization at which paa at 10^-5 over 10^-4 is inside the flies' interval, and the one at which it is so read linearly, fixed by rules that do not use the main target; the family fitted again at those on the four searched wirings, and on the four held-out wirings only on an equal or better. The lab's input in every verdict run; no recording is read again. Readout: the pattern part with its amplitude and shape parts, the loss and the mean APL effect, against all recordings and halves, classed as an equal (within 0.003, inside the bands), better (0.003 lower, held on the held-out wirings) or a price; the ratios' classes by the rules of the APL-strength experiment; the response rates at low concentration against the flies'; the realism bands. Prediction: a price of 0.003 to 0.012 on the pattern part refitted (an equal at about one in six, better at about one in twenty), with the mean APL effect on raw counts at the flies'; the paa ratio inside only through the indicator's nonlinearity at about two in three; the KCs' responses at 10^-7 with APL off further from the flies' at about three in four, which would say that in this family the flies' steep APL and their recruitment at weak input pull the antennal lobe in opposite directions.
103. **E103, the flies' APL across odors in the held-out vial series (whether five flies that no rule was fixed on side with the 2021 or with the 2022 flies on how strongly APL responds to ms against paa and pa, whether the best family's APL agrees with them, and what two vials of one odor differ by).** The readings of the flies' APL disagree across odors: in the 12 recordings of 2021 APL responds to ms at 10^-3 about as strongly as to paa (paa at 10^-4 over ms at 10^-3 is 0.81, 0.67 to 0.96), in the 2022 recordings paa evokes two to three times as much as ms and pa three to five times, and the best family's APL gives 1.22 to 1.32 for paa over ms, so whether its APL is too even across odors depends on which flies are read; a weaker antennal-lobe normalization raises that ratio, toward one set of flies and away from the other. One series that Yang processed has not been read: 30 recordings of 5 flies from October and November 2021, with ms, paa and pa at three concentrations each, the 0.5 s pulse, planes at 15 and 18 µm in every calyx, and two further vials of pa or of paa at the same concentrations, her control for what the same odor from another vial gives. Goal: read that series as held-out flies, by rules fixed before any of it is opened, for which side it is on, for the family's APL against it, and for the size of the differences that are not the odor's. Design: no simulations; the eligibility table from the stimulus files first; six ratios that involve ms and two that do not, each against the 12 recordings by a permutation of flies between the two series; the comparison of paa over ms against the family's saved APL on the eight wirings by the rules of the APL-strength experiment and at the concentration the input was measured at; the ratio of two vials of one odor within a recording and the part of it that persists over a day's recordings; the odor-specific part of APL's map between two vials of one odor against two odors at the same block distance and against its repeat within a block; the same stimuli repeated 16 blocks apart, the presentations within a block, and paa over ms at 15 against 18 µm in eight calyces. Nothing is fitted, chosen or scored on the main target. Readout: the series' side (the 12's, the 2022 flies' or between), held under the baseline, the presentations read, the runs Yang's notes mark and each fly left out; the family inside, above or below the held-out flies' interval on at least six of eight wirings under each of five estimates of ms's input; the vials' yardstick; the share of the map's repeatable remainder that another vial of the same odor reproduces; the plane's sign over calyces. Prediction: the APL-effect loss does not move; the series sides with the 12 at about one in two and with the 2022 flies at about one in five; on the 12's side the family lies above the flies under every estimate of ms's input at about one in four, which would make its APL's preference for paa over ms a second held departure from the 2021 flies, opposite in direction to its departure from the 2022 flies.

104. **E104, the flies' KCs beside their APL along concentration (whether the steep fall of the flies' APL with concentration is in its claws' input or is APL's own, and whether the KCs' recruitment with and without APL at the weak odors agrees with it).** Background: the best family's APL falls less with concentration than the flies' APL calcium, and the repair in the antennal lobe costs on the per-pair APL effect and takes the KCs at 10^-7 further from the flies. Whether the flies' KC dendrites fall as their APL does has not been read without a leak between imaging channels. Goal: decide whether that fall is in the claws' input, in APL's own transfer, or only in APL's calcium signal, before any further change of the antennal lobe or of APL's drive is planned. Design, part A: no simulations. Yang's KC-only calyx recordings (GCaMP6f in the KCs, 55 processed recordings of 5 flies, not read before) against her 2022 APL flies on the same stimuli, by rules locked before any of their responses is opened; the family's claw stage against the KCs' signal; the weak end against the solvent. Design, part B: at fixed parameters on eight wirings, a ladder of APL strengths at three weak odors where the flies have APL's response and the KCs' response rates with and without APL. Nothing is fitted or scored on the main target. Readout: APL steeper than the KCs' calyx signal, one function, or not decided, held under seven partner readings; the APL strength that the flies' KCs imply, against the flies' APL read linearly and against the family's own, on at least six of eight wirings. Prediction: the APL-effect loss does not move. APL falls faster than the KCs' signal at about two in five. The flies' APL read linearly is too weak for what their KCs lose to APL at about three in five, which would leave the family's APL right in effect at the weak odors and the antennal lobe as it is.

105. **E105, the KCs' response rates at the weak odors read like for like (whether the best family's KCs respond to weak input less than the flies' once the flies' sets of recordings, the solvent's responders, the unit of a response and the three sessions' input estimates are read side by side).** Background: at ma at 10^-7 the best family has been read at a third of the flies' response rate, with APL on and with APL off, and two experiments leave "what recruits the flies' KCs at weak input" as a lead. That reading used the weakest of three sessions' estimates of the input, a control value pooled over two periods of recordings that differ, and response rates that include the solvent's responders. Goal: say whether the shortfall is the model's, the input estimate's or one set of flies', before a recruitment mechanism is planned, and read again what a weaker antennal-lobe normalization costs at low concentration. Design: no simulation for the verdicts. The weak-odor KC recordings as three sets that are never pooled, as evoked response rates in a unit shared with the APL16 recordings, against the saved runs of the eight wirings under each estimate, by classes fixed beforehand; the earlier ladder of normalization strengths and the earlier ratios with APL output blocked (TNT) read again on that reading. Beside, at fixed parameters: the input's size at which the family meets the flies, the family read as the recordings were made, and the trial noise. Nothing is fitted or scored on the main target. Readout: short, inside or over on at least six of eight wirings, per estimate, condition and set, held under six partner readings. Prediction: the APL-effect loss does not move. The verdict differs between the sets of flies at about one in two; the family is short under every estimate against every set at about one in twenty.

106. **E106, the gap in ρ on odor-odor distances read by what could close it (a diagnostic: where the best family's gap to the flies sits, and what "not worse" is measured against; no simulations).** Background: the best family's ρ on odor-odor distances stays well below its ceiling with APL on and with APL off, and the earlier decomposition of that gap read other models. Goal: tested tables of where the family stands and of what each later change could gain. Design: the two ρ's of every archived variant over all pairs and three subsets, beside its APL-effect statistics; the family's gap split by condition, by odor, and into an odor part and a pair part; the ORN input read the same way; the yardsticks as registry entries. Readout: whether the planning estimates hold on tested code. Prediction: no model changes, so neither ρ and no part of the APL-effect loss moves.

107. **E107, the four validation2 odors in the panel their ORN input was recorded in (which reading of the best family's gap at paa and B-myr holds; no model changes).** Background: in Yang's panel the best family holds paa and B-myr much closer than the control flies do, and these odors' ORN input comes from another session than the megamat odors'. Goal: decide whether the gap is paa's concentration, the family's KC stage, the ORN recordings or the joining of two ORN sessions. Design: Remy's validation2 KC soma recordings (20 wild-type flies, not read before) for the distance of paa from B-myr and against the validation2 ORN input alone; then the fits of the eight wirings at fixed parameters on that input against her matrix, with the megamat panel as the reference. Readout: one of four outcomes fixed beforehand. Prediction: no model changes, so neither ρ on odor-odor distances and no part of the APL-effect loss moves.

108. **E108, paa's input at the flies' concentration (whether ρ on odor-odor distances rises when paa's ORN input is moved from the recorded 10^-4 to the flies' 10^-3 by a rule that takes nothing from the KC data).** Background: paa's ORN input was recorded a decade below what the flies' KCs got, a caveat that every result carries. Goal: test that caveat, and say for this handle whether ρ on odor-odor distances can rise with no other measure of match getting worse. Design: a linear, a saturating and a ramp-fitted extrapolation from the lab's recordings at 10^-5 and 10^-4, fixed before any run, with wrong-way, permuted and scaled controls; each read in the fits of the eight wirings at fixed scales with both response rates re-held. Readout: better, a price or no gain, by a gate on the two ρ's, the APL-effect statistics, the realism bands and the response rates. Prediction, a planning estimate: ρ on odor-odor distances rises by 0.01 to 0.03 with APL on and by 0.02 to 0.04 with APL off (an improvement), and the APL-effect loss does not move.

109. **E109, the input of paa, B-myr and 2-but searched for ρ on odor-odor distances on held-out wirings and recordings (whether an ORN input within a factor of two of the lab's recordings raises the order of the odor pairs where the search did not look; an oracle on the input).** Background: about half of the best family's gap in ρ on odor-odor distances sits on the pairs of paa and B-myr, and Remy's wild-type flies hold the two apart at the concentration at which the ORN input holds them close. Goal: say whether such an input raises ρ on odor-odor distances with no other measure of match getting worse, and name what a new ORN recording should test. Design: the three odors' input searched on four wirings of the best family against one half of the recordings, six searches over two splits; each read at 0.6 of its size on the four other wirings against the unseen half, with permuted, inverted and control-odor controls. Readout: passes, a price, carries below the gate or does not carry, by a gate fixed beforehand. Prediction, a planning estimate: on the held-out wirings and recordings ρ on odor-odor distances rises by 0.01 to 0.03 with APL on and by 0.02 to 0.05 with APL off (an improvement on the family's 0.69 / 0.76; ceilings 0.98 / 0.97), and the pattern part of the APL-effect loss stays within 0.002 of the unchanged fits' 0.050 (no gain expected; floor 0.029).

110. **E110, KC-KC inhibition held at zero in the best family (APL-independent lateral inhibition removed).** Goal: to find what the model's KC-KC inhibition, the only inhibition that remains when APL is off, is needed for, and what removing it costs once the other parameters are tuned again. Design: on the eight wirings of the breadth-2 APL-effect fit, the KC-KC scale is set to 0, first at fixed scales with both response rates held, then with the somatic threshold, APL→KC and APL→bouton fitted again from that point; a return fit with KC-KC free, the breadth exponent at 1.5 and 3, and half the fitted KC-KC if a cost shows. Readout: the APL-effect loss with its mean, pattern, amplitude and shape parts on raw counts and under the calibrated readout, ρ on odor-odor distances with APL on and with APL off, the response rates, response rate ρ and odors per KC in both conditions, the block, each wiring paired with its fit and the eight as a population, against margins fixed beforehand (pattern part 0.003, raw-count loss 0.005, mean APL effect 0.01, each ρ 0.005). Prediction: the block is lost, the pattern part rises by 0.003 to 0.015 and the two ρ's fall by 0.01 to 0.04, so that KC-KC, or whatever inhibition survives the block of APL's output, is needed at a stated size; the alternative is that the threshold absorbs it and the family is simpler than fitted.

111. **E111, a residual APL output in the blocked flies in place of KC-KC inhibition (incomplete block of APL's output).** Goal: to find whether a fraction of APL's output left by tetanus toxin can do in the best family what KC-KC inhibition does, and what would tell the two apart. Design: on the eight wirings of the breadth-2 APL-effect fit with KC-KC at 0, the blocked condition is read with the APL→KC and APL→bouton scales at a fraction of their APL-on values instead of 0: as a dose from 0.01 to 0.2 at fixed scales, then with the fraction fitted beside the somatic threshold and the two APL scales, and with KC-KC and the fraction both free on four wirings. Readout: the APL-effect loss with its parts on raw counts and under the calibrated readout, ρ on odor-odor distances with APL on and with APL off, the response rates, response rate ρ and odors per KC in both conditions, the block, and each family's prediction for removing the residue in a fly, against the margins of the experiment with KC-KC held at zero. Prediction: a residue of a few percent recovers part of what removing KC-KC costs, and the recordings on hand do not tell the two apart, so that a GABA-receptor antagonist or APL ablation in the blocked flies is the deciding experiment; the alternative is that only inhibition among connected KCs carries the match.

112. **E112, ρ on odor-odor distances read as the recordings are made (the reading of the model's side, no model changed).** Goal: to find how much of the gap in ρ on odor-odor distances is the comparison of raw spike counts with noisy, per-cell z-scored calcium signals, and whether the gain of the searched input of paa, B-myr and 2-but stands once both sides are measured the same way. Design: the saved runs of the breadth-2 APL-effect fit on its eight wirings are read as raw counts, through the calibrated saturating readout, through the per-KC session z-score, through that reading with measurement noise on pseudo-recordings of three trials (11 with APL on, 8 with APL off, as in the flies) and through the threshold-jitter trial noise; the gap's bound and its odor part and pair part are read again under each, one half of the wirings against the other gives what a model that was the flies would score against itself through the noise, and the saved gates of the claw-share, paa-concentration and input-search experiments are re-scored from their archived counts; the trial noise is then simulated on the four held-out wirings and on two searched inputs and the two control searches. Readout: ρ on odor-odor distances with APL on and with APL off over the 120, 66, 54 and 91 pairs, per wiring and as populations, the distance of paa from B-myr, and the APL-effect loss with its mean, pattern, amplitude and shape parts under each reading, against a verdict fixed beforehand (a reading moves ρ if the population gains 0.020 in one condition on 6 of 8 wirings with the other not lower by more than 0.005; the searched input's gain holds if at least half of it remains with its controls below half). Prediction: read through the noise the family's two ρ's rise by 0.01 to 0.04 with APL on and 0.03 to 0.06 with APL off, as its hemibrain member did (0.664 / 0.697 on raw counts, 0.702 / 0.755 through the trials), so that part of the gap is the reading and which reading carries later gates is Tom's decision; the alternative is that raw counts stay the reading, or that the searched input's gain shrinks because part of the flies' separation of paa from B-myr is measurement noise.

113. **E113, the searched input of paa, B-myr and 2-but inside the ORN flies' spread, reduced and fitted again (cut to the input of paa and B-myr without a search: each ORN fly's own recording, and the three entries that two independent searches both named).** Goal: to find whether the gain in ρ on odor-odor distances that the searched input bought on held-out wirings is carried by the three entries on which the searches against Yang's flies and against Remy's agree (DM1 lowered for paa and for B-myr, DP1m lowered for paa), or lies inside what single ORN flies already show, with no search in either case. Design: on the eight wirings of the breadth-2 APL-effect fit with both response rates re-held, after an identity check against the held readings of E109 and E116, each of the six validation2 ORN flies' own paa alone and its paa and B-myr together as the input; then three fixed-entry arms with every other entry at the lab's recording: DM1 lowered for both odors by E109's mean factors, DM1 lowered for both by the factor-of-two bound, and DM1 for both odors with paa's DP1m at E116's mean factors, which owe nothing to Yang's recordings and carry the class; controls with the first arm's factors on the four glomeruli nearest DM1 in each odor's response and with DM1 raised by the same factors; the family fitted again on the four held-out wirings only with an input that passes. Readout: ρ on odor-odor distances with APL on and with APL off on the held-out wirings against all recordings and against each half, the pattern part with its amplitude and shape parts, the raw-count loss, the mean APL effect, the realism bands and response rate ρ in both conditions, by the input search's gate, a single fly's input being picked on one half of the wirings and recordings and read on the other, with the block as a column only, paa's distance from B-myr, and the entry arms on the validation2 ORN input against Remy's validation2 somata beside. Prediction: the entry arms gain 0.005 to 0.02 with APL on and 0.005 to 0.015 with APL off, most likely below the gate, and the best single fly's input up to 0.02 / 0.01 with as many flies below the family as above it, so that the searched gain belongs to a direction over many factors and not to a glomerulus or a fly, and the input route closes; the alternative is that the three entries pass at sizes found without Yang's recordings, which would make DM1 and DP1m the lead for the two odors' input. *(Cut on 2026-11-20 from the design approved on 2026-11-19, after E116 came out below its gate with entries that agree with E109's no better than chance; the searches inside the flies' spread, the penalty on response rate ρ and the refit with E109's input are held and not run.)*

114. **E114, a compressive map from the ORN recordings to the input rates (an exponent below 1 on every odor's input).** Goal: to test whether a one-parameter change of how the ORN recordings become input rates, with no KC data in its form, raises ρ on odor-odor distances without a cost to the APL effect. Design: on the eight wirings of the breadth-2 APL-effect fit, each positive ORN response is raised to the power 0.6, 0.7, 0.8 or 0.9 and rescaled to the odor's sum, with 1.15 as the wrong-way control and a saturating form at three ceilings beside, at fixed scales with both response rates re-held; the family is then fitted again on all eight wirings at the best exponent, and on a pass the map is read on the validation2 ORN input against Remy's validation2 somata and on top of the reduced searched input. Readout: ρ on odor-odor distances with APL on and with APL off for the population and per wiring, against all recordings and each half, the pattern part under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts, the raw-count loss, the mean APL effect, the realism bands and response rate ρ, by the gate of the paa-concentration experiment, with the PN totals and bouton-stage distances against the stage recordings beside. Prediction: a gain of 0.00 to 0.02 with APL on and 0.01 to 0.03 with APL off, with response rate ρ with APL on higher, as on the hemibrain wiring at 0.75 (0.664 / 0.696 to 0.674 / 0.720; response rate ρ 0.66 to 0.73), at a cost of about 0.004 on the calibrated pattern part that a refit is expected to remove; the alternative is that the gain is the hemibrain wiring's own or that the two goals trade off through this handle.

115. **E115, two yardsticks the gates on ρ on odor-odor distances lack (the megamat gap against a second set of flies and the spread of response rate ρ under control input changes, no simulations).** Goal: to find how much of the best family's megamat-level gap is a difference between Yang's flies and Remy's wild-type flies that the model shares with her flies, and whether the price in response rate ρ of the searched input of paa, B-myr and 2-but is more than any input change costs. Design: no model is changed; on the 66 megamat pairs the family, its eight wirings and the ORN input are read against Yang's flies, against Remy's megamat somata and against the two sets' mean rank, the family's rank residuals against Yang's flies are correlated with her flies' rank residuals against Yang's, with recordings, flies and wirings resampled together, and each pair is classed by a rule fixed beforehand as one on which both sets stand against the family, one on which Yang's flies stand alone, or neither; the same is read for the validation2 odors' pairs her validation2 panel holds and for the saved candidates of the input-search, paa-concentration, claw-share and antennal-lobe experiments; then, from the archives, every control input change at fixed scales with both response rates re-held gives the change in each wiring's response rate ρ, its paired spread per wiring, for means over four and eight wirings and with the response fractions pooled, and the searched inputs are placed in that distribution. Readout: ρ on odor-odor distances with APL on and with APL off against each set of flies and against both, the residual correlation with its 5th to 95th percentile, the shares of the gap by class and odor, and the control changes' spread of response rate ρ with APL on and off, against a verdict fixed beforehand (shared if the residual correlation is 0.30 or more in both conditions with its 5th percentile above 0; the megamat level is the flies' if the family is within 0.03 of Remy's flies against Yang's; the price is inside the controls if it lies within their 5th to 95th percentile in each direction and the pooled reading falls by no more than 0.02); the APL-effect loss and its parts are unchanged by construction. Prediction: the errors are partly shared (planning probes: residual correlation +0.43 / +0.37; the family at 0.824 / 0.806 against Remy's flies, where her flies have 0.847 / 0.867 against Yang's) and the price lies inside the controls' spread (0.015 to 0.054 per wiring over 24 permuted controls, against a margin of 0.05), so that the megamat room is a few hundredths and the decision on the price has its numbers; the alternative is that the megamat gap is the model's own, or that the price belongs to the searched input.

116. **E116, an input of paa and B-myr that owes nothing to Yang's KC recordings (searched against a second set of flies, and Hallem's measured paa).** Goal: to test whether an input of paa and B-myr found without any of Yang's recordings raises ρ on odor-odor distances against her flies, and whether two sets of flies ask for the same glomeruli. Design: first, with no search, Hallem's paa on the 16 shared glomeruli as measured, rescaled to the lab's level, and at DM6 alone, on the eight wirings of the breadth-2 APL-effect fit with both response rates re-held, with permuted controls; then the input search's method on four wirings with paa's and B-myr's 36 responses free within a factor of two, its objective ρ on odor-odor distances over the 136 pairs of Remy's validation2 panel against one half of her wild-type flies, with model-only penalties so that the search reads no recording of Yang's; four searches over two splits of her flies; each input at 0.6 of its size and at full size on the four held-out wirings, read against all of Yang's recordings, each half of them and Remy's unseen half; controls with the factors permuted and inverted; the entries compared with those of the search against Yang's flies. Readout: ρ on odor-odor distances with APL on and with APL off on the held-out wirings, the pattern part under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts, the raw-count loss, the mean APL effect, the realism bands and response rate ρ in both conditions, by the input search's gate, with the block as a column only, the correlation and sign agreement of the entries between the two searches against a permutation null, and each entry in the ORN flies' standard deviations. Prediction: the input searched against Remy's flies gains 0.015 to 0.035 with APL on and 0.010 to 0.030 with APL off against Yang's flies (from 0.685 / 0.762), since the transfer held in the other direction (0.689 to 0.717 to 0.737 against her somata), and Hallem's paa gains 0.00 to 0.01 / 0.01 to 0.03, as on the hemibrain wiring (0.664 / 0.696 to 0.669 / 0.721); the alternative is that the change is one set of flies' own, or that the gain is the separation of the two odors by whatever entries reach it. paa's input is the 10^-4 recording, what Remy's flies got and a stand-in for Yang's flies' 10^-3; Hallem's paa is at 10^-2.

117. **E117, the megamat odors' input searched for ρ on odor-odor distances (an oracle input with independent measurements to check it).** Goal: to find whether the input of the megamat odors, searched as the input of paa, B-myr and 2-but was, raises ρ on odor-odor distances on held-out wirings and recordings at no cost to the APL effect, and whether the entries it finds agree with the PN dendrite recordings and Hallem's data. Design: on the wirings, splits and held reading of the input search, eb, ms, 1-8ol and 2h with each of their 36 responses free within a factor of two (six searches), and all twelve megamat odors with each input a weighted mean of the nine ORN flies' own vectors (four searches); controls with the factors permuted, inverted, and the same search for pa and IaA, the megamat odors with the smallest shares of the gap; if the megamat errors prove shared between the two sets of flies, the objective counts only the pairs on which both sets stand against the family; the recurring entries are read against the PN dendrites' departure from the ORNs, against Hallem's values and in the ORN flies' standard deviations. Readout: ρ on odor-odor distances with APL on and with APL off on the held-out wirings against the unseen half of the recordings, the pattern part with its amplitude and shape parts, the raw-count loss, the mean APL effect, the realism bands and response rate ρ in both conditions, by the input search's gate, with the block as a column only, each input against Remy's megamat somata beside, and the best input together with the searched input of paa, B-myr and 2-but. Prediction: the four odors gain 0.015 to 0.035 with APL on and 0.010 to 0.030 with APL off, since the search of 2h and 6al, run as a control, gained 0.019 / 0.016, the search inside the flies' spread 0.005 to 0.020 / 0.005 to 0.015, and the two searched inputs together add within 0.01; the alternative is that any odor's input can be searched for about as much, so that the gain is the looseness of the input as a whole, or that the megamat input trades the pattern part against ρ. *(Returned to the open proposals on 2026-11-19 without being run, after the decision not to start new searches of many free input factors.)*

118. **E118, the KC types in the best family and in the recordings (a model property with published recordings to hold it against, and an unknown of the comparison).** Goal: to find whether the best family's three KC types respond as published recordings say they do, how far the unknown type composition of the recorded somata could move ρ on odor-odor distances, and whether holding the types' response rates at measured ratios raises it at no cost to the APL effect. Design: first, with no simulations, the saved runs of the breadth-2 APL-effect fit on its eight wirings read by KC type and with the γ KCs weighted from one half to eight times, with type labels permuted among KCs of equal claw count as the control and published per-type response rates beside; then, if the family's ratios are outside the published range or the composition moves ρ beyond its control, per-type factors on the somatic threshold that hold the types' response rates with APL on at the published ratios, at equal rates and half-way, on the eight wirings at fixed scales with both response rates re-held, with permuted and wrong-way controls, and the family fitted again on a gain or an equal. Readout: ρ on odor-odor distances with APL on and with APL off, the distance of paa from B-myr, the APL-effect loss with its mean part, its pattern part and that part's amplitude and shape parts, the response rates by type and the realism bands. Prediction: the composition reading gains about one to two hundredths; the model change is most likely an equal; the family's γ KCs, at about four tenths of the αβ KCs' response rate with APL on, may prove outside what recordings show.

119. **E119, the model's boutons, claws and claw-to-soma step against the per-ROI recordings (a check of the model's internal stages against single imaged regions, with no model change).** Goal: to find whether the two-signed core of the error common to all wirings is made before the KC claws, at the claws or at the step from claws to somata, and whether the best family's PN boutons and KC claws respond as the flies' do in strength, breadth and spread of amplitudes. Design: with no simulations, the saved stage runs of the breadth-2 APL-effect fit on its eight wirings against the responses of every imaged region at the PN boutons and the KC claws of wild-type flies, in baseline-noise units with the model's overall response rate matched to the flies'; each of the nine earlier readings of a claw's signal kept or dropped on the regions' own statistics; and the step from claws to somata in one fly recorded at both on the same trials, against the step read between flies and the model's. Readout: per stage, the spread of the odors' totals, the per-odor response rates, the number of odors per region, the spread of amplitudes and the share of responses below baseline, each against the flies' own range; cosine and Pearson distances by group of pairs with the trial noise removed; the realism bands as they stand. Prediction: no number of the APL-effect loss moves; the yield is a verdict per stage and at most one measured change to test afterwards.

120. **E120, the bouton stage held to the recorded boutons (a measured setting of the antennal lobe's normalization, read first on saved runs).** Goal: to find whether a setting of the model that can be fixed from recordings of the PN boutons, and not fitted to the KC data, brings the model's boutons to the flies' in how unevenly the odors drive them and how widely the amplitudes spread, and what the best family's match to the APL effect owes to a bouton stage unlike the flies'. Design: with no simulations first, the per-region bouton statistics of wild-type flies read on the saved runs of the breadth-2 APL-effect fit through each member of the family of indicator curves, along the ladder of weaker antennal-lobe normalizations, along the doses of APL's inhibition of the boutons and under the compressive maps of the input; then, at most, the family fitted again on four wirings at the one normalization strength the boutons ask for, or at the weakest of the ladder if none reaches them, with the bouton stage of each refit saved. Readout: the spread of the odors' bouton totals, the spread of amplitudes, the top 1 % share and the share of responses below baseline against the flies' own ranges; the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts, the mean APL effect, the loss on raw counts, the two ρ's on odor-odor distances and the realism bands. Prediction: no saved setting reaches the flies' boutons, and the refit comes at a small price on the pattern part (+0.002 to +0.006), in the shape part.

121. **E121, the rise of the input by odor and by glomerulus (the onset race: which KCs fire before APL has settled, read first on saved spike times).** Goal: to find whether the order in which glomeruli and odors rise at the onset, which the model gives one 20 ms rise for all, decides which KCs respond with APL on, and whether a measured rise per odor brings the pattern of the APL effect closer to the flies'. Design: with no simulations first, when each responding KC's second spike falls against APL's rise in the saved spike times of the breadth-2 APL-effect fit; then, with a rise time constant per glomerulus and odor in the ORN stage (off by default), a sensitivity probe on the hemibrain wiring at fixed scales with both response rates re-held (one slower rise for all, random rises per glomerulus, a rise that shortens with the glomerulus's drive and its inverse); a measured arm on eight wirings with each odor's rise taken from the megamat bouton time series, with the rises permuted over odors as controls; one refit on four wirings only on a pass; no rise time is searched or fitted to the KC data. Readout: the share of APL-on responses complete before APL reaches 90 % of its plateau, by ester and alcohol group and by the KC's breadth; the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts, the mean APL effect, the loss on raw counts, the two ρ's on odor-odor distances and the realism bands. Prediction: there is a race, and the measured arm lowers the pattern part by 0.002 to 0.006, in the shape part, with about a one-in-three chance of passing its gate.

122. **E122, one feedback neuron that survives the block in place of KC–KC inhibition (a rank-1 KC–KC matrix: one pooled neuron, one per KC type, or a γ-targeted one).** Goal: to find whether the second inhibition that the best family needs with APL on, which it now takes from the pairwise KC–KC matrix and which a residual APL output did not supply, can be carried by a single neuron that pools KC spikes and inhibits KCs in both conditions, and whether a γ-targeted one does better than the matrix. Design: with no simulations first, how much of the fitted matrix's weight lies within KC type and how well each KC's received KC–KC inhibition follows its type's pooled activity; then, with the matrix replaced by a rank-1 matrix of matched total incoming weight, three arms at fixed scales on four wirings with both response rates re-held (one uniform neuron, one neuron per type, all KCs in and γ KCs out), with the hemibrain matrix shuffled within each type pair and the γ arm's output moved to αβ as controls; refits of the threshold, both APL scales and the neuron's scale for each arm that keeps at least half of KC–KC's advantage, read on the four other wirings; no change to the model's C++. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts, against the family and against the family without KC–KC; the mean APL effect, the γ KCs' response rates and their APL-off / APL-on ratio, the two ρ's on odor-odor distances, the fitted threshold and the realism bands. Prediction: the pooled arms recover none to most of what KC–KC buys (0.050 to 0.062 against the family's 0.048 and 0.062 without KC–KC), and the γ-targeted arm has about a one-in-five chance of coming out below the family.

123. **E123, the form of the inhibition among KCs (which property of the KC–KC matrix the best family uses: random sparse partners, each KC's amount, or inhibition that follows APL's block).** Goal: to find what the second inhibition of the best family has to be, after a matrix shuffled within type pairs matched the hemibrain's and no pooled neuron did: whether that result holds over draws of the shuffle, whether a few equal random partners per KC are enough, whether each KC's own amount adds anything, and whether the inhibition has to survive the block of APL's output at all. Design: at fixed scales on all eight wirings, each at its own fit, with the arm's matrix at the fit's KC–KC scale and matched total weight and both response rates re-held: the shuffle within type pairs with three more seeds; a shuffle that keeps each KC's row and amount and redraws its partners; random matrices with 4, 16, 64 and 256 equal partners per KC; and the hemibrain matrix and the first shuffle with the KC–KC scale at zero in the APL-off condition only, which is inhibition carried by APL; the family, the family without KC–KC and one uniform neuron as references; then at most two sets of refits on all eight wirings, of the simplest random arm and of the APL-borne arm, only if classed equal to the family by rules fixed beforehand; no change to the model's C++. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts, on the searched and the held-out wirings apart and against halves of the recordings; the mean APL effect and the loss on raw counts; the response rate ρ of every arm beside the spread over draws; the γ KCs' response rates, the threshold and the realism bands. Prediction: no arm comes out below the family by more than 0.003 on the pattern part (the family 0.050 and 0.049; 0.061 and 0.067 without KC–KC); the experiment says which property buys that difference, and the APL-borne arm should raise the mean APL effect on raw counts from +0.12 toward +0.15 (the flies' +0.178), with its pattern part uncertain by 0.005 either way.

124. **E124, the inhibition among KCs as a part of APL, read off its corner (a KC–KC scale for each condition: present with APL on, absent with APL off, held and fitted).** Goal: to find whether the second inhibition of the best family has to outlast the block of APL's output, after the matrix present only with APL on recovered 43 % and 61 % of the gain at fixed scales with APL at its lower bound, and to find what equal random partners lack against the shuffled matrices, the hemibrain matrix's weights being nearly all one value. Design: at fixed scales on all eight wirings with both response rates re-held, the hemibrain matrix with the KC–KC scale of the APL-off condition at 0.25, 0.5 and 0.75 of the fitted scale; then a fit on each of the eight wirings of the threshold, the tied APL scales, the APL→bouton factor and a KC–KC scale that acts with APL on and is zero with APL off, started from the held point of the form experiment, and the same fit with the first shuffle only if the first keeps the gain; beside them, at fixed scales, random matrices of 16 equal partners per KC drawn within the hemibrain's totals between KC types and further draws of the 4- and 16-partner equal matrices; the family, the family without KC–KC at fixed scales and fitted, and the earlier arms as references read from saved runs; classes fixed beforehand, a fit that ends at a bound reported as a corner without a class; no change to the model's C++. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts and the spread ratio, on the searched and the held-out wirings apart and against halves of the recordings; the mean APL effect and the loss on raw counts; the fitted threshold and APL→KC scale beside the family's, the γ KCs' response rates and the realism bands. Prediction: the fit comes out between the family and the family without KC–KC (0.050 to 0.056 against 0.048 and 0.062; the same arm at fixed scales 0.057), the difference in the shape part and the spread; the mean APL effect on raw counts may rise from +0.12 toward +0.15 (the flies' +0.178), uncertain by 0.015; the type-structured and further equal draws move nothing by more than 0.003.

125. **E125, what has to outlast the block (one pooled neuron or a KC's own feedback as the surviving part of the inhibition among KCs, with the sparse part following APL).** Goal: to find whether the part of the best family's second inhibition that has to remain with APL's output blocked must be sparse and specific to pairs of KCs, or only sets a level, after the gain returned by degrees (43 %, 58 %, 65 % and 83 %) with none, a quarter, a half and three quarters of the hemibrain KC–KC matrix left with APL off, the amplitude part falling along that ladder while the shape part stayed. Design: at fixed scales on all eight wirings with both response rates re-held and nothing fitted, the hemibrain matrix at 1 − d of its fitted scale present with APL on only, and a survivor at d of its total weight present in both conditions: one neuron that pools all KCs, or each KC inhibited by its own spikes at its type's mean amount, each at d of 0.25, 0.5 and 0.75, and one neuron per KC type at d of 0.5 (56 runs); the family, the family without KC–KC, the ladder with the hemibrain matrix as the survivor and the uniform neuron in place of the whole matrix as references read from saved runs; before any run, a check that d of 0 reproduces the earlier arm and that a diagonal matrix alone changes a KC's spike counts; classes fixed beforehand, an arm with the threshold or APL at a bound on more than two wirings reported as a corner without a class; no change to the model's C++ expected. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts and the spread ratio, on the searched and the held-out wirings apart and against halves of the recordings, each arm beside the ladder's rung at the same d; the mean APL effect and the loss on raw counts; each arm's threshold and APL→KC scale beside the family's and the ladder's, the γ KCs' response rates and the realism bands. Prediction: no arm comes out below the family (0.048; the flies' floor 0.029); the arms fall between 0.050 and 0.056, against 0.053 for the hemibrain matrix surviving at half its scale and 0.057 with nothing surviving, the difference in the spread; a pooled survivor has about an even chance of keeping the gain, with no estimate for a KC's own feedback; the mean APL effect stays within 0.01 of the family's (+0.178 under the readout, the flies' +0.178).

126. **E126, the ladder of the pooled survivor (one neuron per KC type as the part of the inhibition among KCs that outlasts the block, at a quarter and three quarters of the matrix's weight, and KC groups drawn without regard to type).** Goal: to find whether the one positive arm of the survivor experiment, one neuron per KC type carrying half of the hemibrain KC–KC matrix's weight in both conditions while the sparse part follows APL, is one point or a ladder, and whether the KC types matter in it, after that arm, the neuron that pools all KCs and the hemibrain matrix surviving at half its scale came out within 0.0011 of one another on all eight wirings and the pooled neuron at three quarters failed on the searched wirings with thresholds of 15 to 40 Hz on three wirings. Design: at fixed scales on all eight wirings with both response rates re-held and nothing fitted, the hemibrain matrix at 1 − d of its fitted scale present with APL on only and one neuron per KC type at d of its total weight present in both conditions, at d of 0.25 and 0.75 (16 runs); then, only if the arm at 0.75 keeps the gain with no threshold under 60 Hz on any wiring, one neuron per group with the KCs dealt into groups at random and the groups' sizes those of the types, in two draws at d of 0.75 (16 runs); the family, the family without KC–KC, the ladder with the hemibrain matrix as the survivor and the survivor experiment's arms as references read from saved runs; classes fixed beforehand, two forms said to differ only if one is lower on at least seven of the eight wirings and on both populations against every target, an arm with the threshold or APL at a bound on more than two wirings reported as a corner without a class; no change to the model's C++ expected. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts and the spread ratio, on the searched and the held-out wirings apart and against halves of the recordings, each arm beside the ladder's rung at the same d and with its share of the gain kept over all eight wirings; the mean APL effect and the loss on raw counts; each arm's threshold and APL→KC scale beside the family's, the ladder's and the survivor experiment's, the γ KCs' response rates and the realism bands. Prediction: no arm comes out below the family (0.048; the flies' floor 0.029); the neuron per type falls at 0.053 to 0.056 at d of 0.25 and at 0.050 to 0.054 at 0.75, against 0.0544 and 0.0507 for the hemibrain matrix surviving at those shares and 0.0544 and 0.0528 for the pooled neuron, the difference in the spread and none in the shape part; the mean APL effect under the readout stays at +0.19 to +0.20, above the flies' +0.178.

127. **E127, an expansive output at the PN boutons (the boutons' output raised to an exponent above one, with the exponent set by the recorded boutons and not by the KC data).** Goal: to find whether an output at the PN boutons that grows faster than the PN's rate, placed before APL's bouton site and before APL's drive, brings the model's boutons, which are more even across odors than the flies' (per-odor totals with a CV of 0.103 against the flies' 0.357 to 0.412), and its APL, which is flatter along concentration than the flies', to the recordings, and what that does to the pattern of the APL effect. Design: with no simulations first, on the saved PN and bouton stages of the breadth-2 APL-effect fit on its eight wirings, bouton activity r_ref · (r / r_ref)^q for q from 1 to 3, read against the per-ROI bouton recordings and against the flies' two APL ratios along concentration through the model's claw-to-APL relation, which fixes q; then, with the exponent and its reference rate as an option of the bouton stage (off by default), eight wirings at fixed scales at that q and one step either side with both response rates re-held; one refit on the four searched wirings at that q, read on the four held-out wirings; the experiment closes without code in the model if no q up to 3 brings the totals' CV inside the flies' range on 6 of 8 wirings without taking an amplitude statistic beyond the flies'; no exponent is searched or fitted to the KC data. Readout: for each q the bouton totals' CV, the responders' CV and the top-1 % share against the flies' ranges, and APL's ratios along concentration; the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts and the spread ratio, the mean APL effect, the loss on raw counts, the two ρ's on odor-odor distances and the realism bands; the per-odor response fractions of ms and B-myr, the KCs' response rates at the weak odors, bouton rates above 300 Hz and APL's ratio of paa over ms. Prediction: an exponent between 1 and 3 brings the totals' CV inside the flies' range; after the refit the pattern part moves by −0.004 to +0.006 against the family's 0.048 (the flies' floor 0.029), the amplitude part falling from 0.009 toward 0.003 as the spread ratio rises from 0.84 and the shape part going either way; the mean APL effect on raw counts rises from +0.124 by 0.01 to 0.03 toward the flies' +0.178.

128. **E128, the flies' bouton totals by tuning group (whether a few groups of boutons make the recorded PN boutons uneven across odors, and whether the flies' sample of boutons can account for the model's even ones).** Goal: to test the one reading left of the model's boutons being a third as uneven across odors as the flies' (per-odor totals with a CV of 0.103 against the flies' 0.357 to 0.412), that a few dominant glomeruli among the imaged boutons make the flies' totals uneven, after the normalization, the dose, the indicator, a compressive input map and an expansive output had each failed to close the difference; no model is changed and the APL-effect loss is not moved. Design: with no simulations, on the per-ROI bouton recordings of five flies grouped by tuning (36 to 70 groups per fly), each fly's totals' CV as measured, with every group at equal weight and with the 1, 2, 3 and 5 groups that carry most of the totals' variance across odors left out, against ROIs dealt at random into groups of the same sizes (200 deals per fly) and against groups made from two of the three repeats with the totals read on the third; then, on the saved bouton stages of the breadth-2 APL-effect fit on its eight wirings with APL on, each fly's group sizes dealt at random onto the model's 28 shared glomeruli (1000 deals per fly and wiring), beside the model with its glomeruli at equal weight; two classes fixed beforehand, with half-way at a CV of 0.230; nothing is searched or fitted, and the glomeruli of the deals that pass are leads only. Readout: per fly the totals' CV under each weighting and each group's share of the totals' variance, with the null's distribution; per wiring the share of deals whose totals' CV is inside the flies' range with neither the responders' CV nor the top-1 % share above the flies'; the family's realism bands as reported before. Prediction: none is made between the outcomes; if a few dominant glomeruli are the reason, equal weights or leaving out up to three groups bring the totals' CV below 0.230 in 4 of 5 flies where random groups do not, and at least 5 % of the deals bring the model's totals inside the flies' range on 6 of 8 wirings, in which case the even boutons are a difference of sampling and not established as the model's; if the unevenness is spread over the groups, that reading is closed and the model's input stage stays a stated departure.

129. **E129, the inhibition that outlasts the block reduced to a static level (in the blocked condition only, the family's inhibition among KCs replaced by its time average, a constant per KC, a constant per KC type and one constant for all KCs, which is a threshold difference between the genotypes).** Goal: to find whether what has to outlast the block of APL's output in the breadth-2 APL-effect fit has to be an inhibition among KCs at all, or only a level, after every surviving part tried so far (the hemibrain matrix, a neuron that pools all KCs, one neuron per KC type, a KC's own feedback) followed the KCs' spikes with 50 ms, and after the fit without KC–KC had to raise the one threshold that serves both conditions from 100 to 211 Hz; it bears on the question of how a pooled inhibition per KC type that does not depend on APL could be realized. Design: at fixed scales on all eight wirings with nothing fitted and no size free, the APL-on run the family's saved run and the common threshold the fit's, so that only the blocked condition changes: there the KC–KC matrix is replaced by the arm's inhibition, with one scale bisected to hold the family's response rate with APL off (0.150); first, with no new simulation, the family's APL-on runs paired with the saved APL-off runs of the family without KC–KC, and each KC's KC–KC input per odor split into a value per KC, per KC type, per odor and the rest; then each KC's and odor's time-mean of the family's own KC–KC input as a static inhibition, one constant for all KCs and odors, a pooled neuron filtered with 50 ms and the hemibrain matrix filtered with 1 s (32 runs of the one condition); a constant per KC and a constant per KC type only if the two ends fall in different classes (16 runs, and 8 with the per-KC constants permuted within type if those two differ); only if the one constant at least keeps the gain, its run with the level removed, as the reading for an acute block against a three-day one (8 runs), and the matrix at a half and at none of its fitted scale with APL on, the tied APL scales re-bisected (16 runs); one neuron with MB-C1's counted synapses in the matrix's place only if the use of those connectome tables is confirmed; classes fixed beforehand ("equal" within 0.003 of the family on both populations and on 6 of 8 wirings against every target with every run in the bands, "keeps the gain" at or below half-way to the family without KC–KC), two arms said to differ only if one is lower on at least seven of the eight wirings and on both populations against every target, an arm with its scale at a bound on more than two wirings reported as a corner without a class; no change to the model's C++. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts, the spread ratio and Pearson r on the per-pair APL effect, on the searched and the held-out wirings apart and against halves of the recordings; the mean APL effect and the loss on raw counts; the static level in Hz and as a fraction of each KC's threshold, the response rates by KC type with APL off, the largest spike counts with APL off and the realism bands. Prediction: no arm comes out below the family (0.048; the flies' floor 0.029); the time-averaged inhibition is within 0.003 of the family at about two chances in three and the one constant at about one in two, otherwise at 0.051 to 0.056 with the loss first in the amplitude part (the spread ratio falling from 0.84 toward 0.65), against 0.057 for the matrix present with APL on only and 0.063 for the family without KC–KC; the mean APL effect under the readout stays within 0.02 of the flies' +0.178, and its direction on raw counts (the family +0.124) is not predicted.

130. **E130, the best family on the male CNS's two calyces (a second fly's wiring, APL synapses and claw-to-claw synapses among KCs in place of the hemibrain's).** Goal: to find whether the breadth-2 APL-effect fit, the error common to its eight wirings and its need for an inhibition among KCs are the hemibrain's, since the seven re-drawn wirings and the wirings given the male CNS's claw proportions all kept every hemibrain KC's type and number of claws, while the male CNS's γ KCs carry 6.4 claws on average against the hemibrain's 7.9 and the shared responders that the family's APL removes are broad, many-clawed and often γ KCs; and to read the family under the loader's new default for claws spread over several boutons, which the male CNS wiring requires. Design: with no simulations first, the three calyces (the hemibrain's right, the male CNS's left and right) side by side: KCs, claws and boutons by type, APL's counted synapses and the claw-to-claw matrix among the modelled KCs; then at fixed scales the hemibrain fit's factors on each calyx's own base with both response rates re-held, with the calyx's own matrix at the fit's KC–KC scale and at zero (6 runs); then the family fitted on each of the three calyces as it was on the re-drawn wirings, and on the two male calyces with KC–KC held at zero (5 fits); the three real calyces read as one population against the 21 populations of three of the family's eight wirings that contain the hemibrain wiring; classes fixed beforehand for the new default, for each male calyx, for the population and for the replication of the gain from KC–KC; nothing is searched and no parameter is added; no change to the model's code, the male CNS wiring being in the model since 2026-11-20 and its claw-to-claw matrix passed as the earlier custom matrices were; only the hemibrain and male CNS tables curated by Pratyush. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts, the spread ratio and Pearson r on the per-pair APL effect, for each calyx beside the eight wirings and for the population of three, against all recordings and halves of them; the cost of KC–KC at zero on each male calyx; the errors of the ester and the alcohol pairs; response rates by KC type and γ's ratio of APL off to APL on; each fit's threshold, APL scales and KC–KC scale, and the realism bands. Prediction: each male calyx at its own fit falls inside the eight wirings' range (0.047 to 0.064 one wiring at a time; the family's population 0.048; the flies' floor 0.029); the three real calyces as one population come out lower than every population of three by 0.003 or more at about one chance in five, the gain in the shape part; the mean APL effect under the readout is held near the flies' +0.178 by the fit; no prediction is made for the new default or for whether the gain from KC–KC replicates.

131. **E131, MB-C1's two cells beside the inhibition among KCs (a second calyx feedback neuron, which the block of APL's output spares, run as cells of its own with their counted synapses next to the KC–KC matrix).** Goal: to find what MB-C1 does in the breadth-2 APL-effect fit at sizes around the weight of its matrix of inhibition among KCs, present with APL on and off because the toxin is expressed in APL, after the two tests so far had made it carry everything, a γ-directed stand-in at the matrix's whole weight and MB-C1's counted synapses as the only inhibition among KCs in the blocked condition at 18 times that weight, and both had silenced the γ KCs; MB-C1 takes 0.79 of its KC input from γ KCs and gives 0.83 of its KC output onto them, the family's APL nearly silences γ and the block releases γ 3.9-fold against 2.2-fold overall, and its output synapses onto the modelled KCs are 0.40 of the synapses among those KCs (corrected 2026-11-23 from 0.16, which was its input from those KCs). Design: at fixed scales on all eight wirings with both response rates re-held and nothing searched, MB-C1 as its two cells, linear with 50 ms, with all of each KC's calyx synapses; first, with no simulation, each cell's synapses by KC type and its activity per odor in the family's saved runs, which fixes one threshold and one scale; then MB-C1 beside the matrix at a quarter of, once and four times the matrix's weight (24 runs); in half of the matrix's place, weight for weight, with the half matrix alone as the reference (16 runs); as the surviving part of the earlier split, with the matrix at half its scale with APL on only (8 runs); and, beside the matrix at once its weight, with a time constant of 1 s and with a threshold above anything the cells see with APL intact (16 runs), the two cells pooled as one only if their activities differ; the family fitted again on the four searched wirings with MB-C1's scale as a fifth free parameter and read on the four held-out wirings, and MB-C1's counts permuted within KC type as the control, only if an arm comes out below the family, stands in for half the matrix or keeps the gain; classes fixed beforehand ("equal" within 0.003 of the family on both populations and on 6 of 8 wirings against every target with every run in the bands, "below" by 0.003 on the same, "keeps the gain" at or below half-way to the family without KC–KC), two arms said to differ only if one is lower on at least seven of the eight wirings and on both populations against every target; no change to the model's code beyond the MB-C1 option added on 2026-11-23; only the hemibrain tables curated by Pratyush, his MB-C1 tables among them. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts, the spread ratio and Pearson r on the per-pair APL effect, on the searched and the held-out wirings apart and against halves of the recordings; the mean APL effect and the loss on raw counts; response rates by KC type and γ's ratio of APL off to APL on; each cell's activity, MB-C1's share of the inhibition a γ KC receives beside its share of the inhibitory synapses onto γ, the thresholds, the largest spike counts and the realism bands. Prediction: no arm comes out below the family (0.048; the flies' floor 0.029), at about seven chances in eight; beside the matrix MB-C1 is within 0.002 of the family at a quarter of the matrix's weight and higher by 0.002 to 0.015 at once and four times it, the rise in the shape part and the spread widening past the flies'; the mean APL effect under the readout rises above the flies' +0.178 by up to 0.05, and on raw counts (the family +0.124) rises toward it; the largest dose is expected to leave the odors-per-KC band with APL off.

132. **E132, recurrent excitation among KCs with a pooled inhibition (the hemibrain's synapse counts between KC claws run with the excitatory sign of their transmitter, in place of the inhibitory sign the model has always given them).** Goal: to find what the breadth-2 APL-effect fit does when its synapses among KCs excite, since KCs release acetylcholine, the model's code refuses a negative weight so that the sign had never been run, and every other carrier tried for the fit's second inhibition (a residual APL output, a neuron that pools all KCs in the whole matrix's place, a KC's own feedback, a static level, MB-C1 as wired) had been closed, leaving the matrix of synapse counts with an inhibitory sign as its only form; and to learn what lies on the other side of zero, near which fits started there had stayed. Design: with no simulation first, the saved runs along the inhibitory side of the same axis (the fit, half the matrix, none) and where the excitation would land by groups of odor pairs; then, with no change to the model's code, at fixed scales on all eight wirings, a neuron that pools all KCs in the matrix's place at the fit's threshold, its scale set by the response rate with APL off and the tied APL scales by the response rate with APL on (8 runs), and the same with the matrix beside it at an inhibitory scale of 2, 5 and 10, where the fits have 23 to 46 (24 runs); then, on an option of the model's code that lets the weights among KCs be negative, off by default and awaiting approval, the matrix at −2, −5 and −10 with the pooled neuron (24 runs) and alone with the threshold re-held (24 runs); only for a dose that stays within 0.005 of the fit on the four searched wirings, keeps the largest spike count with APL off under twice the fit's, stays in the realism bands and is lower than its own dose 0: the matrix shuffled within pairs of KC types at that dose as the control, the male CNS's two calyces beside, and the family fitted again on the four searched wirings with the excitation's scale and the pooled neuron's scale free, read on the four held-out wirings, with the same fit held to the inhibitory sign as its control; classes fixed beforehand ("equal" within 0.003 of the family on both populations and on 6 of 8 wirings against every target with every run in the bands, "below" by 0.003 on the same, "keeps the gain" at or below half-way to the family without KC–KC), two runs said to differ only if one is lower on at least seven of the eight wirings and on both populations against every target; only the hemibrain and male CNS tables curated by Pratyush. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts, the spread ratio and Pearson r on the per-pair APL effect, on the searched and the held-out wirings apart and against halves of the recordings; the mean APL effect and the loss on raw counts; the errors of the ester and the alcohol pairs and their place against the line along which every form of APL's inhibition trades them; the largest spike counts, the share of spikes after the pulse's end, the pooled neuron's scale, the thresholds and the realism bands. Prediction: no dose comes out below the family (0.048; the flies' floor 0.029); the pooled neuron alone stands at 0.054 to 0.060, the inhibitory doses fall from there toward the family, and the excitatory doses add up to 0.008 in the shape part, with the excitation alone at 0.063 to 0.072 against 0.063 for the family without KC–KC; about one chance in eight that a dose reaches the refit and one in twenty-five that the refit holds on the held-out wirings; the mean APL effect under the readout at +0.17 to +0.21 against the flies' +0.178, its direction on raw counts (the family +0.124) not predicted; the largest dose is the most likely to run away with APL off or to leave a realism band.

133. **E133, the differences between the hemibrain and the male CNS's left calyx moved one at a time (which feature of a calyx's tables, or which KC holds which claw, makes the best family fit the left calyx of a second fly worse).** Goal: to find what carries the distance of the male CNS's left calyx from the hemibrain in the breadth-2 APL-effect fit, 0.072 against 0.058 in the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout at fixed scales, after the family fitted on each calyx had put the left one above the range of the hemibrain's eight wirings and the right one inside it and had named three candidates without moving any, the γ KCs' claw counts, APL's synapses per KC and the matrix among KCs, all of which both male calyces share; and to follow a reading of those saved runs in which the left calyx's whole distance lies on the 42 odor pairs that hold 2-but, paa or B-myr (0.090 against 0.051 there, 0.060 against 0.061 on the other 78 pairs, on every half of the recordings), weak-input odors that the left calyx's APL moves apart about twice as far as the flies' does. Design: with no simulation first, that reading as committed code, the three odors' responders on the three calyces, and the claws, boutons and APL synapses of the glomeruli that carry those odors; then, at fixed scales with both response rates re-held, seven within-type claw permutations of each of the three calyces (21 runs), which keep every KC's type, claw count, APL synapses and partners among KCs and re-deal which KC holds which claw; then, unless the left calyx's re-drawn wirings lose the distance, six transplants in both directions on four wirings of each of the two calyces (48 runs): the claws per KC by type, the glomeruli's shares of the claws, APL's synapses at the KCs, APL's synapses at the boutons, the type-by-type shares of the matrix among KCs, and all five together; where the re-drawn wirings lose it, permutations in which each claw's APL synapses move with its input and the co-input-preserving shuffles (6 runs); for arms that pass, the right calyx as host and as donor, one split of the arm, and two fits; classes fixed beforehand, an arm said to carry the distance only if it opens at least half of it on the hemibrain and closes at least half on the left calyx, on three of four wirings and against every half of the recordings; a run outside the realism bands, at a corner or with a largest spike count above twice its own wiring's gets no class; nothing is searched and no parameter is added; no change to the model's code; only the hemibrain and male CNS tables curated by Pratyush. Readout: the pattern part of the APL-effect loss under the calibrated readout with its amplitude and shape parts, the spread ratio and Pearson r on the per-pair APL effect, against all recordings and halves of them; the error on the 42 pairs that hold any of the three odors and on the other 78; the mean APL effect under the readout and on raw counts; response rates by KC type and γ's ratio of APL off to APL on; the size of every transplant, the thresholds and APL scales after the re-hold, the largest spike counts and the realism bands. Prediction: the family's own pattern part on the hemibrain's wirings (0.048; the flies' floor 0.029) does not move; about two chances in five that the left calyx's own re-drawn wirings lose the distance, which would put it in which KC holds which claw; about one in three that a single transplant carries at least half of it, APL's synapses at the boutons or at the KCs the likeliest and the matrix among KCs the least; an arm that carries it moves the pattern part by 0.007 to 0.014 on its host, in the shape part and on the pairs of the three odors; the mean APL effect under the readout stays within 0.02 of the flies' +0.178.

134. **E134, the models as populations at every recorded stage (one measure of how far a model's responses lie from the flies' at each recorded stage, fixed and tested before any model is fitted to it).** Goal: to say where the saved models stand against the recordings at every stage of the megamat panel, ORNs, PN dendrites, PN boutons, KC claws and KC somata, and at the KC somata of the validation2 panel and of the kiwi and control mixtures, once the main target had become the fit of whole populations in place of the APL effect; no model had been read that way, the stage comparisons having read one statistic at a time, each by its own rule, and a measure has to be shown to order models alike against two halves of the flies before any choice rests on it.
