Design: with no simulation first, a lock, written before any model is read, on the files, flies, odor names, exclusions, response criterion and breadth bins of every stage and panel and on how the model is read at each; then the flies alone, each view of a population (strength by odor, sharing between odors, breadth among units) with the distance of one fly from the others as its unit, the floor from resampled flies, the recorded steps between stages with intervals, and the error of taking the upstream recordings as the prediction for the next stage; then the saved runs, the breadth-2 APL-effect fit's eight wirings at every saved stage and every archived variant at the KC somata of the APL16 panel, whose order is compared between two random halves of the recordings, 200 times; then about 50 short runs at fixed parameters, the family on the megamat and mixture inputs with all stages saved, as fitted and with the response rate held at each panel's flies, beside the lab's published hemibrain model, a uniform random wiring, the bouton baseline, the reference model and the firing-ceiling fit; classes fixed beforehand for the measure (usable if the variants' order agrees between halves at 0.8 or more and one wiring read against the other seven stays at 1.2 or less), for a stage (inside, far or not readable), for a step (explained, in part, or no better than no step) and for a panel (holds within 0.2 of the APL16 value, or panel-specific); nothing is fitted or searched and no model is changed. Readout: for each stage, panel and condition the population error in one-fly units with each view's error beside it, the flies' floor and range, the stage table and the step table; the agreement of the variants' order between halves for the population error, each view, an energy distance and the two ρ's on odor-odor distances; the population error against the pattern part of the APL-effect loss over the variants inside the realism bands; the response rates beside the flies'. Prediction: the measure is usable; at the KC somata of the APL16 panel the family stands at about 1.0 with APL on and with APL off (floors 0.25 and 0.28), with breadth at the floor and strength by odor its worst view (1.29); at the megamat PN boutons strength by odor is above 2, the spread of the odors' totals being 0.103 in the family against the flies' 0.375; at the megamat and validation2 KC somata the family stands at 1.2 to 2, having never been fitted to those flies; the mixtures are not predicted; no APL-effect statistic moves; every APL16 number involves paa, euc and -aPine, whose input is the ORN data within 1 log10 unit of the flies' concentrations.

135. **E135, the antennal lobe held to the recorded PN dendrites (whether the model's errors at the boutons and the KCs are made in the step from ORNs to PNs, which evens out the odors where the flies' does not).** Goal: to find whether the distance of the breadth-2 APL-effect fit from the recorded megamat stages is made upstream of the calyx, after the stage comparisons had found the spread of the odors' totals at 0.074 in the model's PN stage against 0.312 in the flies' PN dendrites and at 0.103 against 0.375 at the boutons, three experiments had failed to close the even boutons downstream of the PN stage, every earlier change of the antennal lobe had been judged on the APL effect, and an analysis that fitted global forms of the step to the dendrites by a correlation had found none better out of sample than the ORN input alone (Pearson r 0.84 for both, against a ceiling of 0.97); and to give that step more freedom than the others, since glomerulus-specific effects are reported both among the ORN terminals, where the model's input is recorded and already holds them, and at the PN dendrites themselves, where only the dendrite recordings beside the same panel's ORN terminals can show them. Design: with no simulation first, the recorded step from ORN terminals (9 flies) to PN dendrites (7 flies) on the 28 shared glomeruli and 17 megamat odors, and the measured step of each glomerulus, the gain of its dendrites on its own terminals, the residual by odor and the other glomeruli that residual follows, with DM1, DL5 and VA2 read by name; then the PN stage alone fitted to the dendrites in rungs built on that reading, its five global parameters, then one gain per glomerulus, set from the measured gains and then fitted, then up to two lateral terms per glomerulus among the measured candidates, every rung fitted on one half of the flies and read on the other half and on odors left out of the fit, with fits to odor-permuted dendrites as controls; then, only after the population measure has been shown to be usable, at fixed scales on the eight wirings with both response rates re-held: the fitted global parameters, the highest kept rung, that rung with glomerulus labels permuted, and the recorded dendrites put in the model's PN stage, rank for rank on the model's own PN rates, with glomerulus-permuted and odor-permuted controls, on the megamat input and on the APL16 input (up to 73 runs); the dendrites enter through an option of the model's code that gives the PN stage its rates directly, off by default, or through an ORN input solved so that the unchanged stage returns them; every arm runs on the terminal recordings and none on receptor spiking data; every parameter of the step is fitted to the dendrites and to no KC recording, none per odor, and nothing of the family is refitted; classes fixed beforehand ("upstream" if the imposed dendrites lower the megamat somata's population error by 0.15 one-fly units or more on six of eight wirings, by more than both permuted controls, and halve the boutons' error on strength by odor; the global parameters or the glomerulus terms "carry" at half of that gain, the terms only beyond their own glomerulus-permuted control). Readout: the measured step of every glomerulus with intervals and the agreement of two halves of the flies; at the PN dendrites the error on the matrix itself and on the three views, on held-out flies and odors; at the boutons, claws and somata the population errors with each view beside, against the unchanged family's; the megamat stage error; the fitted parameters beside the model's defaults and every kept gain and lateral term beside its measured value; the APL-effect statistics, the realism bands on the APL16 input, the megamat response rate beside the flies' 0.0915 and the largest PN and bouton rates. Prediction: a fitted antennal lobe brings the spread of the PN totals to at least 0.19 to 0.23, and to 0.28 to 0.32 if the fit turns the normalization nearly off, and that of the bouton totals from 0.103 toward 0.2 to 0.3; about three chances in four that the gains are kept at the dendrites, one in two that the measured gains do as well as fitted ones, one in three that they carry downstream and one in four for any lateral term; about one chance in two that strength by odor at the KC somata falls by 0.2 one-fly units or more, with sharing within 0.1; the megamat stage error falls by 0.1 to 0.3 if the error is upstream and not at all otherwise; the pattern part of the APL-effect loss is expected to rise; paa and B-myr have no dendrite recording, so a term at DM1 reaches them only out of sample; every APL16 number involves paa, euc and -aPine, whose input is the ORN data within 1 log10 unit of the flies' concentrations.

136. **E136, the best family fitted to all megamat stages at once (one loss over the recorded populations at the PN dendrites, PN boutons, KC claws and KC somata, in place of the APL effect).** Goal: to find how far the breadth-2 APL-effect fit can be brought toward the recordings at every megamat stage when it is fitted to them, since its parameters were set by the APL effect alone and some sit where the stages may not want them (about seven tenths of APL's inhibition made at the PN boutons, where the connectome puts about a tenth of APL's calyx synapses), no model having carried a stage before the somata in its loss; and to learn whether a gain on one panel's flies is general, by reading it on panels the fit never sees. Design: only after the population measure has been shown to be usable; the loss is the megamat stage error over the stages found readable, on the mean views of the four searched wirings against a fixed half of each stage's flies, with the response rate with APL on held at the megamat flies' value and the rate with APL off at 2.2 times that; at the family's own parameters the loss first reproduces the stage table; five fits, the family as it is, the family with the antennal-lobe stage fitted to the PN dendrites (its terms per glomerulus, if any were kept there, fixed), APL's inhibition of the claws as a gain in place of a subtraction, the per-claw threshold free, and the family without the inhibition among KCs, each with at most eight free parameters, none per odor and none per glomerulus; a control fit against flies whose odor labels are permuted at every stage; then about 130 runs at fixed parameters, each fit on the four held-out wirings against the held-out half of the flies, on the eight wirings with the validation2 and the mixture inputs, and on the APL16 input with APL on and off; classes fixed beforehand ("improves" if the megamat stage error is lower by at least twice the standard error over wirings and no stage's error is higher by more than its own spread, "holds" if no held-out panel's KC population error is higher by more than the spread there, "megamat only" and "one stage at another's cost" otherwise), with no class for a fit outside a realism band, at a bound or no better than the permuted control. Readout: the megamat stage error with every stage's population error and its views, on the searched and the held-out wirings and flies apart; the KC population error on the validation2, mixture and APL16 panels; the APL-effect statistics of every arm as a held-out test; every fitted scale beside the family's, the bouton share of APL's inhibition, the response rates, the largest spike counts and the realism bands. Prediction: on the fitted wirings and flies the megamat stage error falls by 10 to 30 % from the family's, and by about half of that on the held-out wirings and flies, with about one chance in two that an arm passes there and one in three that the gain also shows at the KC somata of the validation2 panel and the mixtures; the pattern part of the APL-effect loss rises from the family's 0.048 (the flies' floor 0.029) by an amount that is itself a result; the APL16 read involves paa, euc and -aPine, whose input is the ORN data within 1 log10 unit of the flies' concentrations.

137. **E137, the held-out panels read like for like (whether the family's distance from the flies on the mixture and validation2 panels is the model's or the reading's).** Goal: to fix how the flies are read on the panels no fit sees, before the fits to the megamat stages are classed on them, after the baseline had found the breadth-2 APL-effect fit far from the flies on strength by odor at the KC somata of the kiwi mixtures (2.43 one-fly units, the most atypical fly 1.67) and inside on validation2 (1.05): Remy's mixture sessions give their stimuli in a fixed order, solvent, the five components and then the mixture at three dilutions, three consecutive presentations each, a component given again after the mixtures evokes 0.08 to 0.95 of its first block's response rate, the model's mixture somata get no trial noise, and validation2 is read on 6 of its 20 flies and 15 of its 22 odors. Design: with no simulation and no model changed; a lock, before any model is re-read, on Remy's per-trial processed files and the stimulus file of each of the eleven mixture flies, with the uploaded table reproduced from them; the flies alone by position, each presentation's response rate and mean response, the third presentation over the first, the second block over the first, and the solvent and probe odors at the session's start and end; five readings of the flies fixed before any score (as uploaded, first presentations only, components from the first block, from the second block, and from both), each with its own one-fly units and floor; the flies' trial noise put on the model's mixture somata; the saved mixture runs of the family and of five reference models re-scored under each reading, beside a flat profile and the ORN recordings taken as the prediction, every strength error split into a spread part and an order part; the mixture's rise over its three dilutions, which position can only flatten; validation2 on all 20 flies and 22 odors, each fly on its own odors, under two rules for partial panels fixed beforehand; classes fixed beforehand (a panel is "readable on strength" if the family's class, inside, between or far, is the same under every reading by position, or on six flies and on twenty; a reading replaces the lock's only where the panel is readable and the reproduction holds). Readout: the flies' response rates by position; the family's strength error at the KC somata of each mixture panel under each reading, with sharing and breadth beside, its bounds and its class; the same on validation2 on six and on twenty flies; how much of a one-fly unit an odor's place in its block carries on megamat and validation2. Prediction: on the kiwi mixtures about 2.4 against the components' first block and roughly half that against their second, with about one chance in two that the panel is not readable on strength; the control mixtures within 0.3 of their 0.76 and validation2 within 0.15 of its 1.05; sharing and breadth on the mixtures fall by more than strength once the model's somata get the flies' noise; megamat does not move, its order being drawn anew per block and fly; no APL-effect statistic moves.

138. **E138, the odors that carry the strength error and the glomeruli that feed them (what the errors on strength by odor at the KC somata are made of, odor by odor, and how much of them a fit of the calyx can reach).** Goal: to say which odors carry the breadth-2 APL-effect fit's error on strength by odor at the KC somata of every panel and what feeds them, after a read of the saved populations had put about three quarters of the megamat somata's squared error on aa alone (the error 0.90 one-fly units, about 0.47 without aa's entry), whose largest inputs include DC4 and DP1l, two glomeruli the dendrite recordings do not hold, and after the first fit to the megamat stages had moved the spread of the odors' strengths and not their order, a control fitted to permuted labels finding about half of its gain; a single odor's share being a lead until it holds on halves of the flies. Design: with no simulation; three classes of glomeruli fixed before any residual is read (fed by IR-expressing ORNs, absent from the 28 glomeruli of the dendrite recordings, with fewer boutons or claws in the hemibrain than the median glomerulus); each odor's entry in both parts of the strength error, for the family as fitted and held and for the reference models, at the KC somata of megamat, the APL16 panel with APL on and off, validation2 and the mixtures, with the flies' range and each odor's share, on all flies and on seeded halves of them; each megamat odor's strength at the flies' dendrites, boutons, claws and somata beside the model's; for the odors that carry a panel's error, the glomeruli that carry their ORN input and what the model's input, the dendrite recordings, the bouton line's published expression and Pratyush's hemibrain and male CNS tables give each; the family's residual regressed, over the odors of megamat and the APL16 panel, on the share of each odor's input on a class, the fit then read on validation2's odors against 1,000 random classes of the same size, a step that waits for Tom's or Yang's word, being fitted on a held-out panel; classes fixed beforehand ("concentrated" if a panel's two largest odors carry more than half of the squared error on both halves of the flies; "a class passes" at a correlation of 0.5 or more on validation2's residuals, above 95 % of the random classes, on both halves). Readout: the entries and shares by odor and panel with their agreement between halves; the spread and order parts of every strength error; the strength of each megamat odor along the stages; the table of glomeruli; the regression's fit and its test on validation2. Prediction: the megamat somata's error is concentrated on aa (56 to 75 % of the squared error), and with aa's entry at the flies' value the megamat stage error on strength would stand at about 0.84 in place of 0.927; on validation2 the error is spread over PEA, -bCar, Z4-7al and 1-3ol, and the model's and the flies' strengths correlate at 0.43 and 0.56 over its odors; whether a class of glomeruli passes is not predicted; no APL-effect statistic moves; every APL16 entry involves paa, euc and -aPine, whose input is the ORN data within 1 log10 unit of the flies' concentrations.

139. **E139, the weaker antennal-lobe normalization without the calyx refit, along its strength (how much of the stage fit's gain needs only the antennal lobe's normalization, at which strength, and which of the fit's three changes carries its price on the APL effect).** Goal: to say what carries the one arm of E136 that holds, whose point joins a normalization 32 times weaker in its additive term, an APL→bouton scale at 0.155 of the family's and a KC–KC scale at 3.24 times the family's, after its single-factor returns had mixed stage errors with hold penalties and after E102 had named the same strength from the flies' APL along concentration. Design: with no simulation first, the fit's saved evaluations binned by the additive term, E102's saved ladder on the APL16 input scored as E136's reads, and the PN stage alone against the recorded dendrites; then, at fixed scales with both response rates re-held, the family's own calyx at pn_inhadd 100, 314, 1000 and 3141 on the megamat, validation2 and mixture inputs of the eight wirings (96 runs), and the refit's two calyx factors one at a time at 314 and 1000 on those inputs and the APL16 input (96 runs), with 16 runs beside on the extended input for the flies' APL along concentration and the KCs at low concentration; E136's classes, with a price rule on the calibrated pattern part of the APL effect (more than 0.003 above the family's), fixed beforehand; one refit of the two calyx factors at a fixed rung only if a rung below 1000 improves at under half of the fit's price. Readout: each setting's megamat stage error on strength by odor on the held-out wirings against the held-out half of the flies, stage by stage, with the held-out panels, the APL-effect statistics and the realism bands beside. Prediction: at 1000 the family's own calyx keeps about two thirds of the fit's fall (0.06 to 0.10 of 0.115) at about a third of its price, but its APL16 somata with APL on are worse beyond their spread (+0.09 against 0.051, already in the saved runs); at 314 about half of the fall with the APL16 panel inside its spread, the chance that a rung serves both about one in four; every APL16 number involves paa, euc and -aPine, whose input is the ORN data within 1 log10 unit of the flies' concentrations.

140. **E140, aa at the step from the claws to the somata (why the model's response to the one odor that carries the megamat somata's strength error falls where the flies' rises).** Goal: to say what makes the megamat somata's error on strength by odor, about three quarters of which E138 put on aa on both halves of the flies, after the stage table had shown the flies' aa rising from 0.92 of the panel's mean at the claws to 1.35 at the somata while the family's falls from 1.18 to 0.65, after no arm of E136 had moved it (0.70 to 0.76 on raw counts), and after a read of the same-fly recording had found the rise inside one fly (0.77 at the claws, 1.28 at the somata). Design: with no simulation first and a lock on the readings; aa's response rate, mean response and the concentration of its responders (the top tenth's share of the summed response) at the boutons, claws and somata of every recording, the same-fly step, aa's somatic entry under each reading, and its row of the sharing view; the same statistics on the family's held megamat runs, E136's arms and E135's imposed dendrites, with aa's responders by the glomeruli of their claws; over the 17 odors, what the somata's strength follows in the model's input and in the flies' own upstream stages; aa's model input beside each of the seven ORN flies given aa, Hallem's aa and the dendrite recordings; then, only if the flies' aa is few and strong at the claws and the model's many and weak, aa's input taken from each ORN fly's own vector on the four held-out wirings (28 runs, nothing searched); a ladder on the IR-fed glomeruli held for Tom's or Yang's word; classes fixed beforehand. Readout: aa's entries and ranks at every stage in the flies and in every saved run, the four classes, and the megamat somata's and stage errors with aa's entry beside. Prediction: the flies' aa is few and strong at the claws and the model's the reverse (mean response over response rate 1.14 against 0.74), no ORN fly's own vector brings the somata's aa into the soma flies' range (about seven in eight), and the megamat stage error would stand at about 0.86 in place of 0.918 with aa's entry at the flies' value; no APL-effect statistic moves, and no APL16 number is computed.

## Model changes likely to move distances toward the data

The changes with the best expected payoff per unit of work are the ones that add constraints the model has never been fit to, before adding mechanisms. Ordered by that payoff:

**F****i****t to more than the mean response rate.** Today one scalar threshold and one APL scale are tuned to a mean rate; the rank structure is never fit. Use `make_kc_data_loss` with per-odor rates plus the correlation matrix, and add the TNT matrix as a second condition (E10). The lab's bouton and claw matrices are further stage-wise targets the model already produces internally (`bouton_sims`, `claw_sims`) but never compares.

1. **An APL stage shaped like the real one.** Per-claw connectome KC↔APL weights and PN-bouton↔APL weights are in the repo; APL units with coupling fit to data (E2) turn the single scalar into a spatial field. Two additions need C++: a saturating nonlinearity on each unit's potential (APL is non-spiking and switches from local to global activation with stimulus strength, Inada et al. 2017), and separate tuning of the KC→APL and APL→KC scales, which are currently moved together (`add_to_apl_weight_scales`). Constrain the KC→APL gain with the published scaling of APL response against PN input (Prisco et al. 2021) until the lab has its own APL imaging.
2. **Heterogeneous KCs from data in hand.** Per-KC thresholds that depend on claw count (hemibrain claws, Pratyush's segmentation) and per-type excitability and APL weights (α′/β′ most excitable and most APL-coupled, γ least; Inada et al. 2017), via `fixed_thr` vectors and `_wAPLKC`/`_wKCAPL`. Give the `unknown`-type KCs real weights rather than zero.
3. **A readout that matches the imaging.** Convolve model spike trains with a nuclear-GCaMP6s-like kernel with saturation (the chapter-3 logistic is a first version), take the peak over the lab's response window, and compute Pearson on that graded signal; count spikes only from odor onset. This removes a known mismatch before any mechanism is judged, and it is what E6 needs.
4. **Measured ORN time courses as input.** Replace the smoothed step with the lab's own ORN terminal traces (or a fitted adaptation model), so the late phase that feedback acts on is right; include all pebbled glomeruli rather than the connectome∩Task subset where ORN data exist.
5. **Glomerulus-specific inhibition in the AL.** The model's single global LN driven by mean ORN rate is a known simplification; the hemibrain LN→PN connectivity gives a glomerulus-weighted inhibition matrix, and the lab's PN-dendrite matrices (GH146) are the direct test of that stage. This matters less for the KC-specific remapping (PN dendrites barely move) but it sets how much drive contrast reaches APL (E3).
6. **Connections the connectome has and the model ignores.** KC-claw→KC-claw synapses (data already in `from_pratyush/2025-12-05`), MB-C1 and LH centrifugal inputs to the calyx (E8, E9). These are the only candidates on the list that could supply APL-independent, category-structured inhibition.

Data the lab is well placed to collect, each tied to a mechanism:

- **PN boutons and KC claws in APL>TNT flies on the same 16-odor panel** (mechanism 2 vs 3): tells whether APL's pair-specific effect is already visible at the claws (calyx action) or appears only at the soma (lobe action), and whether the bouton-level VCF shift is APL-dependent. An acute block (APL>shibire-ts or GtACR) alongside the 3-day TNT separates APL loss from homeostatic threshold changes.
- **Volumetric APL calyx imaging on the panel** (mechanism 2): odor-specific localization of APL activity parameterizes the units' drive and coupling directly instead of fitting them.
- **KC-subtype-resolved soma imaging** (α/β, α′/β′, γ drivers) on the panel (mechanisms 1 and 2): tests whether the remapping and the APL share are type-specific, as the food-PN convergence onto α/β and α′/β′ KCs predicts.
- **One indicator across compartments** (confound 7): soma with cytoplasmic jGCaMP8m or claws with a nuclear indicator in a few flies, to show the claw→soma change is not an indicator artefact.
- **Per-fly TNT and TNT3c matrices and per-odor response rates** (already collected): the noise floor for residuals of \~0.03 and the density-matched null (E7) both need them.
- **A concentration series for three or four panel odors in both genotypes** (mechanism 2): separates intensity-driven APL recruitment from identity-driven structure.

## Open questions and what I need from you

The conclusions above rest on two cross-fly mean matrices; the items below would turn the rough shares into numbers with error bars.

- [ ] Per-fly KC matrices and per-odor response rates for both genotypes, with n flies, the odor concentrations used, and the response window. This sets the noise floor for the \~0.03 residuals and allows the density-matched null (E7).
- [ ] Confirm the genotypes: TNT3c is the functioning-APL control (the file with the sparser, more decorrelated code, consistent with that reading), and whether, as in the chapter-3 TNTin flies, some hemispheres lacked APL labeling and can serve as internal controls.
- [ ] The analysis you remembered as "the difference moving toward KC or VCF": I get a related result (APL's pair-specific effect correlates with the wild-type KC−ORN residuals at 0.39 and with VCF at 0.28), but comparing against your version would show whether we are measuring the same thing.
- [ ] ORN responses for the four validation2 odors at the concentrations used in the APL experiment, if they differ from the consensus panel values (paa −4, -aPine −1.5, euc −1.75, B-myr −2).
- [ ] The model variant and metric behind "hemibrain looks worse than the simpler model": the thesis reports 0.64 (uniform) vs 0.71 (hemibrain wd20) on 17 odors, and on the 12-odor subset here the two are indistinguishable (0.85 vs 0.84), so the comparison that looked worse probably used a newer claw or bouton variant and would be worth pinning down.
- [ ] Whether KC claws or PN boutons were imaged in any APL>TNT flies, and the status of the early-vs-late claw analysis (draft figure 7).

Analysis files: the scripts and outputs behind the numbers here are attached in chat (`apl_analysis.py`, `null_models.py`, `extra_checks.py`, with the 66-pair and 120-pair tables and the 16-odor consensus ORN matrix).

## Footnotes {#footnotes}

**How the calibrated readout works.** The model's side of every odor-odor distance is a cosine distance across KCs on raw spike counts (KCs x odors, the spikes in the response), and the data's side a cosine distance on z-scored nuclear GCaMP6s trial means, which compress large responses; on raw counts a few dozen high-firing KCs set the cosine (in the reference model, the 40 Hz fit at the control flies' 6.8 % response rate from E44, the top 1 % of KCs carry about two thirds of each odor's squared response vector). The readout used in the compressive readout of the model's spike counts (E65) and in the APL-effect fits (E67) replaces each spike count n by n / (n + k), with k the half-saturation count in spikes. Zero stays zero and the map is monotone, so which KCs respond (two or more spikes), every response rate and the realism bands are unchanged; only the weight of each responding KC in the cosine changes. At the reference model's k of 10.5 spikes, 2 spikes read 0.16, 5 read 0.32, 20 read 0.66 and 78 (its most active KC with APL on) read 0.88, so that KC weighs 5.5 times a 2-spike KC instead of 39 times. k is set per model and is not fitted to the APL effect: on the model's APL-on spike counts alone, it is the value (bisection on log k between 0.001 and 1000 spikes, 60 steps) at which the participation ratio of the readout equals the control flies'. The participation ratio of one odor's response vector over the N KCs is (Σx²)² / (N Σx⁴), the effective fraction of KCs that carry the odor's squared response (1 when all carry it equally, 1/N when one KC does); it is computed on responders only (model entries under 2 spikes and data trial means at or below 1 set to 0, for this statistic only), averaged over the 16 odors and, for the data, taken per recording and averaged over the 11 control-fly recordings. It is 3.4 % in the control flies (8.3 % with APL output blocked (TNT)) and 1.1 % for the reference model's raw APL-on counts; a smaller k evens the responders out and raises it, a larger k leaves the counts nearly raw. The same k is then applied unchanged to the model's acute APL-off counts (all entries, single spikes included), so the APL-off distances and the per-pair APL effect stay predictions, not fits. The calibration gives k = 10.5 spikes for the reference model and 4.5 for the first joint fit (E10), and 4.5 to 15 across the key models of E65; where the control flies' value cannot be bracketed the nearest bound is used (47 of the 728 archived variants). In the APL-effect fits (E67) k is calibrated again at every evaluation of the fit, on that evaluation's APL-on counts.

The participation ratio was chosen because it measures directly what the cosine weighs (how the squared response is spread over KCs) and is computed the same way on the model and on the data. The alternatives run in E65 agree with it: a power readout n^p calibrated the same way; calibrations to the control flies' responders-only top-1 % share of the squared response vector (48 %), to the coefficient of variation of the responders' amplitudes (0.48) or to the participation ratio on all values (6.1 %); and a rank-matching map of the model's APL-on counts onto the control flies' pooled responder amplitudes. For the reference model these give an APL-effect loss of 0.072 to 0.089, against 0.075 under the readout described here and 0.130 on raw counts (lower is better; the data's floor is about 0.04), and fixed, uncalibrated readouts of moderate compression (a square root; n / (n + k) at k of 10 or 20 spikes) give 0.075 to 0.076. The readout is a static, elementwise map: it has no indicator kinetics, no nuclear localization and no per-KC baseline or z-scoring (how the recordings were z-scored is an open question for Yang). Whether the main target is scored on raw counts or through this readout is Tom's decision; until then both are reported.
